Interface targets check the method set; map/chan conversions `Named`-wrap the same storage so Go's aliasing survives. Fixing element-type resolution needed `TypeDef.Binds` stamped on emitted composite typedefs (compile + `SpecialContext.ResolveType`) and `resolveTypeRef` consulting `from.Binds` for bare idents. Testdata: `minigo/testdata/conversions`.
- [x] **Conversion residuals** (found implementing `[]byte`): `P(p)`/`F(f)` now `Named`-wrap when the target typedef is declared (`td.Spec != nil`) — `x.(P)` asserts on declared identity: `P`↔`*Sq`, `F`↔`func()`, named↔anonymous containers (`Ints`↔`[]int`) all fail as in Go. Member lookup separates them too: anonymous `*Sq` still promotes `Sq`'s methods while declared `P *Sq` has an empty method set (Go spec: no methods on pointer-underlying types); `p.F`/`p.F = v`/`*p = v` work through `Named{P,cell}`. Shape spellings are package-aware — bare idents qualify as `pkgPath.Name`, `a.T` selectors resolve through the file's import table — so `[]Foo` from two packages no longer compares equal, while same-package selectors and predeclared names still match. `string(sx)` traps on non-int64 elements instead of skipping them. Testdata: `minigo/testdata/{conversions,convfooa,convfoob,convident}`. Remaining approximations: `type A []int` bounds are checked on entry but container `Typ` is stamped on the shared storage, so a later `var s []int = Ints{...}` bind still passes unchecked (documented — restamping would corrupt aliased views); struct/map shape equality stays spelling-based for anonymous spellings (a source-text heuristic, not resolved element identity).
- [x] **Declared types are erased at run time** → `var x T` annotations now bind a typed zero via `OpCoerce`/`OpCoerceTop`/`OpCoerceGlobal` (locals, params, named results, returns): `var z T` in a generic body materializes T's zero, and `var s Sq` fills struct fields with their declared-type zeros through the `FieldTypes` hook. Interface slots box typed nils: `var x any = (*int)(nil); x == nil` evaluates `false` (`TypedNil` vs `IfaceNil`), like Go. `x.(T)` on a typed nil checks the typedef identity (a `*runtime.TypeDef`), and `*T` is a real `KindPointer` typedef so `x.(*int)` works. Remaining approximation: `x.(T)` still checks shape not declared-type identity for named non-struct types, and non-NIL values bound into declared-type slots pass through unchecked (no assignability check).
- [x] **`type` declarations inside function bodies**: a local `type S struct{...}` compiles to a `*TypeDef` const bound as a local, so literals/asserts/conversions/methods resolve as usual. Remaining gap: nested literal elements under an anonymous `func` element type (e.g. `[]func() int{...}`) still can't be inferred — `FuncType` typedefs carry no signature.
- [x] **`goto` scoping rules**: labels record their block-ID set and visible-variable snapshot; a `goto` whose label sits in a deeper block, or that jumps over declarations the label can see, emits an `OpTrap` (compile stays total — the diagnostic is a run-time trap with Go's message shape: "jumps into a block" / "jumps over declaration of x").
- [x] **Interface-constraint elements**: `interface{ ~int }`, unions, and `comparable` are collected into `TConstraints` and checked at instantiation — `F[bool]` under `T ~int|~string` traps. Satisfaction is an approximation of the spec's rules (underlying-kind match for `~T`, per-element union member check, `comparable` allowing non-struct-of-interfaces), not the full `go/types` algorithm.
- [x] **`minigo.dev/host` stub package** (plan §11): `minigo/host` ships gopls-friendly stubs (`panic("minigo intrinsic")` bodies); `installStdlib` binds one host table under both `minigo.dev/host` and the in-repo path. `host.Exit` always errors; env/argv/wd helpers bind only when the engine is unrestricted.
- [x] **`SpecialContext.ResolveSymbol`** (plan §12.5): resolves `pkg.Sym` or a bare identifier to a `SymbolID` through the caller file's import table without materializing or initializing anything — the index-level laziness quoters need. `Resolve`/`ResolveType` landed below.
- [x] **REPL + CLI subcommands**: `engine.NewREPL()` gives a persistent session — declarations accumulate, `:=`/`var`/`const` names hoist to package-global cells, a trailing expression becomes the value (`x := e` re-declaration updates rather than shadows — documented divergence). `cmd/minigo` gained `run --entry F` and `repl` subcommands; the `minigo <ref> [func]` shorthand is unchanged.
- [x] **`SpecialContext.Resolve`/`ResolveType`**: `Resolve` maps a bare identifier or `pkg.Sym` selector to its runtime value through ordinary name lookup (locals/upvals first, then globals — materializing only the named decl — then the file's import table via `Member`); non-symbol expressions are rejected so resolution stays declaration-level lazy. `ResolveType` maps a type expression to its `*TypeDef`: named types through `Resolve`, composite forms (`[]T`, `map[K]V`, `*T`, `chan T`, `struct{...}`, `interface{...}`, `func` types) as the same `Anon`-spec typedefs the compiler emits for declared types, `T[Args]` through the generic instantiate path. `resolveGlobal` gained a non-trapping `resolveGlobalE` twin so failures return as errors.
- [x] **REPL multi-line input**: `minigo.IncompleteInput` tokenizes the pending fragment — an open `()`/`[]`/`{}` group, or a line ending where Go inserts no semicolon (trailing operator, comma, dot), reads as a continuation, and the `minigo repl` loop accumulates under a `.. ` prompt until the fragment completes. `} else {` keeps Go's same-line rule; comment-only and unterminated-token lines evaluate immediately so their errors surface instead of waiting. Leftover: a mistyped line that happens to look incomplete (e.g. a trailing `+`) waits silently — no abort-fragment command yet.
- [x] **REPL directory imports**: `import "./dir"` (also `../dir`, `/abs/dir`) — illegal in real Go source but natural at a prompt launched from a project root — resolves eagerly against the engine's start directory: a typo fails the import line itself, and the recorded spec binds the package's declared name (`import "./oddname"` binds `oddpkg`) instead of the path basename, which may not even be a valid identifier. Explicit aliases and dot/blank forms work unchanged; the `Load` hook of a dir-form `ImportRef` routes through `loadDir`. Echo cleanup in the same pass: only an input ending in an expression prints its value (declarations and `:=`/`var`/`const` stay silent — no more `(0x0,0x0)` noise), and multi-return results render as tuples (`fmt.Println` → `(6, <nil>)`) instead of a raw box.
- [x] **`minigo vet`** (plan §16): `Engine.Vet(ctx, ref)` lists call sites `pkgAlias.Sym(...)` whose target declaration is a stub member (body is exactly `panic("minigo intrinsic")`) and whose `SymbolID` is neither a registered special form nor a bound host symbol — the failure they would hit at run time, reported statically with file:line positions. `minigo vet <ref>` prints findings and exits nonzero; `--special import/path.Sym` declares intercepted symbols for CLI use (embedding apps get theirs from `RegisterSpecial`/`Bind` for free). Dot imports are invisible to the checker.
- [x] **`gen-intrinsics` subcommand** (plan §15): `minigo gen-intrinsics -output <dir> <pkg>...` emits `<path>/install.go` with `func Bind(e *minigo.Engine)` registering every exported member: non-generic functions via the new `minigo.WrapFunc` reflect adapter (marshal-by-copy, trailing `error` result unwraps to the call's error, multi-results become `*runtime.Tuple`), vars/consts as `minigo.ValueOf` snapshots, types as boxed `reflect.Type`. Generic functions and methods are skipped. `ValueOf` is the public marshal helper — note `runtime.Value` is an `any` alias, so it switches on concrete runtime types explicitly.
- [x] **Typed-zero follow-ups** (found in round 5): `Cell` gained a `Typ` field stamped by `OpCoerce`/`OpCoerceGlobal`/`new(T)`, so plain `x = v` stores re-coerce like `var x T = v`; `coerce` now enforces assignability — basic-family shapes, interface satisfaction via the method set, `*Struct`/`Slice`/`Map`/`Chan`/`Func`/`Pointer` kind checks, and `*Map` re-binds only through matching element shapes (two different named map types trap). Declared named-basic types wrap values in `*runtime.Named{Typ, V}`: `var c Celsius`, `c = v` and `T(x)` keep the tag, arithmetic on one declared tag re-tags the result (two different tags trap "mismatched types"), `x.(T)` matches the declared type rather than the builtin name, and `type A B` shares B's storage but not its methods. Unresolvable struct field types fall back to a synthetic `NamedBasic` typedef, so the field zero is a `TypedNil` hole that still boxes non-nil.
- [x] **Typed-zero residuals** (found in round 6, addressed in round 7b): values now carry a declared tag — `Struct.Def`, `Map.Typ`, and new `Slice.Typ`/`Chan.Typ` (stamped by composite literals, `make`, and declared-type binds) — so `var a A = sqVal`/`mapVal`/`sliceVal` traps named-to-named while `type A = Sq` aliases and anonymous literals still bind. Unnamed struct literals bind named struct types on field-name match. Named pointers check the pointee's declared type against `ElemOf` (`var p P = &other` traps). `OpBox` cells take the pointee tag so `*p = v` through `&T{...}` coerces, `s[i] = v` / `ch <- v` coerce to the declared element type, and `const k T = v` keeps its tag (`x.(T)` works on consts). Remaining approximations: bare basic values still act as untyped constants (`var y MyInt = intVar` wraps — the runtime cannot distinguish literal from `int`-typed var); inherited const specs don't propagate the written type (`const (a T = 1; b)` leaves `b` untagged); `type F func()` and `type I2 I` values carry no identity beyond their underlying shape; struct field tags/element types are not compared (field-name match only); `&s.f`/`&s[i]` ref stores stay unchecked.
- [x] **File-level entry points**: `Engine.LoadFile`/`Engine.RunFile` build a package from one named file, bypassing directory discovery and build-constraint filtering — a `//go:build codegen` DSL file (or a script outside any package) is a first-class entry. Synthetic `<file>+abs` import path, separate `e.files` cache so `byDir` stays directory-pure; `resolve.BuildConfig.CheckDir` is exported so `AllowedRoots` still applies to the file's directory.
- [x] **`convert-define` on minigo**: `examples/convert-define/internal/interpreter.go` now drives a minigo `Engine` — `define.Convert`/`define.Rule` dispatch as `OpSpecialCall` quoted calls, quoted `pkg.Sym` args resolve to `SymbolID`s via `ctx.ResolveSymbol` (import-table lookup, no materialization; locals/upvals shadowing import names are rejected), and `goscan.Scanner` stays host-side for `scanner.TypeInfo`/`FunctionInfo`. Conditions and decisions: [docs/sketch/plan-minigo-convert-define.md](./docs/sketch/plan-minigo-convert-define.md).
- [x] **`convert-define` plan-acceptance tests**: `internal/plan_test.go` (`TestConvertDefineSatisfiesPlan`) runs a `//go:build codegen` DSL through the real `Runner` with a spying `resolve.Resolver` and proves docs/sketch/plan-minigo-vm.md §12 — alias-agnostic `SPECIAL_CALL` dispatch (canonical symbol identity), specials fire only when reached (dead `if false` branch), zero `Locate`/`LocateDir` calls (the `define` package is never loaded or parsed by the runtime), and quoted `FuncLit` args walked host-side. `migration_guard_test.go` (`TestNoMinigoV1Dependency`) scans every `.go` file in the module and fails if any imports v1 `minigo` again (v1/v2 share the Go module, so the guard is source-level, not go.mod). `Runner.resolver` is the unexported hook that makes the spy possible.
- [x] **`convert-define` without vendored go-scan (experiment, draft PR #18)**: `examples/convert-define/pkg/` (vendored goscan/scanner/locator, ~2000 lines) is deleted and replaced by `engine.Package` + the `inspect` view layer — `TypeExpr`/`SymbolID`/`Resolve`/`FieldsOf`/`SignatureOf`/`DefOf` supply everything `scanner.TypeInfo`/`FieldType`/`FunctionInfo` provided, and `ImportManager` moved to `generator/` (it never needed a scanner). `e2e_test/generated.go` regenerates byte-identically; `plan_test.go` now asserts the stronger laziness claim "exactly the DSL-named packages are located". Fixed along the way: cross-module `define.Rule` matching was silently dead (rule keys used the package-name spelling while lookup compared `PkgPath`-qualified names — canonical SymbolID keys fix both sides), and `FieldInfo.JSONTag` was never populated (Priority-2 json matching was dead code — now filled from `inspect.Field.Tag`). Findings + remaining gaps: [docs/sketch/experiment-convert-define-on-inspect.md](./docs/sketch/experiment-convert-define-on-inspect.md). Rebased onto main after #20 landed the four inspect/engine accessors (`Sub`, `Engine.SourceOf`, `Sig.ParamFields`/`ResultFields`, `TypeExpr.CanonicalName`) — the experiment's workarounds are all deleted. One finding worth noting: `SourceOf` works but convert-define deliberately doesn't use it — bound-stdlib opacity is the correct codegen policy. This is an experiment branch — may not merge.
- [x] **`inspect.TypeExpr`: reach non-`Children` sub-expressions** (found by the convert-define experiment; landed on main via #20): `Children()` drops `IndexExpr.X`/`IndexListExpr.X` (generic instantiation bases), `ArrayType.Len`, and `ChanType.Dir`. `withExpr` is now exported as `(*TypeExpr).Sub(ast.Expr)` — host code re-wraps any sub-expression in the same file/pkg context. convert-define's `registerImports`/`getTypeName` use it so generic-typed fields (`pkg.List[T]`) get their base package imported and a qualified rendering. `ChanType.Dir`/`ArrayType.Len` need no child view — `Expr()` exposes them.
- [x] **Host-side `engine.SourceOf`** (landed via #20): `Engine.SourceOf(ctx, path)` reaches the real decl behind a bound shadow. convert-define tried it and reverted — bound-stdlib opacity is the correct consumer policy (a real `time.Time` decl costs laziness, misroutes same-type fields into nonexistent converters, and introspects unexported internals codegen can't use), so `NewHostDecl` pseudo-decls remain.
- [x] **Host-friendly signature access on `inspect.Sig`** (landed via #20): `SignatureOf` keeps the script FFI shape (`*runtime.Slice`); `sig.ParamFields()`/`ResultFields()` return `[]*Field` for host callers. convert-define's hand-rolled `sigFields` unboxer is deleted.
- [x] **VM: Go panics inside host handlers lose their stack** (already landed by #17 — an earlier revision of this entry was wrong): a nil-pointer in a `SpecialFunc` becomes `*runtime.Panic` via `asScriptPanic` with *both* the script frames (which DSL call panicked, plus its source line — `CallFrames`) and the host Go stack (`debug.Stack()` in `GoStack`, rendered by `Panic.Error()`). Debugging the convert-define rewrite had needed a temporary `debug.Stack()` patch; that is what #17's machinery now provides.
- [x] **Range-over-func iterators**: `for x := range f` over `func(yield func(K, V) bool)` (Go 1.23 `iter.Seq`/`iter.Seq2`) now works — `newIterator` accepts `*Function`/`*Closure`/`*BoundMethod`/`*BuiltinFunc` values as kind `'f'`, and `driveFuncIter` runs the whole loop inside the first `OpRangeNext`: the producer is invoked once with a `yield` builtin that pushes its arguments as loop values and re-runs the loop body bounded to the loop's `(top, end)` instruction range (`frame.boundLo`/`boundHi` + `runBounded`). `yield` returns `true` when the body reaches the back-edge (continue), `false` when the body left the loop (break/goto/return) — so infinite producers stop on early `break`; a further yield after that panics like Go ("range function continued iteration after yield returned false"). Body panics propagate through the producer's frames (its defers run, `recover` may catch) then through the caller — matching Go. Remaining approximations: a `goto` that exits the loop backward/forward keeps its target only at loop level (yield still returns false); host `*GoValue` funcs are not callable producers; `iter.Pull`/`Pull2` are not bound.

- [x] **`docgen` on minigo**: `examples/docgen/loader.go` now evaluates the patterns DSL with minigo — `NewEngine(dir-of-config)` + `LoadFile` + `EnsureReady` + `Globals.Get("Patterns")` + `Result.As`, and `Fn` references arrive as `*runtime.Function`/`*runtime.BoundMethod` whose `Pkg.Path` is already the import path (v1 needed a ModuleDir/ModulePath dance). docgen has no v1 `minigo` dependency left; `examples/minigo` is the last v1 consumer. Notes: `LoadPatternsFromSource` (string-eval) was dropped — minigo entries are file-oriented; method `Function.Name` reads `"Type.Method"` with `Recv` now stamped at materialize time, and `(*T).M` spelling is rebuilt from `Recv`/`Name`+`PtrRecv`. See docs/sketch/plan-minigo-vm.md rounds 15-16.
- [x] **`Function.Recv` unset on materialized methods** (found migrating docgen): `td.Methods` entries now stamp `Recv: <declared-type-name>` at materialize time (`Name` still reads `"Type.Method"`), so consumers read the receiver name directly; `examples/docgen/loader.go` prefers `fn.Recv` and keeps the split-on-`.` fallback for values built elsewhere.
- [x] **Error tracebacks**: unwinding `Trap`/`Panic` collect `Frames` as `name at file:line`, now with the source line under each entry (syntax.File.Src / disk read, gated to files the package loaded), host panics lifted into `*Panic` at the exec boundary (frames + recoverable like Go + `Panic.GoStack` carries the host goroutine stack via `debug.Stack()` so panics inside builtins/host handlers show their Go file:line), a `(deferred call)` entry for panics escaping deferred calls, param-coerce positions, decl-pos fallback, `BinOp`/`UnOp` symbol names (`unsupported types: int64 + bool`), `*GoValue` panic payloads unwrapped, a 10000-frame stack cap + head/tail render elision (1000 entries).
- [x] **Builtin frames in tracebacks**: a panic propagating out of `c.Fn` appends a `File "...", line N, in strings.Repeat() (builtin)` entry attributed to the caller's OpCall site (host panics are lifted into `*Panic` at that boundary too, so the entry sticks).
- [x] **Generic names in tracebacks**: frames render `Id[int]()` — `frameName` rebuilds the suffix from `TParams`+`Binds`.
- [x] **Callee compile-error positions**: `EnsureCompiled` failure now reads `compile <name> declared at <file:line>: <err>` — the trap is still recorded at the caller's OpCall site but names the callee's declaration. (Compile errors carrying a node `Pos` don't exist yet — decl position is the closest anchor.)
- [x] **Missing-argument check**: `add(1)` where `add(a, b)` traps `not enough arguments to add: 1 given, want 2`; excess args on non-vararg calls trap `too many arguments`. Vararg tails still collect zero extras.
- [x] **Traceback header/format**: `panic:`/`runtime trap:` now print `Traceback (most recent call first):` then `File "<path>", line <N>, in <name>()` entries with the source line indented under each — PR #3's layout.
- [x] **inspect host API surface** (issue #19, round-4 notes in [docs/sketch/plan-package-introspection.md](./docs/sketch/plan-package-introspection.md)): `Engine.SourceOf` is exported (bound-shadow bypass for host tools — real decls behind `time.Time`-style bindings reachable via `NewDecl`), `inspect.Sig` gained `ParamFields()`/`ResultFields()` (`[]*Field` unbox of the FFI slices — the host counterpart of `FieldsOf`), `TypeExpr` gained `CanonicalName()` (`"*import/path.Name"` identity spelling, `""` for composites) and `Sub` (re-wraps sub-exprs `Children` misses — generic instantiation bases, array lengths). Host-panic Go stacks were already covered by `Panic.GoStack` (#17) — pinned via the `BoomViaBuiltin` traceback test.

- **Script-side traversal of function bodies** — **still open** as `[ ]`; tracked in `TODO.md`.
- [x] **`convert-define` fuzz round** (thought-guided fuzzing over ~40 edge cases; [docs/sketch/ja/fuzz-convert-define.md](./docs/sketch/ja/fuzz-convert-define.md)): fixed — `info.Structs` keyed by bare name collided across same-named types in different packages (rekeyed to canonical `pkgpath.Name` via `model.DeclKey`); discovered (non-explicit) pairs never materialized `StructInfo` so their call sites emitted undefined functions (generator now materializes via `model.StructInfoFromDecl`); ptr→ptr fast path discarded the converted result and fired at any pointer depth (`**T` passed as `*T`) — now guarded to depth 1 and assigns `dst = convert...`; `**T`/`[]**T`/`map[K]**T` handle correctly through generic nil-check paths; ptr→value / value→ptr / ptr→ptr expression modes (anonymous funcs) added for nested positions; `[N]T` arrays were treated as slices (now `isArray` vs `isSlice`, element-wise loop); map keys compared by declKey only (`""`==`""` for builtins) — now `SameType` + leaf casts (`int64(key)`, `destination.Key(key)`); named leaf types bare-assigned (now `dstT(src)` casts when named or both-numeric); unexported fields emitted (`dst.token`) — now skipped via `token.IsExported`; `c.Map(dst.ID, src)` panicked on `SplitN[1]` — args are now validated as selector chains rooted at the actual param names; `c.Map`/`c.Convert`/`c.Compute` dst typos silently fell back to name matching — now an error; `define.Rule` accepted `func(T) R` while call sites pass `(ctx, ec, src)` — signature is now checked; `c.Convert` converter packages were never recorded in `info.Imports`; `info.PackagePath` is now set from the define file's directory (self-package types no longer self-import — fixes an import cycle); colliding converter names (`a.User->b.User` + `a.User->c.User`) get deterministic disambiguation suffixes; map-key struct pairs are auto-discovered; `exprToString` renders via `go/printer` so `c.Compute` accepts arbitrary expressions.
- [x] **`convert-define` fuzz residuals** (same round — [issue #22](https://github.com/podhmo/minigo/issues/22), all four items landed): leaf-type mismatches now emit a generate-time warning on the converter's doc comment + `slog` (`int`→`string`, `[]int`→`[]string` elements — the honest raw assignment is kept so a missing `define.Rule` remains the intended fix); `c.Map`/`c.Convert`/`c.Compute` accept nested field paths (`c.Map(dst.Inner.ID, src.ID)`, `c.Map(dst.Flat, src.In.V)`) — pointer intermediates nil-guarded on read, nil-initialised on write, explicit leaf maps emit after auto-matches so they override an ancestor's copied leaf; `model.ConvertTag`/`TypeRule.ValidatorFunc` dead surface removed (all field maps now flow through one `pair.Mapping.Maps` channel, the `convert:`-tag automatch path is gone); `SameType` per-field semantics for same-name cross-package types documented in the README's new semantics section. Coverage: `internal/nested_test.go` (paths + bad-segment errors), `warnings_test.go` golden, e2e `SrcNested`→`DstNested` incl. nil-pointer runs.
- [x] **`define` generics API** (issue #13, experiment — [docs/sketch/ja/experiment-define-generics.md](./docs/sketch/ja/experiment-define-generics.md)): `define.Convert[Dst, Src]`/`define.Rule[Src, Dst]` are generic functions and `c.Convert[Dst, Src]`/`c.Compute[T]` are generic methods on go1.27+ toolchains, split by `//go:build go1.27` (a file-level release constraint also lifts that file's `-lang` so generic methods compile inside the `go 1.26` module; `!go1.27` keeps the `any` variants for older toolchains). `c.Map` stays `any` on purpose — mapped pairs may differ in type (`[]SrcItem`→`[]DstItem` through a registered pair), which no signature can express. The DSL source is unchanged; `minigo` already filtered package files by build constraints (`resolve.ReadPackageFiles` → `go/build` `MatchFile`, toolchain ReleaseTags — the same file set `go list` picks). Fixed along the way: explicit instantiation on DSL calls (`define.Convert[D, S](...)`, `c.Convert[D, S](...)`, `c.Map[T](...)`) bypassed special-form dispatch — `compiler.call` and the `mappingWalker` now unwrap `IndexExpr`/`IndexListExpr`/`ParenExpr` (before: silently generated an empty file / dropped the mapping). Corpus: convert-define fuzz 23/23 expected.
- [x] **`convert-define` stmt/expr unification** (issue #48, experiment — [docs/sketch/ja/experiment-convert-define-frag.md](./docs/sketch/ja/experiment-convert-define-frag.md)): every conversion shape returns one `frag{stmts, expr}` (no `dst == ""` dual path, no IIFEs); temporaries come from a per-field `fresh` allocator that skips visible identifiers; struct type names are qualified in the emit pass (template execution no longer registers imports — that side effect had made goimports silently add the src/dst imports); a goimports syntax failure now fails the run with all errors, numbered excerpts and the converter/field that emitted them instead of writing unformatted code; tests fail when goimports has to add an import.
- [x] **`convert-define`: `ConversionPair.Variables` was never populated** (found by the #48 experiment): no DSL call filled it, so the template's `var {{ .Name }} {{ .Type }}` loop always rendered nothing. Deleted (`model.Variable`, the field, the template loop and the name reservation).
- [x] **`convert-define`: temporaries vs. same-package identifiers** (#48 residual — a real bug on main, not just theoretical): with a same-package `type item struct{...}` and a `[]*item -> []item` field, main emitted `for i, item := range ...` and then `var z item`, failing with `item (local variable) is not a type`. `generator.PackageIdents` now collects the top-level identifiers of the output directory's .go files (syntax only, no type check, unparsable files contribute what the parser recovered) and `fresh` skips them (`item2`).
- [x] **`convert-define`: DSL evaluation errors do not say who has to act** (#48, found while writing the README's Debugging section): handlers now return `internal.DefineError{Pos, Msg}` positioned at the offending `c.Map`/`c.Convert`/`c.Compute` call (it used to point at the enclosing `define.Convert`), and the CLI renders `define file X is invalid at L:C: c.Map: ... Fix the define file at that position` with an excerpt and, when `define.Convert` was reached through a helper, the DSL call frames. Needed the core change below.
- [x] **`runtime.Trap` keeps the host error it was raised from** (`Trap.Err` + `Unwrap`): the VM flattened every special-form/builtin/host error into `Reason: err.Error()`, so a host could not `errors.As` its own typed error back out of a trap (convert-define needed it for `DefineError`). All eight `Reason: err.Error()` sites now also set `Err`; VM-raised traps keep `Err == nil`. `TestSpecialFormErrorUnwrap` pins the typed error and the trap's frames.
- [x] **`convert-define`: provenance for type errors** (#48 residual, [issue #49](https://github.com/podhmo/minigo/issues/49)): `-strict` covers the anticipated leaf mismatches; the opt-in `-check` runs `go build -overlay` on the output inside its package (nothing written first), traces each error in the generated file to its converter/field with an excerpt, and reports errors elsewhere as inconclusive (fix the input first). Never default: regeneration must keep working while the package does not compile. Collecting `-strict`-escaping cases for #49 (usecasefuzz `c20`, `c21`, `check01`–`03`, `neg04`) found and fixed: the template's own `context`/`errors`/`fmt`/`model` imports were unknown to the ImportManager, so a `fmt.Stringer` dst field imported `fmt` twice; a dst field written by `c.Compute` or a top-level `c.Map` from another src field was also auto-matched (an ill-typed dead assignment, plus a false `-strict` failure); a `c.Convert` func literal without the `(ctx, ec, src)` params was emitted and called with three args (now a DSL error; two parser tests had pinned 1-param literals).
- [x] **`convert-define`: named composites convert element-wise through their underlying types** (`check03-named-slice` follow-up): `conv` gained a `namedComposite` branch — `underlyingOf` peels declared layers (`type IDs []int`), the ptr/slice/array/map shapes run on the unwrapped types, and the result is re-tagged with the written dst name (`destination.DstIDs(s)`); identical underlyings still take a single cast, and incompatible elements keep the leaf-mismatch warning (`check03` stays BUILD-FAIL, now with `dst.V: no conversion covers int -> string`). `model.StructElemOf` unwraps named layers too, so `type Items []SrcInner` fields discover the `SrcInner`->`DstInner` pair. Cast spellings go through `castExpr` (`(*int)(x)`, not `*int(x)`). Coverage: e2e `SrcShapes`/`DstShapes` `Named*` fields; usecasefuzz `c22-named-composites` (OK).
- **`convert-define`: element-wise conversion through generic instantiations** — **still open** as `[ ]`; tracked in `TODO.md`.
- [x] **`convert-define`: fail on goimports-added imports in normal CLI runs** (#48 diagnostic residual): the test-only `strictImports` switch is gone — `formatCode` always returns a `*missingImportsError` naming each missing path, the name it is referenced by, and the converter/field of its first use with an excerpt; `run` returns before writing, so no output is produced. The usecasefuzz convert-define corpus stays 28/28.
- [x] **`convert-define`: bound syntax-error provenance to the field body** (#48 diagnostic residual): `provenance` now balances `ec.Enter`/`ec.Leave` while scanning backward (element segments nest inside a field), so a line after a field's `Leave` reports `converter X (outside any field)`; the format test that had attributed a `return`-line error to `Items` is corrected.
- [x] **`convert-define`: validate `-tags` before generation** (#48 diagnostic follow-up, found by a blind review): `-tags 'foo &&'` exited successfully and wrote an invalid `//go:build` expression. `-tags` is now parsed with `go/build/constraint` before anything runs and rejected as a command-line problem; the obsolete `// +build` line is no longer emitted. Same round: a define file that does not parse is reported as the user's to fix with every distinct error and a numbered excerpt (it used to be one wrapped line ending in "(and N more errors)"), and the CLI prints the multi-line report as is instead of as an escaped slog attribute.
- [x] **`convert-define`: `-file` errors and `c.Compute` type checks** (#48, found by a satisfaction evaluation): a missing or directory `-file` used to surface as `failed to run definition script: loading define file into interpreter: parse ...: no such file or directory`; `run` now checks it first and says `Fix the command-line arguments`. `c.Compute(dst.Value, src.N)` (int into string) passed `-strict` and failed at `go build`; the interpreter now records `ComputedField.ExprType` for the shapes knowable without go/types (a `src` field path, a non-generic package func with one result) and the generator warns on a mismatch, so `-strict` rejects it. Unknown shapes stay silent. Found while testing: packages referenced only inside a `c.Compute` expression were never recorded in `info.Imports` (goimports had been adding them; the integration test passed only because a `c.Convert` in the same define file registered the same package); the walker now registers every `pkg.Name` in the expression.
- [x] **`go.mod` language version (`-lang`) is not enforced** — gated, matching gc's per-file semantics: `syntax.CheckLang` walks each parsed file before indexing and reports the first too-new feature with gc's exact wording and suffix (`(-lang was set to go1.x; check go.mod)`, or `(file declares //go:build go1.y)` when the file's own release tag declares the effective lang). Effective lang mirrors `cmd/compile`: the enclosing module's `go` directive (`resolve.ModuleLang` walks up to the nearest `go.mod` — a module with no `go` directive compiles at go1.16 like the toolchain — `PackageMeta.Lang` carries it, `File.LangMod` receives it at parse sites), flatly *replaced* by a file-level `//go:build go1.x` floored at go1.21, and unbounded outside any module (`LoadFile` on module-less files, REPL snippets — like `go run` on loose files). Covered gates: type parameters on decls and aliases (go1.18/1.23), generic methods (go1.27), `any` (go1.18 — decls, builtin args like `make([]any)`, conversions, `new(T)`), embedded `~`/union elements (go1.18), index/index-list instantiation incl. `pkg.F[T]` selectors with type-shaped indices (go1.18, function/type wording via package decl kinds), implicit `Id(1)` instantiation of declared generic funcs (go1.18), literal range-over-int (go1.22), `min`/`max`/`clear` (go1.21), `new(expr)` (go1.26); shadowing mirrors gc — package-level decls and scope-aware function-level decls (`min := ...`, `type any = int` in a block, signature params) keep the old meaning. Deliberately ungated (need type info minigo never computes — documented permissiveness): range over int *values* and function values (go1.22/1.23), `new(ident)` non-literal args (go1.26), `pkg.F[T]` with a bare-ident index (ambiguous with package-var indexing), imported-name implicit instantiation (decl kinds are per-package), 1.27 inference relaxations, promoted-field literal keys. Testdata: `testdata/langgate/*` pinned by `TestLangGate`; `testdata/go127` carries the `//go:build go1.27` its generic methods now require.
- [x] **inspect: `Implementers` — the index-level subtype lookup** — `inspect.Implementers(p, iface)` returns the type decls of p whose method set covers iface's requirements — named specs plus whatever embedded interfaces pull in transitively: declared, promoted, and alias-borrowed members (`MethodSet` now borrows an alias's target set, since `GB = GreetBase` carries the same methods); signatures compare via `SameType` — params/results/variadicity — so `Add(a,b int) int` satisfies but the `string`-result near-miss and the `[2]int`-not-`...int` miss do not. The method set answers "usable through *T": a pointer-receiver method declared on the type itself still counts. Interface decls satisfy too (an interface embedding the specs, including iface itself), so gen-sync filters `Def(d).Kind == "InterfaceType"` for concrete `-variants=` names; constraint interfaces (`~T`, unions, embedded non-interface types) trap — no value type implements them.
- [x] **gen-sync: bound packages introspectable through `inspect.SourceOf`** — `Explorer.Lookup` resolves a package's decl table via `SourceOf` instead of `PackageOf`, so a bound-shadowed path under the subtree yields its real source decls rather than host pseudo-decls with no index; `TestBoundPackage` `Bind()`s `app/internal/bound` and asserts `BoundRef`→`bound.Marked` earns `requiredgen` anyway.
- [x] **inspect: non-trapping `MReqs`/`IEmbeds`** — both report nil on non-interface *type* decls (non-type decls still trap), so scripts iterate directly without the `Def(d).Kind == "InterfaceType"` pre-gate; gen-sync's `RequiresMethod` dropped the spec-shape ceremony.
- [x] **inspect: structured `Position` view** — `Decl`, `Field`, and `Import` `Pos` fields and `inspect.Pos(d)` return `*inspect.Position{File, Line, Column}` (nil for host symbols) instead of a `"file:line:col"` string; `Pos.String()` keeps the old spelling, so scripts compare fields rather than split text.
- [x] **`inspect`: `SymbolID` as an askable question** — the script-facing `inspect.SymbolID` returns `*runtime.SymbolID` (nil for non-named exprs) instead of `runtime.NIL`-disguised values, so `sid == nil` is valid Go and correct under minigo; scanx's `TypeRefName` keys on the id's fields directly rather than re-parsing `CanonicalName` text.
- [x] **inspect: enum members and value-spec types** — `inspect.DeclType(d)` returns the type expression declared on a var/const decl (the explicit annotation, or the type an empty const spec inherits via `Decl.InheritedType`; nil for untyped specs, trap for other kinds); `inspect.EnumMembers(typeDecl)` collects the package's const decls explicitly typed with it, in source order — the go-scan `IsEnum`/`EnumMembers` shape over the index, linked by `TypeExpr.SymbolID` so untyped and selector-typed consts never match. gen-sync's enum rule dropped its raw-line `HasConstOfType`/`SpecHasType` scan for `len(inspect.EnumMembers(d)) > 0`.
- [x] **inspect: alias vs defined type decls** — `inspect.IsAlias(d)` reads `TypeSpec.Assign` directly, partitioning every source type symbol into alias|defined (orthogonal to enum-ness — a const may still be typed with the alias — and to the underlying shape `Def` reads); gen-sync dropped its raw-line `IsAlias`/`DeclLine`/`IsAliasLine`/`PosFile`/`PosLine`/`LinesOf` helpers.
- [x] **inspect: instantiation bases in `TypeExpr.Children`** — `Children()` leads an `IndexExpr`/`IndexListExpr`'s children with the base (`List[Inner]` → `List` then `Inner`), so recursive walks reach generic containers through instantiations of them; `SameType` now distinguishes `Pair[int]` from `Cage[int]`; gen-sync's `Safe`/`Vault` fixture pins `requiredgen` earned through the base alone; convert-define's `registerImports` dropped its `Sub` special-case.
- [x] **inspect: `TypeFields` — member elements of composite type expressions** — `inspect.TypeFields(te)` returns a struct spelling's fields (names, type, tag) or an interface spelling's elements (named method specs vs Embedded/constraint elements), the TypeExpr-level counterpart of `Fields`; reflect-backed expressions answer too (host structs carry tags; interfaces flatten embeds to method specs). gen-sync's `hasRequiredTag` descends into anonymous composites, so `Inline`/`ListAnon` earn `requiredgen`.
- [x] **inspect: `MethodSet` — flattened method sets with promotion** — `inspect.MethodSet(d)` returns a type's declared methods plus members promoted through embedded fields, walked transitively: `Method{Name, Sig, Decl, Via}` (Decl nil for interface specs, Via names the promoting decl). The set answers "usable through *T": declared methods count with either receiver, while promotion follows the value method-set rule (`struct{ T }` lifts non-pointer receivers, `struct{ *T }`/interface embeds lift all, and a pointer embed on the path down keeps deeper pointer receivers visible); members promote breadth-first so shallower spellings shadow deeper ones; alias and generic-instantiation embeds resolve to the owning decl; cycles terminate; output is sorted by name. gen-sync's `scanx.HasMethod` reads the set, so `EmbedEvent` joins `-variants=` — and interface decls are gated out of it (their own specs are their set, a different question `RequiresMethod` asks).
- [x]**`[]byte` boundary marshal: byte fast-path + `io.ReadFull` write-back** ([#362](https://github.com/podhmo/minigo/issues/362); probe: minigo-usecasefuzz `bytes/`): `toReflectValue`'s Slice case now marshals uint8-elem script slices in one pass via `scriptBytes` instead of boxing a reflect.Value per byte (~150ns/B+4 allocs/B at 1MiB → ~5ns/B+0 allocs/B), and `callReflectFunc`'s arg write-back mirrors it (one `rv.Bytes()` read + a tight `Tag` loop) — an addressable `[N]byte` array keeps the generic loop. `io.ReadFull` rebinds as a raw `*runtime.BuiltinFunc` so `borrowBytes` sees the script slice before `goNative` flattens it and commits the filled bytes back — previously `h.fn`'s `byteSlice` copy dropped every byte the reader wrote. A packed `Bytes []byte` representation stays deferred on #362.
- [x] **`map[string]*[3]int` interior writes still trap** (found while reviewing the map-element lvalue gate, PR #364): `pa["a"][1] = 9` on a map of *pointer-to-array* values is legal Go (the stored pointer derefs to shared storage) but trapped `index assign on *runtime.Struct` — `IndexRef`'s map-element gate only saw the copy-elem vs shared-elem split on the map's element, not the interior pointer hops past it. The root cause was deeper: `makeComposite`'s `KindPointer` case built a `*runtime.Struct` for *any* pointee, so `*[3]int` literals stored a bogus empty struct (even `pa["a"][0]` reads trapped). Pointer composites now build their pointee through the same `compositeOf` kind switch a direct `T{...}` literal uses — `*[3]int` stores `*Cell{*Slice}` — and `setIndex`/`setField` resolve ref chains past a gated map element via `refThrough`.
- [x] **`map[string]struct{F []int}` `m[k].F[i] = v` traps** (accepted leniency of the same gate): a field select off a *copy* struct element must trap for `m[k].F = v`, but writing into the field's own backing store is legal Go when F is reference-shaped. `refThrough` resolves a write's base ref (`IndexRef`/`FieldRef`/`DerefRef` chains) to a `(value, writable)` pair — a struct/array hop inherits its container's writability while a reference-shaped hop restores it — so `m[k].F[i]`, `m[k].P.x`, `*m[k].P = v`, and `m[k].M[k2] = v` all land, while `m[k].F = v`, `m[k].a3[i]`, `m[k].g2.x`, and `m[k].ms2[k2].x` still trap. Pinned by `testdata/maplval` (`TestMapElemLvalueTraps`/`TestMapElemLvalueWrites`).
- [x] **gen-sync: `syncFile` swallows I/O errors into exit 0** — `os.WriteFile`/`os.ReadFile` failures print to stdout and count as "no change"; a read-only target leaves a stale file while reporting `N file(s) updated`, and `-check` can miss drift the same way.
- [x] **gen-sync: index-dropped `.go` files are invisible** — unreadable ones are now errors (the whole run refuses, since decls *and* import edges vanish), readable-but-excluded ones warn `not in the package index`, and a malformed build constraint is an error too (go/build's MatchFile drops it with an error, not a miss); detection lives in gen-sync (resolver-side reporting still residual).
- [x] **gen-sync: writes escape the scanned directory** — `syncFile` now refuses paths outside the dir arg (rel path leaves root); shadowed decls still resolve from the other tree silently.
- [x] **gen-sync: directive-level diff reporting** — `-`/`+` lines name every dropped/added directive (multiset diff, so a removed duplicate counts too) and the summary appends `; dropped N, added M`.
- [x] **gen-sync: positional args past the first are silently discarded** — `NArg > 1` now exits 2 naming the extras.
- [x] **gen-sync: degraded no-module scans rewrite managed blocks** — collect walks up for a `go.mod` ancestor and refuses outside any module (and on a synthetic `<dir>` path).
- [x] **gen-sync: CRLF files miss the sentinel** — `scanx.leadingComment` trims `\r`; mixed-EOL files also keep each line's own terminator on write (untouched LF lines no longer become CRLF).
- [x] **`resolve.LocateDir`: `%!w(<nil>)` when the path is a file** — `entry directory %q not found: %w` wraps a nil error on the `!st.IsDir()` branch (should say `not a directory`).
- [x] **fmt `%#v`: nil interface fields/elements print `<nil>`** (`fmt_sharpv_ifacenil`): Go prints the static interface type — `S{E:error(nil), I:interface {}(nil)}`, `[]interface {}{1, interface {}(nil)}` — but minigo prints `S{E:<nil>, I:<nil>}` and `[]any{1, <nil>}` (the `any` spelling differs as well). Seen through `fmt.Printf("%#v")` and the REPL's `:dump`; the untyped `runtime.Nil` in a field or element carries no declared type, so the fix likely needs the field/element typedef (`fmtValue.et`) in `nilGoSyntax`.
- [x] **`*s = T{...}` inside a pointer method is lost when the receiver is taken implicitly** (`ptrrecv_derefassign`, found while running `go/parser` on grafana sources — realworld `grafana-openapi`): `var v S; v.Init(1)` and `pp.s.Init(1)` with `func (s *S) Init(a int) { *s = S{a: a}; s.b = 2 }` left `v`/`pp.s` unchanged, breaking `go/scanner.Scanner.Init` (`*s = Scanner{...}`) and so every `go/parser.ParseFile`. An implicit `&v` receiver wraps the variable's struct object in a fresh cell, so the store's cell rebind only reseated that wrapper. `*p = T{...}` now overwrites the pointee struct's fields in place (`overwriteStruct`, from both `assignRef`'s `DerefRef` arm and `setIndirect`) — Go's memory overwrite, visible to every holder of the struct.
- [x] **generic call rejects `[]Iface` for `[]N` with `N Node`** (`generic_sliceelem_static`, found via `go/parser` — `go/ast.walkList[N Node](v Visitor, list []N)`, so `ast.Inspect`/`ast.Walk` trapped `cannot use []ast.Stmt as []N`): `argTypedef`'s `*runtime.Slice` case stored the first element's runtime type in `Elem`, and `Engine.elemOf` prefers `td.Elem`, so `[]Node{&A{}}` bound N to an unnamed `*A` typedef. A resolvable declared element type of the slice now wins; the first element's dynamic type stays the fallback for element types the index cannot resolve (function-local types).
- [x] **CLI/diagnostics rough edges** (found in the grafana exploration).
- [x] CLI/diagnostics rough edges > **`minigo run <file.go>` rejects files** (`is not a directory`), though the README advertises "dir, file, or import path". A ref ending in `.go` is now a one-file package, like `go run file.go` (build constraints ignored, siblings not read).
- [x] CLI/diagnostics rough edges > **`-h` is an unknown flag**: there is no usage text to discover subcommands and flags. `minigo -h`/`help [command]` and `<command> -h` now print usage to stdout; argument errors print the subcommand's usage to stderr and exit 2.
- [x] CLI/diagnostics rough edges > **the error traceback is printed inside one slog line** with escaped `\n`/`\t`, so a runtime trap is hard to read in a terminal. The CLI now prints `minigo: <error>` and the traceback as plain lines on stderr; difffuzz parses that form.
- [x] CLI/diagnostics rough edges > **recover-then-repanic loses the origin**: `go/parser.ParseFile`'s bailout handler re-panics, and the traceback keeps only the defer frames. A panic raised by a deferred call while its frame unwinds now continues with the unwinding panic's frames (the original site), like Go's traceback.
- [x] Interpreter hot paths found by profiling realworld `grafana-openapi` > **cache struct field typedefs**: every field store re-resolved all field types from AST (`setField` → `fieldTypedefs` → `Engine.fieldTypes` → `elemTypeRef` → `resolveTypeRef`) — 55% of allocations. `Engine.fieldTypes` now caches its result on the `TypeDef` (`CachedFieldTypes`/`SetFieldTypes`, an `atomic.Value`; the Binds-swapping shallow copy in intrinsics resets it). grafana-openapi: 4.6 GB → 2.0 GB allocated, ~5.5 s → ~4.0 s.
- [x] Interpreter hot paths found by profiling realworld `grafana-openapi` > **`append` scalar conversion goes through `VM.Call`** (`builtins.go`, `v.Call(et, a)`): each one paid `callBounded`'s `goroutineID()` (`runtime.Stack` parse). `VMCaller.Convert` now runs `T(x)` directly on the calling goroutine and `append` uses it: 400k numeric appends ~5.4 s → ~0.2 s, grafana-openapi ~4.4 s → ~3.4 s. Other intrinsics that call `v.Call` on a `*TypeDef` could follow.
- [x] Interpreter hot paths found by profiling realworld `grafana-openapi` > **every `sync.Mutex.Lock`/`RLock` spawned a helper goroutine** (`blockingHostMethods` in `callReflectFunc`): 29,522 spawns from `go/token`'s mutex. `hostMember` now tries `TryLock`/`TryRLock` inline (`uncontendedLock`) and keeps the watched helper for contention only: grafana-openapi ~3.25 s → ~3.06 s, and the macOS CPU profile no longer pins half its samples on `pthread_cond_signal`.
- [x] Interpreter hot paths found by profiling realworld `grafana-openapi` > **interface satisfaction re-derived signatures on every check** (regressed by #473/#523's signature pass): each assertion/conversion re-synthesized the interface's required-method shells (`ifaceSigFuncs`) and re-ran `SigIdentical` for every member, +1.5 GB allocated on grafana-openapi (~3.05 s → ~4.0 s after rebasing onto main). The shells are now cached on the interface `TypeDef` (`CachedIfaceSigs`), and comparisons are memoized per (requirement, method) pair in an engine-wide `vm.Hooks.SigEq`: ~4.0 s → ~3.47 s, 3.58 GB → 2.26 GB.
- [x] Interpreter hot paths found by profiling realworld `grafana-openapi` > **interface caches stored incomplete results and skipped the requirement names** (follow-up to #531, ported from a skill-only agent trial): `ifaceSigFuncs` cached its shells even when an embedded element failed to resolve, so a set built before a package became loadable stuck. The shells and the newly cached flattened requirement names (`ifaceReqSet`, `CachedIfaceReqs`) are now stored only when every embed resolved. The pair memo became a typed `runtime.SigMemo` (`vm.Hooks.SigMemo`) with a unit test.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **`lang` domain first hunt**: `-domain lang` (structs/methods/embedding, interfaces and typed nils, labels/goto/defer/recover, range-over-func, channels/goroutines, user generics — `tools/difffuzz/lang.go`) is wired up and compiles under gc but has never been hunted; run `/difffuzz hunt lang` and pin one case per root cause.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **difffuzz harness: go-side unrecoverable death poisons the batch verdict** (found by the first `lang` hunt, seed 20261005): when a probe's spawned goroutine panics (outside the probe's own `recover`), go dies mid-program and every later probe records `want=""` — ~71 of 80 emitted cases were this phantom, not real divergences. `EvalProbes` marks probes with a missing oracle line `Skip` (with the oracle's death cause), and `emitCase` now verifies each reduced case standalone — unemitting cases that can't re-diverge and oracle-broken programs whose `want.stdout` would be a crash truncation.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **`s[i].PtrMethod()` on a slice element mutates a copy** (`sliceelem_ptrmeth`, `lang_value_4b500c90`): `for i := range s { s[i].Scale(2) }` on `[]Point` kept `{1 2}` where go writes `{2 4}` — the selector's index operand now emits the element's storage ref (`(&s[i]).M()` lowering) instead of a copy; a base the ref cannot walk (map element, `m[k].f[i]`) re-bases or reads the element value so copy semantics hold.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **value method sets include pointer-receiver methods** (`methodset_ptrrecv`, `lang_value_fe7c68b7`): `fmt.Println(Counter{1})` dispatched `(*Counter).String` (`#1`) where go prints `{1}`, and `var s fmt.Stringer = Counter{1}` was accepted where go rejects it at compile time. `methodSetOfValue`/`methodSetOfU` now thread "reached through a pointer" (deref, anonymous `*T` typedef, embedded pointer field), so a value's set keeps value receivers only; and host-side interface probes — fmt's Stringer/error dispatch, the `io.Reader`/`io.Writer`/`sort.Interface`/`sync.Locker` adapters, `error.Unwrap`, and `toReflectValue`'s `adaptIface` — check membership on the arg's box (`&w` keeps its pointer receivers) instead of member existence.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **binary operands read eagerly in function frames** (`binop_evalorder`, `lang_value_ba4e8d62`): `return c + inc()` evaluated to 5 where go gives 6 — gc defers reading a pure left operand to the operation point, so the call's side effects show in it (3+3). Compile now emits the right operand first plus an `OpSwap` when the left is pure (no calls/receives), keeping lexical order whenever the left carries a call.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **interface-conversion panics name internal runtime types** (`assert_typename`, `lang_panic-message_592347f7`/`b1688c6c`, `lang_spurious-panic_67539ea8`): a failed `a.(int)` reported `*runtime.Cell` for `*main.Point` and `slice` for `[]int`. `typeNameOf` now peels pointer boxes into leading stars (`*runtime.Cell` of Point → `*main.Point`, one per hop), and the value cases spell their declared typedef through `spelledTyp` — slices/maps/chans via `Typ`, typed/iface nils via `Typ`, funcs via `TypGoSpelling` of the decl — matching Go's conversion-panic text byte for byte.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **`%T` drops type arguments on instantiated generics** (`instant_tname`, `lang_type_891f8723`, `lang_value_f73ed1da`): `Pair[string,int]` printed `main.Pair` instead of `main.Pair[string,int]`. `DisplayName` now appends the bound type arguments in declared parameter order — `[string,int]`, nested instantiations recurse — so `%T`, `%v` type names and conversion-panic text match reflect's spelling.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **inferred type args fail on nil-slice spread calls** (`spread_nilinfer`, `lang__0113bfab`): `Sum(n...)` with `n` a nil `[]int` trapped `undefined: T` — inference read the nil value's runtime shape instead of the declared element type. Now `popArgs` returns the spread slice's element typedef and `inferBinds` unifies the variadic pattern against it, so a nil/empty `[]int` still binds `T=int`. In the emitted case the trap fired inside a goroutine holding a mutex and deadlocked `wg.Wait()` into a HANG.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **unrecovered panic inside `go func` does not crash the process** (`lang__0b3c2a7d`/`b228cfc7`): go dies on the spot (stdout empty); minigo's goroutine panic used to let a sibling released mid-unwind (`wg.Done` inside the dying defer) resume and keep printing (`after wait` reached stdout where go prints nothing). Now the process fails at the unwind-tail of the root frame — the instant the panic is decided fatal — and `v.call` checks `proc.done` at entry, so a sibling resumed by the dying defer dies at its next call instead of producing output. `TestHostParkLeak`/`testdata/hostpark` was rewritten to a deterministic signal-in-builtin form (the leaked goroutine must enter the host call while the process is still alive). The emitted cases stay unpinnable (Go's own exit-vs-print race is nondeterministic); pinned as `TestWaitUnblockThenPanic`/`WaitUnblockRead` in `testdata/concurrency`. A residual HANG mechanism — a sibling parked inside a real host blocking call (`mu.Lock` on a held mutex, `wg.Wait`) can't be woken by channel select — is fixed by running blocking host methods on a helper goroutine selected against `proc.done`, so process death unwinds the script frame (the helper leaks with the real call — the documented host-park limit); pinned as `TestHostBlockingCallReleased`/`HostLockThenPanic`.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **generic inference binds T to the argument's dynamic type instead of its static type** (`infer_staticbind`): `id(v_err_0)` trapped `undefined: T` and `Describe(v_sh_3)` trapped `cannot use interface value as *Tri` — `inferBinds` unified the param pattern against the value's runtime type, but Go binds T to the argument's declared type (`error`, `Shape`). The compiler now pushes each argument's declared typedef above the args (`argStatics`/`staticTyp`, NIL for expressions carrying none), `popArgs` returns the statics beside the spread element typedef, and `unifyType` prefers the static typedef — falling back to the runtime type for args that carry none.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **interface `==` misses the uncomparable panic when one operand carries a typed nil** (`ifaceeq_uncomparable`): `any(v_is_0) == v_any_5` (nil `[]int` vs a live `[]int`) printed `false` where Go panics `comparing uncomparable type []int` — `eqlValue` only fired the panic for live `*Slice`/`*Map` pairs, and the `IfaceNil`/`TypedNil` branches returned false before it could. A `uncomparableDynamicTyp` pre-check now panics whenever both dynamic typedefs name the same uncomparable type, covering nil-vs-live, live-vs-nil, nil-vs-nil, map and `!=` pairs; mismatched dynamic types and `== nil` still return false.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **a panic raised inside a deferred call leaks into the next unrelated recover()** (`deferpanic_stalerecover`): batch probes whose recover ran on normal return kept reporting the *previous* probe's panic (`err:invalid memory address`, `rec:close of closed channel`) — `runOneDefer` installs the deferred panic on `inflight`, and the normal-return unwind path propagated `p = v.inflight` without restoring the saved outer state, so each enclosing unwind read the panic as its outer panic and the consuming try-recover restored it into inflight. The `r==nil` drain end now restores `v.inflight`/`v.unwindDepth` to the saved state like the `r!=nil` path.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **a panic inside a synchronous host callback fails the process instead of reaching the caller's recover** (`synconce_panicrecover`): `once.Do(func(){ panic("boom") })` exited `panic: boom` where go prints the recovered value and continues — `callReflectFunc` runs blocking host calls on a helper goroutine, so the callback's `vc.Call` took the foreign-goroutine spawn path and the child VM's panic hit `failProc`/`p.fail` like a real goroutine crash. Calls arriving from a helper registered on `proc.syncCallers` now spawn a `syncCall` child VM whose panic propagates back through the join (helper recover → `panic(r.p)` on the calling goroutine) instead of failing the process; genuinely foreign callbacks (WaitGroup.Go, time.AfterFunc) keep the crash semantics.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **an embed path visiting a type as a value hides a sibling `*T` embed's pointer receivers** (`methodset_embedpath`): `struct{ A; *T }` with `func (*T) M` reported `false` for `.(interface{ M() })` where go gives `true` — `methodSetOfU`/`methodFuncs` shared one `seen` map across embedding paths, so visiting `A.T` as a value consumed the visit before the direct `*T` embed could contribute its pointer-receiver methods. The marks now lift on unwind (per-path cycle detection like `methodInner`/`embedTypeDepth`), so each path keeps its own contribution.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **`x.(func(int) int)` asserts by kind, not signature** (`funcsig_assert`): every `*runtime.Function`/`Closure`/`BoundMethod`/`BuiltinFunc` value satisfied an anonymous func typedef (`td.Kind == KindFunc && td.Spec == nil`), so `func(Point) Point` asserted to `func(int) int` where Go panics. The func case now runs `funcAssert`: the value's declared signature typedef (`funcSig`, receiver-aware for method expressions) is compared `TypIdentical`-style against the asserted typedef — matching signatures assert, mismatched ones take Go's `interface conversion:` panic/comma-ok-false path. Host funcs boxed in `GoValue`/`BuiltinFunc.Target` compare their real reflect signatures against `TypGoSpelling`, and `typSpelling`'s variadic arm now spells `...T` (was `[]T`, conflating `func(...int)` with `func([]int)`).
- [x] difffuzz harness > **`$GOROOT/test` corpus sweep**: top-level `// run` files are all swept — remaining items there are boundary-class (the GC-finalizer family (`deferfin`/`tinyfin`/`mallocfin`/`finprofiled`/`stackobj`/`stackobj3`), `peano.go` frame limit, `unsafe.Pointer`/`unsafe.String`/`unsafe.Offsetof`/`runtime.FuncForPC` traps (pointer-model and host-PC boundaries; unsafe.Pointer tracked in #40), `linkmain_run.go` nondeterministic tmpdir, throughput HANGs; per-file fix log in `TODO-archive.md`). **Next sweep: subdirectories** — never covered by `-goroot-tests`; run `corpus <dir>` per the `corpus` section of `tools/difffuzz/README.md` (subdir list, flag ordering, `make` wrapper caveat are documented there). Pilot (2026-10-06) already confirms yield: `typeparam` 321 → 12 SILENT / 24 TRAP, `ken`+`interface`+`chan` 80 → 3 SILENT / 2 HANG / 6 TRAP, `syntax`+`abi`+`stress` 61 → 2 SILENT / 3 HANG / 2 TRAP — unpinned divergences awaiting triage; `fixedbugs`, `simd`, `dwarf`, `codegen` and the `*.dir` multi-file packages not yet run. Triage → pin one `PENDING` case per root cause → fix per the difffuzz loop.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **package-level var init ignores dependency order** (`initorder_dep`): `_ = f("_", c, b)` must run c,b's initializers first — minigo initializes in declaration order (`dcb_a`) where go prints `abc_d`. Corpus: fixedbugs/issue22326.go (also issue43444.go).
- [x] difffuzz harness > **`%T` on `*iface`-of-nil prints `*<nil>`** (`iface_spelling`): `fmt.Printf("%T", &x)` on a nil `any`/`interface{}` var reads the nil dynamic value instead of the static pointer-to-interface type. Corpus: fixedbugs/issue49665.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **multi-valued assertion in `var` decl panics instead of comma-ok** (`multiassign_commaok`): `var a, b = x.(T)` fails `interface conversion` instead of assigning zero+ok; same family as the `multi-assign from non-tuple` TRAPs (map-index/chan-recv forms). Corpus: fixedbugs/issue53619.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **underflowing negative float const keeps -0** (`negzero_const`): `var m = -1e-10000` stays Signbit-true; go rounds the untyped constant to +0. Corpus: fixedbugs/issue12621.go (+issue12577.go).
- [x] difffuzz harness > **typed nil pointer equals nil interface** (`nilptr_ifaceeq`): `(*ET)(nil) == error(nil)` evaluates true. Corpus: fixedbugs/issue19911.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **makeslice limit counts elements, not bytes** (`makeslice_zerolen`): `make([]struct{}, maxInt)` traps `len out of range` though zero-size elements allocate 0 bytes. Corpus: fixedbugs/issue29190.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **absent vs empty struct tag treated as different types** (`structtag_ident`): `struct{x int}` vs `struct{x int ""}` compare unequal/unassignable; the same tag-insensitive identity gap shows in `map[interface{}]` keys (`mapkey_structtag`). Corpus: fixedbugs/issue15439.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **two-var range over nil ptr-to-array derefs** (`nilarr_refrange`): `for i, _ := range arr` nil-panics though go never reads the `_` element. Corpus: fixedbugs/bug454.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **`len(*p)` on an addressable nil array-pointer derefs** (`nilarr_len`): the operand must stay a static constant; only non-addressable (`*func()`) operands deref. Corpus: fixedbugs/issue72844.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **value-method-on-nil-ptr panic text** (`valuemethod_nilptr`): go says `value method main.T.F called using nil *T pointer`; minigo prints a plain nil-deref panic. Corpus: fixedbugs/issue19040.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **func-local type decl shadowing breaks type identity** (`shadowtype_assert`): an outer-T value satisfies an assertion to the shadowing inner-T decl — same-named defined types are conflated across scopes (same root cause as `localtype_identity` from the typeparam sweep). Corpus: fixedbugs/bug148.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **uint64-range float consts fail in case labels** (`pow10_constconv`): `case 1e19` does not match x=1e19 — const conversion for values outside int64 range. Corpus: fixedbugs/issue43480.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **`complex()` escapes exact const arithmetic** (`complexconst_eval`): `imag(1i + complex(0,2)/3 - 5i/3)` gives -2.2e-16, not 0. Corpus: fixedbugs/issue43908.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **reflect.Call cannot take script-declared arg types** (`reflectcall_scripttype`): `minireflect: cannot resolve type Empty` — the reflect facade cannot bridge script types into call args. Corpus: fixedbugs/issue26335.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **reflect.ValueOf of a typed-nil ptr during package init becomes zero Value** (`reflect_typednil`): `.Type()` fails `call of reflect.Value.Type on zero Value` only in package-init call context. Corpus: fixedbugs/issue30606b.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **map keys of anonymous struct types ignore tags** (`mapkey_structtag`): `map[interface{}]` collapses `struct{int}{}` and `struct{int "x"}{}` — tag-insensitive type identity (same root cause as `structtag_ident`). Corpus: typeparam/issue46591.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **defined-type identity keyed by name, not decl site** (`localtype_identity`): two func-local `type T int` in different funcs compare `==` — same root cause as `shadowtype_assert`. Corpus: typeparam/issue54456.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **func literal inside a generic func keeps AST types in reflect** (`reflectfunclit_typeparam`): `reflect.TypeOf.String()` prints `func(func(R) bool)` — type args not substituted into the literal signature. Corpus: typeparam/issue48645a.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **generic receiver methods drop blank type params** (`blankrecv_typeparam`): `*Foo[_,_]`'s String() is invisible to fmt on `*Foo[string,int]`. Corpus: typeparam/issue50419.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **interface assertion checks method names only, not signatures** (`ifaceassert_methodsig`): `i.(interface{ foo() int })` succeeds for `foo() float64` (type switch too; same root cause as `methsig_assert`). Corpus: typeparam/typeswitch5.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **generic container instantiation loses reflect type identity** (`generic_slice_typeid`): `make([]T,n)` inside a generic keeps `[]main.T`, DeepEqual against `[]string` fails. Corpus: typeparam/map.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **sort.Float64s is not NaN-aware** (`sortfloat_nan`): `[2 NaN -1]` stays unsorted where go sinks NaN first. Corpus: typeparam/ordered.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **delete during range skips the next key** (`maprange_delete`): `for k := range m { delete(m,k) }` deterministically leaves one key. Corpus: typeparam/maps.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **interface method expression traps unrecoverably** (`iface_methodexpr`): `f := I.M; f(nil)` surfaces `runtime trap: type I has no method M` instead of a nil-deref panic. Corpus: typeparam/issue51521.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **local generic type's reflect name misses outer type args** (`localtypeparam_typename`): a generic type declared inside a generic function prints `main.T[int]` where go prints `main.T[int;int]`. Corpus: typeparam/nested.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **pointer-receiver `*s = v` on a named scalar writes to a copy** (`ptrrecv_scalar_write`): `type S string`'s ptr-receiver deref-assign does not reach the caller's var (struct field writes do propagate). Corpus: typeparam/issue47713.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **float64 conversion of huge int constants keeps integer repr** (`float_bigconst`): `float64(16717361816799281152)` prints `16717361816799281152` not `1.6717361816799281e+19`; `9223372036854775808` wraps to negative (float-vs-int const conversion path). Corpus: ken/convert.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **interface→interface assertion ignores method signatures** (`methsig_assert`): asserting to an interface type checks only method names — `error` asserted as `interface{ Error() int }` succeeds (same root cause as `ifaceassert_methodsig`). Corpus: interface/returntype.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **text/template `.Maybe` on ptr-to-interface field fails** (`template_ptrmethod`): `{{if .Maybe}}` on a struct whose field is `*runtime.Cell` panics `can't evaluate field Maybe` — reflect member walk misses pointer-indirect through embedded/iface field. Corpus: chan/select5.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **reflect.Value.Call can't resolve named param types** (`reflect_call-namedparam`): `reflect.ValueOf(F).Call` traps `minireflect: cannot resolve type N` when a func param is a package-level named type — funcTd lacks Pkg/File/Binds context for resolveTypeRef. Corpus: abi/bad_select_crash.go.
- [x] difffuzz harness > **bodiless host func stubs silently return zero** (`bodiless_func_zero`): GOROOT `math/big` asm stubs with `fn.Decl.Body == nil` compile to no-op zero return — `new(big.Int).Add(1,2)` → 0 instead of trapping (should trap like other unsupported host surfaces). Corpus: abi/idata.go.
- [x] gen-sync: error-vocabulary experiment > **`quotedArg` does not unquote `%q`**: describeFailure's dir-argument blame compares the `%q`-quoted operand against `dir` raw, so a dir arg containing `"` or escapes never matches and the blame degrades to "gen-sync bug". Cheap fix is `strconv.Unquote`; left because adversarial dir names are out of the demo's scope.
- [x] gen-sync: error-vocabulary experiment > **`dedupeDirectives` keeps only the first reason** for a shared directive line — when two rules infer the same `//go:generate` line, `-explain` reports only the first rule's reason. Collating reasons would be more informative but the line is what matters downstream.
- [x] gen-sync: error-vocabulary experiment > **`filepath.Base` in `-explain` reasons** can name the wrong file when two scanned files share a basename (e.g. dep package's `events.go` vs the scanned one) — reason strings print the base only. Paths would be precise but noisier; revisit if multi-dir `-deps` output confuses.
- [x] gen-sync: error-vocabulary experiment > **`scanx.Dedupe` is dead code** — kept on purpose: it is exported library surface of the shared helper package, and removing it buys tidiness only while any outside caller (or a script copy-pasted from this one) breaks.
- [x] **test-detect: malformed nested go.mod aborts with an identifiable error** (F8 decision: abort, no warn+skip degrade — [docs/sketch/ja/experiment-test-detect.md](./docs/sketch/ja/experiment-test-detect.md)): every `modulePathOf` failure now carries the go.mod path plus the underlying error, so a broken module under `-root` exits non-zero naming the file.
- [x] **`Engine`: enumerate installed native bindings** (feature request from the minigo-generate round, #390): nothing lets a host or a script ask "which packages are bound in this engine" — `e.binds` already tracks every bound package (std intrinsics and user `Bind`s alike), so this is mostly an accessor (package paths + exported symbols). Motivation: a plugin can then answer "is `go/format` bound?" before importing it, and error vocabulary improves ("that import isn't bound by this runner" instead of a bare resolve failure).
- [x] **REPL: unresolvable import is accepted silently and completes as a non-identifier** (found while checking `:comp`): `import "gopkg.in/yaml.v3"` outside the module's requires raises no error at the `import` line — the resolve failure only traps on first use (`yaml.Marshal`). Meanwhile completion (`scopeNames` over `r.pkg.Scopes[file]`, `complete.go`) offers `package yaml.v3` — the raw path tail, not a valid identifier — since `Materialize` fails and the declared package name is never learned. Decide whether `acceptImport` should resolve eagerly (or at least warn), and keep non-identifier scope keys out of the candidates.
- [x] **REPL: `:ls`/`:cd` accept session import names; `:doc` runs `go doc`** (#430): `REPL.ImportPathOf` maps an import's bound name (alias included; declared package name when it differs from the path tail; dir imports anchored) to its path, and `:ls`/`:cd` also take quoted paths. `:doc <pkg>[.<sym>]` shells out to the Go toolchain's `go doc` — Go's documentation, not minigo's (a bound package may expose fewer symbols; `:help` says so).
- [x] **REPL: `:load <file|dir>`** (#433): reads a .go file or a directory's buildable files into the repl package — decls join the package block (exported and unexported reachable from the prompt), each file keeps its own import scope, initializers run in dependency order plus `init()`, the newest definition wins between prompt and load, re-`:load` replaces what the ref loaded; failures restore the session.
- [x] **REPL: `:ls` labels consts as `var`**: REPL consts are hoisted into read-only `*runtime.Cell`s, and `List` (`repl.go`) prints every hoisted cell as `var` — `const C = 1` lists as `var C`. The cell's `ReadOnly` flag could pick `const`.
- [x] **REPL: `iota` const groups fail at the prompt**: `const (A = iota; B)` traps `undefined: iota` — `hoistSpecs` (`repl.go`) lowers const specs to plain assignments in a step, which has no `iota` binding and drops implicit repetition (`B` gets no value). Loaded files are unaffected (`compile.InitFunc` binds iota). Lowering could substitute the spec index for `iota` and repeat the previous spec's values.
- [x] **`copy(dst, pkgConstString)` traps `copy on non-slice`** (pinned: `copy_pkgconst`): `const c = "ab"; var a [4]uint8; copy(a[:], c)` fails at runtime (go: 2) while `copy(a[:], "ab")` works — a package-level string constant does not reach copy as a string. Breaks interpreting `encoding/base32` (`copy(e.decodeMap[:], decodeMapInitialize)` in `NewEncoding`) and any stdlib package doing the same; worth a `testdata/difffuzz` case.
- [x] **Const-group implicit repetition is implemented three times** (stack review of #429–#440): `index.Build` (`Decl.Inherited`/`InheritedType`), `compile.InitFunc`'s spec walk, and the REPL's `hoistSpecs` each track the previous spec's type/values. A shared helper in `index` yielding the effective type and values per spec would serve all three.
- [x] **Make the process-death concurrency regression deterministic**: `TestWaitUnblockThenPanic` assumed a sibling released by `wg.Done` could never run before panic unwind completed. Park the sibling during unwind and release it after process death, then verify its next script call cannot update the flag.
- [x] **pointer method on a nil slice-typed field traps** (`nilslice_ptrmethod`, found via `go/parser.ParseExpr("a +")` while checking CLI tracebacks): `p.errors.Add(x)` with `errors scanner.ErrorList` still nil and `func (p *ErrorList) Add` traps `cannot use nil ErrorList as *ErrorList` instead of taking `&p.errors`. Any `go/parser` input with a syntax error hits it.
- [x] **`append(s, nil)` stores a bare `runtime.Nil`** (`append_nilelem`, found while probing `nilslice_ptrmethod`): append's per-arg conversion only covered `UConst`/numeric scalars, so a `nil` element skipped the declared element type's zero — reads panicked on member select and `fmt` printed `<nil>` where go prints `[]`. Other writers (`s[i] = nil`, composite literals, `make`) already coerce.
- [x] **binary operand panic order diverges from gc** (`binop_evalorder`, gen seed 20261006): the pure-left deferral ran whenever the left was pure — reordering even all-pure pairs — and classified conversions, type asserts, slice checks and literals wrongly. `int(s[i]) + f(r[j])` reported s[i]'s panic where gc reports r[j]'s; `x.(int) + f(r[j])` and `r[:i][0] + f(r[j])` deferred checks gc materializes eagerly. Now both operands go through the callArgs two-phase: eager ops hoist to temps in lexical order, deferred reads run left-to-right at the op.
- [x] **`print` separated non-string operands with spaces** (`print_space`, corpus): gc concatenates `print`/`println` operands with no separator at all; minigo joined adjacent non-strings with a space. Fixed in builtins; `want.stderr` oracle support added for the `println` side.
- [x] **multi-assign wrote through the post-assign pointer** (`massign_pinbase`/`massign_refpin`, corpus + PR #560 review): `p, p.i = new(T), v` and `s.P, s.P.X = new(T), 7` resolved the second target's pointer base after the first store — gc pins the operand before any store. `refTarget(lhs, pin)` + `OpFieldRef` bit-2 snapshot non-struct ref operands of every kind (Cell/FieldRef/IndexRef), not just cells.
- [x] **`sort` package rejected or over-accepted nil slices** (`sortslice_nil`/`sortnil_typednil`, corpus + review): `sort.Slice`/`SliceIsSorted`/`SliceStable` trapped `first arg must be a slice` on a nil slice, then the fix accepted nil pointers/maps/chans too. `nilSliceArg` accepts only TypedNil slice typedefs — nil slices are a no-op like gc, other typed nils still error.
- [x] **map iterator skipped elements deleted via NaN keys** (`mapiter_nankey`, corpus): `MapIter` re-canonicalized stored keys on the `Pairs` probe, so a NaN display key never matched and the element looked deleted. Probe by stored canonical key instead.
- [x] **const decls initialized after vars they precede** (`initorder_constdep`, corpus): `orderSpecs` emitted vars before consts within a decl block; types2's init order runs consts first, so `var a = f(); const c = f()` mis-ordered.
- [x] **function-local type identity missed the enclosing instantiation** (`localtype_outerargs`/`localtype_closure`, corpus + review): `type X int` inside `F[T]` — and inside a literal closing over `F[T]` — collapsed to one identity across `F[int]`/`F[string]`. `OpLocalType`/`specializeType` now take the enclosing args via `outerTypeArgs` (shared collector); `Function.OuterTParams` carries the param names into closures, and `instArgsSpelling` renders outer args on non-generic locals (`main.X[int]`).
- [x] **`range` over arrays ran live or panicked where gc copies/iterates lazily** (`rangederef_nilarr`/`rangearr_snapshot`/`rangestar_operand`, corpus + review + TODO item): `range a` iterated the array's live backing (writes visible), `for i,v := range *p` skipped the mandatory `*p` evaluation (nil `[0]` escaped the panic), and `*get().P`-shaped operands peeled lazily despite forcing calls. Now: bare array values iterate a per-element copy, refs (Cell/FieldRef/IndexRef/DerefRef) iterate the live pointee, any second LHS operand materializes `*p` eagerly, and `rangeStarLazy` rejects call/receive operands via `lenOperandCalls`.
- [x] **zero-size element addresses compared unequal** (`zerobase_indexeq`, corpus `bug352`): `&x[1] == &x[2]` for `[N][0]byte`/`[]struct{}` was false; gc folds zero-size element addresses to `runtime.zerobase` (true across index refs and slice containers, but distinct array objects stay false — probed boundary).
- [x] **host nil channel in a channel op trapped** (`nilchan_host`, corpus `typeparam/orderedmap.go`): `goValueOf` folds host nil chans (`context.Background().Done()`) to `runtime.NIL` for `v == nil`, then `chanOf` trapped. Untyped nil in a channel op now parks like a typed nil channel (select arms skip, send/recv block, close still panics).
- [x] **type arguments bound alias typedefs** (`targ_alias`, corpus `typeparam/nested.go`): `T[GlobalInt]` with `type GlobalInt = Int` spelled `main.GlobalInt` inside arg lists. `instantiate` peels `KindAlias` args up front so binds hold the canonical target for both spelling and identity.
- [x] **function-local type names lack gc's `·N` scope marker in instantiation args** (`typeparam/nested.go` remaining diff): gc appends `·gen` — a per-package source-order index over non-alias function-local type decls (noder `declCollector`, `qualifiedIdent` embeds it, `SplitVargenSuffix` splits it) — when a local type's name is spelled *inside* an instantiation's arg list (`main.T[int;main.U[int;int]·3]`); the outer name strips it. Needs a decl-order `gen` assigned at compile time and carried on the typedef for arg-position spelling.
- [x] **function-local types inside generic functions don't spell their enclosing type args** (vargen_argspell sibling gap, found while fixing `·gen`): gc implicitly parameterizes every function-local type by the enclosing params — `var l0 L0` inside `F[A]` prints `main.L0[int]` at head, and a `func(U[int])` arg spells `func(main.U[int;int]·2)` (outer;own args), while minigo showed `main.L0` / `func(main.U[int]·2)`. `typeExpr` now emits `OpLocalType` after every compiled type expression: inside an instantiation it wraps any typedef with `OuterSpell` — a display-only copy of the enclosing args that embedded local names and `[outer;own]` brackets spell from — and keeps the identity-bearing `OuterArgs`/binds clone for `Local` decls. Binds merge stays Local-only because named identity compares them.
- [x] **corpus timeout classification**: all 19 ran to a verdict (go vs minigo, 60-90 s timeouts). Boundary (record only): divconst/modconst (ken throughput), issue62515 (`unsafe.Sizeof/Alignof/Offsetof`). Deadlock-detector family (see entry below): bug069/218/gcc61253/issue4323/8475 — gc exits `fatal error: all goroutines are asleep - deadlock!` in <1 s; minigo parks forever. Pure throughput (progress but > 60-90 s): fibish, uglyfib, select5 (prints continuously), issue13169 (10 goroutines × 100k chan ops + recursion), issue59680 (WaitGroup), issue78081 (goroutines × 100k loop), issue79186 (RWMutex+map); issue15141 dies to the OOM killer on `[0xFFFFFFF]byte` arrays (~256 MB each). Not hangs after all: issue16249, issue39541 (both complete clean). Real bug found and fixed: issue20780b (untyped const adoption — `uconst_adapt_operand`).
- [x] **a deferred call's panic swallowed the owner's remaining defers** (`defer_drain_repanic`, found while fixing bug301): `defer d1(); defer d2(){panic}` + `panic()` — gc still runs d1 on top of the new panic (and its recover() sees the NEW panic value); minigo's `failProc` fired while unwinding the deferred call — the popped owner no longer counts in `len(v.frames)` — so procExit killed d1 mid-drain. A `draining` counter now holds failProc off while any defer drain is live.
