- [x] **interface-stored pointers are never equal and uncomparable func pairs never panic** (`iface_dyntyp_pair`, corpus `issue18595` + `issue76008`): `typeOfValue` fabricated anonymous typedefs for `*T` (KindPointer, no `Anon`) and funcs (bare KindFunc) that `TypIdentical` could never judge identical, so the `ifaceEql` pair guard returned false before values compared — `i1 == i1` was false for interface-held pointers and `any(func(){}) == any(func(){})` returned false instead of panicking. `TypIdentical` now recurses on `KindPointer.Elem`, `typeOfValue` carries the real signature for func values (`FuncSigOf` / `reflectFuncTypedef` like `argTypedef`), and `ifaceEql` panics on the type alone for identical uncomparable dynamic types across nil/live wrapper shapes (`any(f) == any(nilFunc)`, `any(map) == any(nilMap)`).
- [x] **untyped float-notation constants don't adopt the operand's type in binary ops** (`uconst_adapt_operand`, corpus `issue20780b`): `k * 2e6` printed `6e+06` and `x[0] = k*N + 0` trapped `cannot use float64 as int`; Go converts an untyped constant operand to the OTHER operand's type when representable (`2e6` → int). `adaptConst` only adapted through `Named` typedefs; it now also delegates to `scalarConst` — the operand-type conversion comparisons already used — for bare scalar and GoValue operands.
- [x] **float32/complex64 constant conversions kept the wider precision** (`uconst_float32_round`, corpus `bug470`): `float64(float32(0.01))` compared against the float64 nearest of 0.01 instead of the float32 rounding — `convertConst` tagged the constant `float32`/`complex64` but kept the full-precision `UConst` payload, while Go evaluates the conversion in the target width. The tag still keeps the constant domain; the payload now narrows (each complex64 half to float32). `fmt` of a float32/complex64-tagged UConst also formats at the narrower width (`float32(0.01)` spells `0.01`, not the widened digits).
- [x] **`a[i][:]` on a nested array element bound a detached copy** (`indexslice_elemref`, stack-615 review B-1): `s := x[0][:]` sliced the element-READ copy `elemRead` produces, so `x[0][0] = 5` and whole-array `x = …` stores both went invisible to `s` — gc binds the element's storage like `&x[0]`. `SliceExpr` on a refable index base now emits `OpIndexRef` with the new B=2 flag (ref-or-value: `IndexRef` when the base has slice elements, the plain index value otherwise — map elements stay unaddressable copies), and `overwriteArray`'s flat `copy(dst.Elems, src.Elems)` became `overwriteArrayElems`, recursing into array-typed elements so a whole-array store reaches inner backing the refs now bind.
- [x] **an unrepresentable untyped constant silently wrapped through a numeric operand's type** (`uconst_binop_reject`, stack-615 review B-2): `300 - int8var` printed 36 and `v8 == 300` compared false where gc rejects the program — `adaptConst` let a failed `constToBasic` fall through to the default-domain materialization, so the const computed as int64 and `maskInt` wrapped it. A numeric const against a numeric operand now traps via `materializeConstErr`'s conversion-error text (`constant 300 overflows int8`, `cannot use constant (1 + 2i) as int8`); kind-mismatched consts keep the materialize→mismatch-trap path. `fitsIntConst` also learned to reject non-Int/Float kinds (a complex const panicked `Int64Val`), and `constToBasic`'s float32 arm now reports overflow instead of materializing +Inf.
- [x] **typed constant expressions evaluated in the wrapping runtime domain** (`tconst_wrap`, stack-615 review B-3): `uint(4) - 8` printed the wrapped max-uint-ish value, `uint8(250) + 10` printed 4, `-uint(5)` printed the wrapped value — gc rejects each as a constant expression (`constant -4 of type uint overflows uint`) while the same op on a var wraps. `binaryOp` now snapshots `constPayload` for both operands; when both are still constants and the operand tag is numeric it folds via `constBinary` in the exact domain and range-checks the folded `UConst` against the tag (`Shl`/`Shr` key on the left operand's tag, matching gc's left-operand typing). Unfoldable pairs (div-by-zero, kind mismatch) fall through to the runtime path's own trap. `unaryOp`'s unsigned-tag fallback now applies only to `^x` (`^uint(0)` = maxuint), so `-uint(5)` traps as gc's reject.
- [x] **deadlocked goroutines are never detected** (timeout classification family): `bug069`/`bug218`/`gcc61253`/`issue4323`/`issue8475` — a nil-chan op or all-blocked chan set makes gc exit `fatal error: all goroutines are asleep - deadlock!` in under a second, while minigo parks forever. Needs a scheduler-level deadlock check (count runnable goroutines; when zero remain and waits are parked, fail with gc's text) — a feature, not an interpreter-correctness divergence. Implemented: each proc tracks parked/wakeable goroutines; `checkDeadLocked` arms a quiescence watch and fails with gc's text when every goroutine is asleep and nothing wakeable remains (`deadlock_detect` pin; host chans/`time.AfterFunc`-style external waits stay wakeable).
- [x] **`time.Duration` has no method set** (`dur_methods`, corpus `issue56990` continuation — PR #669): `d.String()` on a `var d time.Duration` trapped — the host `time` intrinsic registered `time.Duration` as a bare typedef with no methods. Host-scalar typedefs now keep the raw host value, so the Duration method set (`String`/`Hours`/`Round`/…) resolves through the host facade.
- [x] **untyped const variadic args don't unify to the common default type** (`geninfer_variadic_unify`, corpus `issue58671` — PR #678): `g('a', 2.3)` pinned `P=rune` off the first arg; inference now defers untyped-constant args and joins them at the common default each one still fits into — rune+float → float64. Prints `97` like go.
- [x] **literal args against a constrained type param don't adopt T** (`geninfer_callee_t`, corpus `issue79812` — PR #678): `bar(0, f[T])` bound `T=int` from the literal `0` before `f[T]`'s signature could teach it. The same untyped-const deferral keeps `0` unbound until `f[T]` binds `T`, so `0` converts to `float64`. Same root cause as `geninfer_variadic_unify` — one PR covers both pins.
- [x] **pointer conversions between named pointees rejected the read** (`ptrconv_named`, corpus `issue56990`'s flag uint path): `(*uval)(&u)` converted fine but `*p` trapped `cannot use uint as uval` — the Named-pointer deref coerce applied assignability (two named types reject) where Go re-types the storage the conversion already proved identical. The deref arm now re-tags when the pointee typedef is named and shapes match, and `convertPointer` tags an anonymous pointer whose conversion changes the pointee's static type (`(*uint)(&w)` on `var w uval`) so the result no longer reads as `*uval` at the assign site.
- [x] **`recover()` returned the still-constant panic payload** (`recassert_int`, corpus `issue48898`): `panic(4)` stored the raw `*runtime.UConst`, so `recover().(int)` panicked `interface {} is int, not int (types from different scopes)` — the constant domain's operand survived past panic's `any` parameter where Go materializes `int`. `Recover` now materializes a const payload at its declared (or default) type; `panic('a')` recovers `int32`-rune, `panic(typedConst)` keeps its declared tag.
- [x] **pointer conversions between named pointer types are silently accepted** (`ptrconv_ptrptr` — PR #676): `(**uval2)(&pw)` on `var pw *uval` is a gc compile reject that minigo accepted. `Engine.elemOf` resolved a `*T` element with `resolveTypeRef` (collapses the star — now `elemTypeRef` like the other container arms), and `pointeeTag` gated the cell's stamped type behind `tagIsNamed`, hiding anonymous `*uval` pointee typedefs — it now returns `c.Typ` whenever set. The pin keeps `PENDING`: a gc compile reject surfaces as a `runtime trap`, which `want.stdout` cannot express.
- [x] **`panic(typednil)` doesn't box for recover's interface return** (`panic_typednil` — PR #670): `panic((*int)(nil)); recover(); r != nil` was false — the TypedNil payload returned bare. `panic` now boxes a typed nil into `*runtime.IfaceNil` at the `any` boundary so `recover()` hands back a non-nil interface carrying the dynamic type; `panic(nil)` and an already-interface nil still hit `PanicNilError`. Pin promoted (no PENDING).
- [x] **map key writes accept structurally-identical named structs** (`mapkey_namedstruct` — PR #675): `m[B{1}] = 2` into `map[A]int` was accepted; the map-key operand now coerces to the declared key type in `index`/`indexOK`/`setIndex` (and the map-literal path, which stored `int64(1)` under a `float64` key). `m[B{1}]` traps `cannot use B as A` like gc's reject; the pin keeps `PENDING` for the trap form. The `delete(m, B{1})` residual was fixed in #693 (`mapkey_delete`).
- [x] **a bare `time.Duration` reports an empty method set** (`dur_iface_methodset`, stack-671 review B-1 — PR #685): `x.(fmt.Stringer)` on `2 * time.Second` returned false and `m[d] = 1` into `map[fmt.Stringer]int` trapped — `selectMember` already special-cases the raw scalar, but `methodInfoOfValue` did not, so #675's declared-key check saw an empty set. A raw `time.Duration` now reports `hostMethodSet` like the Named-boxed path.
- [x] **builtin-typed constant args bound the constant's default type** (`geninfer_typedconst`, stack-671 review B-2 — PR #687): `p(int8(3), 'a')` inferred `T=int` where gc binds `int8` — `Named{int8Td, UConst}` deferred as untyped and lost its tag. A builtin-tagged constant is untyped only when the tag equals the constant's own default type; `int8(3)` and `const k T = ...` now bind like values of their type.
- [x] **untyped const join picked the first arg's default, not the widest kind's** (`geninfer_const_join`, stack-671 review B-3 — PR #688): `p(5, 'a')` inferred `int` where gc joins at `rune` (kind ranking `Int < Rune < Float < Complex`, like `constant.BinaryOp`), and `p(1<<40, 'a')` was accepted where gc rejects (overflows rune). `commonConstTypedef` now selects the widest kind's default and validates every contributor with `constFits`; bool and string only join within their own family.
- [x] **delete skipped the declared-key-type coercion** (`mapkey_delete`, round-17 second-review item 3 — PR #693): `delete(m, 1)` on `map[float64]int{1:7}` left the element (stored `float64(1)`, deleted `int64(1)`). The builtin now runs the same `MapKeyOperand` check an index expression does — `delete(m, B{1})` on `map[A]int` traps `cannot use B as A` like gc's compile reject, resolving the documented `mapkey_namedstruct` residual.
- [x] **raw `time.Duration` payload broke every int64-only integer read** (`dur_scalar_reads`, round-17 second-review items 4–8 — PR #694): #669's raw-payload change left `a[d]` trapping, `a[d:]` re-bounding to defaults, `min/max` mis-ordering, `1<<d` and `^d` trapping. All reads now go through `runtime.SmallIntOf` (index, bounds, literal index, shift count, unary `^`, orderedLess); results keep the operand's tag (`-3ns`, `1ns 2ns` like gc).
- [x] **array slice-bounds panic said "capacity" instead of "length"** (`boundsmsg_len`, corpus `issue30116`): `a[lo:hi]` on an array-typed value printed `[:4] with capacity 3` where gc reports `with length 3` — the `*runtime.Slice` arm fell through to the native `Elems[l:h]` panic which always says capacity. `sliceBoundsReason` now takes the suffix word ("length"/"capacity") and the array-typed arms (real and virtual) fabricate the message before slicing.
- [x] **anonymous literals/conversions into named slots lost the declared tag** (`namedlit_iface`, corpus `bug277`): `var s S = struct{x int}{0}` bound the literal's anonymous typedef so `s.m` and `var i I = s` trapped; `m = map[string]int(m)` trapped `cannot use map[string]int as M`. `coerceConcrete` now Named-wraps a `*runtime.Struct` bound into a named struct slot (fields stay on the struct's own Def), `tdShapeEq` spells a named type from its `Spec.Type` so named↔anonymous shapes compare, and the Named arm re-tags when the underlying shapes match and the wrap's own type is unnamed.
- [x] **int→string of an out-of-range value printed a wrong rune** (`runerange_fffd`, fixedbugs `issue15039`): `string(uint64(0x10001f4a9))` emitted `\U0001F4A9` and `string(1<<100)` emitted `\0` — both paths truncated to `rune` before `string()` could see the out-of-range value. Both the const and runtime int→string paths now range-check against `utf8.MaxRune` first and yield U+FFFD.
- [x] **slicing a const string trapped** (`slice_uconst`, corpus `bug237`): `const s = "…"; s[0:i]` trapped `slice on *runtime.UConst` — OpSlice never materialized its base. `v.slice` now materializes like `v.index` does.
- [x] **blank labels redeclared** (`labelblank`, fixedbugs `issue52278`/`issue70175`): two `_:` labels in one function trapped `label _ redeclared`. Blank labels are not declarations — they never redeclare and cannot be goto/break/continue targets — so `labeledStmt` now compiles the statement without registering the name.
- [x] **corpus families queued**: record-only boundary class: bug260 (`%p` address arithmetic — verified: prints `FAIL`, address deltas diverge), cgo files (issue34968/36705/47227/71225/71226 — verified: all trap `cgo is not supported: import "C"` by design), GC/finalizer and unsafe families. Cleared by stack #579 (verified `go run` parity): bug254/266/434, issue23545/31546/50190/21879/22662/29504/9604b/26094/6866/67190/5856/29264/46591/35576/59411/66575, list2. Cleared later: issue54467 (array stores rebind instead of overwriting the shared backing — `overwriteArray`), issue4562's Caller-position half (stack-579 traceback work already reports line 22 — verified `go run` parity), bug301 + issue4562's remaining half (the `[recovered]` chain — `panic: <orig> [recovered]` / `\tpanic: <new>` — plus the deferred panic no longer swallowing the owner's remaining defers; `defer_drain_repanic` + `panic_recovered_chain`).
- [x] **local interface var's nil method value reports the wrong panic** (stack-579 review B6, pre-existing): `var i I = (*T)(nil); i.M()` or `defer i.M()` inside a function traps `"value method main.T.M called using nil *T pointer"` — gc reports a nil pointer dereference instead. The package-level `var` case legitimately panics with the value-method message and is pinned (`valuemethod_nilptr`), so the split is at the local-var path; likely the local call skips the receiver nil-check that produces gc's dereference.
- [x] **untyped constant overflow into a sized type is not rejected** (stack-579 review B9, pre-existing): `x := int8(300)` and `var y int8 = 300` silently truncate to 44; gc fails at compile time (`constant 300 overflows int8`). The constants stay concrete int64 on this path rather than `UConst` — needs a range check when a compile-time-known value is stored under a sized declared/inferred type (`assignCell` sees `constPayload`, not bare scalars).
- [x] **`inspect.Value` on a const runs the package's `init()`** (grafana `pkg/services/pluginsintegration/coreplugin`): reading `const CloudWatch = "cloudwatch"` initialized the package, chaining into `plugins/log` → GOROOT `log/slog` → `unsafe.StringData` trap. A constant needs no init; and `inspect` exposes no syntax-level view of a const/var initializer, so scripts fall back to `go/parser`.
- [x] **`reflect.StructTag.Get` is missing** on the bound `reflect` (`reflect.StructTag(f.Tag).Get("json")` traps `has no field or method Get`) — the first call any tag-driven sync script makes on `inspect.Field.Tag`.
- [x] **realworld has no task that reads a large target package through `inspect`**: the pitch's core comparison is unmeasured. Typed loading of grafana `pkg/api` costs `go vet` 97 s cold / ~2 s warm and 2.8 GB peak RSS, and every current minigo task is either stdlib-only or reads a small clickhouse package. Add one, e.g. grafana's swagger model structs ↔ the bundled OpenAPI spec, measured healthy and with modules missing.
- [x] **`Engine.Call`: unbox host container args** (bug found by the minigo-generate spike, #390): a `[]string` passed as a Call arg arrives as the raw Go slice and the callee's param coerce traps `cannot use []string as []string` — the shape check wants a `*runtime.Slice`. Scalars cross fine; only containers don't. Either `Call` should run args through `goValueOf` (what reflect-call results already get) or the boundary needs a documented conversion. Workaround in place: callers box args as `&runtime.Slice{Elems:}` (examples/minigo-generate).
- [x] **Flaky `hostcycle_struct` regression uses `sync.Pool` as guaranteed storage**: CI for #544 failed at `p.Get().(*Node)` with a nil value after `Put`. `sync.Pool` may discard entries or return nil, so replace it with a deterministic host round-trip that still exercises self-referencing and two-node cycles.
- [x] **`inspect.Value` on a const returns a `*string` cell pointer, not the value**: printing it yields `0x...` instead of the string — an internal cell leaks through the bound boundary (found by the realworld gap-verification pass; sibling of the const-`init()` entry above).
- [x] **alloc_space grew ~100 MB on the frame path across the difffuzz fix stack** (realworld `compare.sh --profile` on the micro probe, `main` → `pr558`): `prepFrame` +72 MB flat, `loop` +36 MB vs main. Wall time on the real grafana-openapi workload moved only +0.9%, and no single stack commit accounts for the micro wall delta (bisect landed on `d4fcecd`, a pure simplification; direct pairwise compare showed −2.1% — i.e. binary-layout noise). `massign_pinbase` pinning pointer bases of implicit indirections is the most plausible alloc contributor — investigate when frame retention is next touched. Actual cause: #551's two-phase binary operands hoisted every call operand into a `$bin` scratch local even when no reorder was needed (`fib(n-1) + fib(n-2)` paid two extra cells per slot per frame); `hoistEagerOps` now skips in-order lists. massign_pinbase allocates nothing.
- [x] **bound `utf8.EncodeRune` is script-shaped** (found during the oapi-codegen run): it returns the encoded string instead of writing into the caller's `[]byte` and returning the length, so source that calls the real signature gets the wrong type.
- [x] **`embedRO` is lost through `Elem`/`Index`** (oapi-codegen run, follow-up of the `reflect_embedro` fix): minireflect's `Field()` computes the embed read-only flag, but values reached through `Elem()`/`Index()` drop it, so `CanSet`/`CanInterface` can over-report on paths below an unexported embedded field.
- [x] **constraint error renders `*ast.UnaryExpr`** (oapi-codegen run): a failed `~T` check reports `does not satisfy constraint *ast.UnaryExpr`; `typeExprName` should spell the tilde term (`~[]E`).
- [x] **`panic(err)` prints the struct, not `err.Error()`** (oapi-codegen run): an uncaught panic with an error payload prints `panic: &{...}`; gc prints the `Error()` text (and `[recovered]` forms) for error values.
- [x] **`panic(err)` inside a goroutine still prints `(type) 0xADDR`, not `Error()`** (pre-existing divergence, noted in the #657 review): a script error payload panicking on another goroutine renders as the composite `(main.MyErr) 0x…` — `Panic.VC` is set but the probe's method call runs on the already-exited panicking proc and falls back — where gc prints the `Error()` text. Host error payloads (`errors.New`) do print correctly.
- [x] **nil-receiver `Error()` that derefs doesn't fatal like gc** (intentional deviation documented in #657, verified still present): `var e *E; panic(e)` with `func (e *E) Error() string { return e.m }` — gc dies `fatal error: panic while printing panic value: type runtime.errorString` (+SIGSEGV) because a panic inside printpanics is itself fatal, while `IfaceCallString` swallows the probe panic and falls back to `panic: (*main.E) 0x0`. Matching gc means letting a probe panic propagate as fatal — only when the method itself panics (a nil-receiver `Error()` that does not deref must still print its text).
- [x] **compile-side import lookups key on the path basename** (oapi-codegen overlay example): `isImportName` and the selector scope in compile.go read `Scopes[file][name]` only, so an unaliased import whose package clause differs from the path tail (gopkg.in/yaml.v3 → `yaml`) is not seen there — e.g. `yaml.V = x` would compile as a storage ref. The VM, the type-ref resolver and the type spellings already fall back to materializing the imports by clause name.
- [x] **host `encoding/json` traps on alias-typed struct fields** (oapi-codegen run, `reflect_alias_field_unmarshaler` without SRC): decoding into `AP BoolSchema-alias` with the bound json fails with `select Has on bool` instead of an unmarshal error.
- [x] **bound `json.Unmarshal` residual decode gaps** (follow-up of the alias-field trap fix, #673): the by-shape decoder now reports `UnmarshalTypeError` on kind mismatches but still misses gc behaviors — non-string map keys are not converted (`{"1":true}` into `map[int]bool` keeps the `string` key), the `,string` tag option is ignored (gc re-decodes the quoted literal as a primitive), `[N]T` arrays shape as slices (no overflow checks), and embedded-field json flattening is unverified. Calling a script `UnmarshalJSON`/`MarshalJSON` stays out — that is tracked under the oapi-codegen `--src` entry.
- [x] **bound `encoding/json` helpers re-implement existing resolvers** (noted in the #690 review): `jsonResolveTypName`/`jsonPeelPtr`, `jsonTypFromSpec`, `jsonFieldIsIface`, and `jsonReadPath`/`jsonEmbedStruct` overlap `Engine.resolveTypeRef`/`typeDefOf`/`fieldTypes` and `minireflect.fieldByIndexWalk`/`FieldRef.structOf`. Those live behind `*Engine`, not `runtime.VMCaller`, so sharing them needs a caller-visible hook — keep the JSON-specific field selection rules (tag names, dominance, fold) local and share only type resolution + structural walks.
- [x] **`fmt` inserts an operand space after a `json.Number`** (found while verifying the json work, pre-existing): `fmt.Print(x, err)` for a `json.Number` operand renders `1e1000 <nil>` while gc prints `1e1000<nil>` — gc's operand spacing treats a string-kind value as a string even when wrapped in a named type, the fmt path likely sees the `Named`/`int64` shape instead of the declared string kind.
- [x] **operand assignability on `==`/`!=`, `switch case`, and bare-numeric sides still evaluates where gc rejects** (issue #724 residual): the runtime gate now traps named↔named and cross-domain named↔bare mismatches on non-equality ops, but `f.Tag == s`, `d == i`, `switch tag { case s: }`, and `d < len(x)` still evaluate — an `any`-carried value and an under-typed generic argument are indistinguishable from a statically-typed operand at run time. Covering these needs compile-time operand typing (the issue's option 2: untyped-const-aware static inference). Escape hatch documented: explicit conversion or a typed variable.
- [x] **a stopped timer's channel still counts as self-wakeable** (round-18 review structural note): `t := time.NewTimer(d); t.Stop(); <-t.C` exits `fatal error: ... deadlock!` in gc but hangs in minigo — a host channel's wakeable flag is decided per park, statically, so the parked receiver is never counted asleep after the timer dies (unlike `time.AfterFunc`'s callback note, which `Stop`/`Reset` manage explicitly). Expressing it needs wakeable-by-liveness bookkeeping (the note registry consulted from `chanOf`), a design change rather than a pin: the harness's `Run` has no timeout, so a HANG cannot be pinned. Same shape: `<-ctx.Done()` parks are wakeable even when the only cancel source is dead.
- [x] **`<-ctx.Done()` parks as wakeable even when the cancel source can never fire** (same shape as the timer case fixed in [#760](https://github.com/podhmo/minigo/pull/760)): `ctx, _ := context.WithCancel(context.Background()); <-ctx.Done()` is `fatal error: all goroutines are asleep - deadlock!` in gc but hangs in minigo — the done channel is not registered in the managed-channel liveness registry, so the parked receive counts pessimistically wakeable. Extending the registry to the context package's done channel (the way the timer/ticker boxes register `t.C`) would cover it.
- [x] **`Named{td, UConst}` materializes through the declared type only inside `display`** (found by the stack-#761 whole-diff review, pre-existing divergence): `display` routes a tagged typed constant through the new `MaterializeConstErr`, but every other host boundary still runs the default UConst materialization — `fmt.Println(uint64(18446744073709551615))` prints `%!v(PANIC=Format method: panic: runtime error: constant ... overflows int)` where gc prints the value, and `minigo.Format` (cmd/minigo) prints the overflow error string. Fix direction: move declared-type materialization down into `goNative`/`namedSized` (or a shared `materializeThroughTag`) so all host boundaries agree. Related: `display`'s unsigned-name list should sit next to `runtime.CanonicalBasicName` as a shared `IsUnsignedName` when this is fixed.
- [x] **`%T`/`reflect.TypeOf` on timers and tickers prints the proxy box type** (introduced by the [#760](https://github.com/podhmo/minigo/pull/760) liveness boxes): `fmt.Sprintf("%T", time.NewTimer(d))` prints `*minigo.timerChanBox` (and `*minigo.tickerChanBox`) instead of `*time.Timer`/`*time.Ticker` — the boxes exist so `t.C` can be a managed channel, but `%T` and `reflect` now expose them. No cheap fix (the box would need a typedef identity, or `%T`/`TypeOf` would need a feed-box special case), so pinned nowhere — known divergence.
- [x] **a package-qualified conversion reports no static type** (round-19 review follow-up): `conversionCall` recognized only `Ident`/`T[Args]` callees, so `time.Duration(5) == s` evaluated to `false` where gc rejects the mismatched operands. `selTypRef`+`selIsType` now resolve `pkg.T` members to type declarations; `pkg.fn(x)` stays a real call (fixed in [#768](https://github.com/podhmo/minigo/pull/768)).
- [x] **a resolvable qualified type stays silent on `x == nil`** (round-19 conservative-gap note): `var d time.Duration; d == nil` evaluates to `false` where gc rejects `mismatched types time.Duration and untyped nil` — `nilableTypExpr` treats every `pkg.T` as maybe-nilable because a qualified name's kind is opaque. → fixed: `selNilable` resolves the member's decl spec or typedef Kind via the same `selTypRef` walk (re-proposed from the closed-unmerged [#771](https://github.com/podhmo/minigo/pull/771)). Interface types keep the dynamic compare.
- [x] **operand-assignability gate: coverage gaps keep gc compile errors silent** (stack-#761 whole-diff review, B3): package-level inferred vars are covered, but these stays silent — `x := 1 + 2` / `y := c` (untyped const expressions and untyped-const idents: `staticOpTyp` yields untyped but `noteDeclTyp`'s default only fires on a bare `*ast.BasicLit`), `for i := range` loop vars (no `declTyp` recorded), `p := &d` (`token.AND` unhandled in `staticOpTyp`), `s[i]` string indexing, `*p`/`pa[i]` ptr-to-array dereference, `v, ok := f()` multi-result binds, select-case binds, and qualified consts (`time.Sunday`). Each is a difffuzz-able SILENT divergence.
- [x] operand-assignability gate: coverage gaps keep gc compile errors silent > `x := 1 + 2` / `y := c` → fixed: untyped operands and untyped-const bindings carry a `dte` (the default type the operand binds at `:=` — literal kind, `bool` for compare/logical results, rank-merged across binary arithmetic) so `noteDeclTyp` records it as the name's static type; untyped-typed mixes in one expression yield the typed side
- [x] operand-assignability gate: coverage gaps keep gc compile errors silent > operand shapes `&x` / `s[i]` / `pa[i]` → fixed: `staticOpTyp` covers `token.AND` (a `*ast.StarExpr` over the operand's static type), `elemTypExpr` yields `byte` for a string index and unwraps one pointer-to-array level, and `namedIdentOf` wraps a named base in `ptrTypID` so `i == &d` reports `int and *D` (`*p`, `a[i]`, `m[k]`, `(d)` already resolved through explicit decls)
- [x] operand-assignability gate: coverage gaps keep gc compile errors silent > bind sites record a static type → fixed: `for k, v := range` notes int-index/string-rune/map-kv/chan-elem/`range n`'s own type via `rangeVarTyps`; `a, b := f()` and `var a, b = f()` (function- and package-level) type each name by the callee's i-th result via `nthResultOpTyp`; `case v := <-ch` / `v, ok :=` note the element/bool; comma-ok second names bind bool at `var` sites too
- [x] operand-assignability gate: coverage gaps keep gc compile errors silent > qualified consts → fixed: `selPkgMemberOpTyp` reads a bound package member's runtime tag — a `Named` const contributes its typedef as `pkg.T` (`time.January` is time.Month), a `UConst` stays untyped with its kind's default; source-indexed foreign consts stay opaque (their values would resolve in the caller's scope)
- [x] **switch-case trap message does not match gc's wording** (stack-#761 review, B7 cosmetic): the gate emits `invalid operation: mismatched types %s and %s` for a switch-case reject where gc says `invalid case %s in switch on %s (mismatched types ...)`. `want.err` substring matching hides it today. → fixed: `opCmpGate` shares its decision with `opCaseGate`; case clauses render both operand spellings through `exprSpell` (go/printer over the package fileset) inside gc's `invalid case %s in switch on %s` wording, nil-operand cases included
- [x] **reflect method invocation on proxy boxes panics** (stack-#761 review, B8 latent edge): `Hooks.TypeAlias` spells `*timerChanBox` as `*time.Timer`, so `reflect.TypeOf(t).Method(i).Func` is a `*time.Timer` method — calling it with the box value panics on reflect assignability unless the call path converts. Unexercised by pins. → fixed: `toHost` consults a new `Hooks.UnwrapHost` when assignability fails; `proxyUnwrapHost` hands the call site the wrapped real timer/ticker (feed bookkeeping stays script-side)
- [x] **realworld `grafana-openapi` bypasses `inspect` for a reason that is only half real**: 117 of the 121 `// swagger:route` annotations in grafana `pkg/api` are doc comments on handler methods, which `inspect.Doc` already exposes; only 4 are free comments it cannot see. The route registrations, however, live inside `registerRoutes`' function body (see "Script-side traversal of function bodies" below). Rewrite the task to read the doc half through `inspect` (exercising lazy surface loading on a large package) and record the free-comment gap, or drop it if those 4 don't matter. → fixed: the task now reads everything through `inspect` — the body view covers the route registrations, `Doc` the 117 method docs, `Comments` (Free) the 4 detached comments; identical output to the `go/parser` oracle at ~0.08s vs ~3s.
- [x] **`path/filepath.Split` is missing from the bound package** (found alongside the above): `go/scanner.Init` traps `undefined: filepath.Split` — an instance of the bound-package no-fall-through gap under "Intrinsic coverage".
- [x] **Script-side traversal of function bodies** (future work, surfaced by the convert-define experiment): nothing in `minigo.dev/inspect` exposes a function decl's body — index/syntax stop at the signature, so script-side tools that reason about statements/exprs inside a `func` have no entry point (today only host special forms walk bodies via `*ast.FuncLit`, e.g. convert-define's `mappingWalker`). An inspect body view (statement/expr children, decl-anchored `TypeExpr`s) would be new machinery, not an accessor — deliberately left out of the experiment's gap list. → fixed: `inspect.Body` roots a `Node` view (Kind/Text/Detail/Role/Pos) over a func decl's body; `Nodes` enumerates children in `ast.Inspect`-identical preorder with the parent field name as Role; `Expr` anchors expression nodes as `TypeExpr`s so `SymbolID`/`Resolve`/`Children` apply; `AST` also accepts a `Node`.
- [x] **task-run error-UX G6: redeclared task func silently last-wins** (broken-input experiment, docs/sketch/ja/experiment-task-run-errors.md §5): `func Default()` twice ran the latter with no error where `go build` rejects `Default redeclared in this block`. → fixed: index.Build rejects duplicate package-block names (funcs/vars/consts/types + per-receiver methods) with a gc-style `X redeclared in this block` load error; inspect DirOf/SourceOf and the REPL index leniently (they must see and allow duplicates). testdata/difffuzz/redeclared pins it.
- [x] **task-run error-UX G5: `-f` loads only one file** (same experiment): helpers in sibling same-package files are invisible — `task-run -f dir/Taskfile.go` traps `undefined: Helper` where `go run ./dir` shares the package scope. Load the directory's package or hint on `undefined`. → fixed: task-run `-f` now loads the file's package scope via Engine.LoadDirScope (same-package sibling .go files, build constraints ignored — Taskfiles carry `//go:build task`); TestRunTaskSiblingHelpers pins it
- [x] **gen-sync: error-vocabulary experiment** (round-3, [docs/sketch/ja/experiment-gen-sync-errors.md](./docs/sketch/ja/experiment-gen-sync-errors.md)) — the failure-vocabulary stack (PR #447 and its successor) landed the script-side half: no-buildable errors name the package and reason, foreign-package files warn and skip (`inspect.File.PkgName`), `-check` exits 2 on a real failure vs 1 on drift, and a failure's first line names who must fix it. Remaining residuals:
- [x] gen-sync: error-vocabulary experiment > **resolver module priority** still reads the shadowed tree's decls (a locally broken package is silently read from the other tree — writes are refused by the outside-scanned-dir guard, but the resolution itself is unreported); index-level `ctx.MatchFile` drops files with no error channel at resolve level (the script surfaces them via its own dir listing — erroring on unreadable, warning on excluded — but the resolver itself stays silent).
- [x] gen-sync: error-vocabulary experiment > **`p` + `p_test` merging** can still emit directives `go generate` cannot resolve — non-test foreign clauses now warn and skip, but the `_test` package merge is untouched.
- [x] gen-sync: error-vocabulary experiment > **resolver/module fallback for no-go.mod dirs** (case x01): inside a worktree a dir with no go.mod resolves imports through the PARENT minigo module and syncs fine, while the same dir shipped standalone traps at script load — behavior depends on the environment, not the input. Decision: keep the nearest-go.mod behavior — it is `go build` parity (worktree builds, standalone fails), and a dir without any module context has no resolvable imports, so strict anchoring at the invoked dir would only break the main use case.
- [x] gen-sync: error-vocabulary experiment > **partial-write semantics** (case 11a): one failed write still leaves the other files synced — now announced with a `N file(s) failed to write` summary. Decision: keep write-then-fail — plans are fully resolved before the write loop starts and in-memory restore would be a post-hoc repair, not atomicity; `-check` remains the CI guard. (Single-file writes still went atomic: each sync writes a same-dir `.gen-sync.tmp` and renames it over the target, so a mid-write failure can no longer truncate the file — and a corrupted file is refused at the next scan's parse anyway.)
- [x] gen-sync: error-vocabulary experiment > **canonical package picked by first sorted file, not majority** (review of the failure-vocabulary stack): a dir mixing `package app` and `package otherpkg` files takes `inspect.Name(p)` from the alphabetically-first file, which can be the minority clause — the "foreign" set then covers most of the package. Majority-vote (or an error on any mix) would be more honest; left as-is because a mixed dir is already a `go build` failure.
- [x] **`inspect.Decls` covers top-level decls only, so doc scans silently miss methods**: all 117 reachable `swagger:route` docs in grafana `pkg/api` live on methods — a script walking `inspect.Decls(pkg)` collects 0 of them with no warning (methods are only reachable through `type` + `inspect.Methods`). The `grafana-swagger-spec` prototype's first version returned routes=0 exactly this way. Include methods in `Decls`, or make the blind spot loud/documented.
- [x] **No comment-enumeration surface in `inspect`**: `inspect.Doc` reaches decl-adjacent doc comments only; free comments are invisible — 4 of grafana `pkg/api`'s 121 `swagger:route` annotations (quota.go:94, dataproxy.go:57, dataproxy.go:71, org_users.go:144). A `File.Comments`/`inspect.CommentsOf` API would close the gap the `grafana-swagger-spec` task documents.
- [x] **decl-anchored raw AST handle in `inspect`** (go-scan reuse): return the retained `*ast.FuncDecl`/`Gen`/`Spec` plainly (`inspect.ASTOf`-shaped), no wrapper view — scripts reach `.Body`/`.Pos()` via the host reflect facade. Revisit a `{Kind,Pos,Text,Children}` view only if real use is noisy or slow (one-off measurement on a large realworld target; no benchmark machinery in the repo). Prerequisite for body traversal, free comments, and const initializer views.
- [x] **`inspect.Methods(pkg)`: package-wide flat method enumeration** (verdict: wanted): per-type `inspect.MethodsOf(typeDecl)` already exists and is bound; the missing piece is only a thin pkg-level helper — the `Decls` → TypeDecl → `MethodsOf` composition works but is undiscoverable. Complements the `inspect.Decls` methods-blind-spot entry above.
- [x] **`gentest` test harness + `FileWriter`** (name decided on #613; go-scan `scantest`): temp-module → run → assert-on-outputs loop, for behavior tests of generator examples only (gen-sync, convert-define; fuzz out of scope). First application example undecided — gen-sync is the natural candidate.
- [x] **compound and nested stores through a package qualifier miss** (found while fixing the clause-name import lookup): `pkg.V = x` works, but `pkg.V += 1`/`pkg.V++` trap `deref of non-pointer *runtime.FieldRef`, `pkg.F.G = v` traps `set field G on *runtime.FieldRef`, and `pkg.V, x = 1, 2` silently drops the package-global store — gc writes through all of them (the FieldRef on a package base doesn't resolve the package var's cell). Independent of the clause-name fix: the same fails on a package whose clause matches the path tail. → fixed: `refThrough` now treats a `*runtime.Package`/`*runtime.ImportRef` operand as a shared base, so a ref chain crossing a package member (`pkg.F.G`, `pkg.A[i]`) resolves through `selectMember`'s live-cell read; `pkg.V += 1`/`pkg.V++`/`pkg.V, x = 1, 2` already stored correctly since the #485 selector lowering
- [x] **bound json ignores `encoding.TextMarshaler`/`TextUnmarshaler`** (residual gap of the script UnmarshalJSON/MarshalJSON dispatch, #715): gc marshals map keys via `MarshalText` and unmarshals them via `UnmarshalText` (and honors both for quoted string values); the bound codec never probes the Text interfaces — `json.Marshal(map[Key]int{"a":1})` prints `{"a":1}` where gc prints `{"K:a":1}`. → fixed: the codec now probes `MarshalText`/`UnmarshalText` after the `MarshalJSON`/`UnmarshalJSON` probes (map keys get the Text probes only — gc ignores `MarshalJSON` on keys), `,string` reads the inner text verbatim into `UnmarshalText`, and `MarshalText` errors ride the `encoding.MarshalerError` channel naming the operand type like gc
- [x] **`Unmarshal` into `**T` does not allocate the pointer chain** (residual gap of the #715 dispatch): gc's `indirect()` walks and allocates each level, so `var p **U; json.Unmarshal([]byte("q"), &p)` lands `p == &U{"q!"}`. The bound decoder hands the script method a nil `*U`, which panics `invalid memory address or nil pointer dereference` inside the method. → fixed: `jsonAllocPointee` recurses through pointer elements — a `**T` target allocates a `*T` cell pointing at a fresh `T` like gc's indirect()
- [x] **bound `encoding/json` does not export the `Unmarshaler`/`Marshaler` interface types** (residual gap of #715): `var i json.Unmarshaler = new(U)` traps `undefined: json.Unmarshaler`, so gc's path of dispatching through an interface value that holds an Unmarshaler (`json.Unmarshal(data, &i)`) is unreachable — with the type bound, an interface-holding target should also route into `UnmarshalJSON` (gc prints the method's result `"w"!`). → fixed: both interface typedefs are bound (`MReqs` lists each method); the interface-holding target reaches the existing `UnmarshalJSON` member probe and decodes like gc
- [x] **an anonymous embedded field's `UnmarshalJSON`/`MarshalJSON` is not called** (residual gap of #715): gc flattens anonymous embeds and hands the WHOLE parent literal to the embedded method — `type HasEmbed struct{ Money }` with `{"Money":"10"}` calls `Money.UnmarshalJSON({"Money":"10"})`. The bound decoder walks the embed's fields by shape and never probes the promoted method (verified: field stays empty). → fixed: `promotedMember`'s pointer-receiver method bind now uses a `*runtime.FieldRef` over the containing struct's field slot, so `*p = x` inside a promoted method writes the stored field instead of a copy — the decoder's UnmarshalJSON probe already saw the promoted method (methodWalkU promotes it); only the write was lost. Same fix makes direct `h.M()` calls through embeds write through like Go.
- [x] **a nested `UnmarshalJSON` error always preempts the input-order first error** (residual gap of #715): gc reports the FIRST error in input order — for `{"a":"notint","b":"x"}` into `struct{ A int; B Fail }` it reports the `a` type error even though `b`'s method returns `custom fail`. The bound decoder's `ectx.callErr` wins unconditionally, so it reports `custom fail`. → fixed: `callErr` competes in input order at `callErrPath`'s member position (`jsonErrOrder`/`jsonErrPos` with the codec's case-folded match); field errors now path on the input's member spelling (`.a`, not the Go name `A`) like gc's UnmarshalTypeError
- [x] **`fmt` does not emit gc's `%!v(PANIC=Error method: ...)` marker** (residual gap of the #714 fmt side): when a value's `Error()`/`String()` panics, gc prints `%!v(PANIC=Error method: <panic text>)`; the bound fmt swallows the panic silently and prints the plain value dump (`{}` for an empty struct). → fixed: the Stringer/Format probes surface the script panic and `fmtPanicMarker` spells it `%!v(PANIC=<method> method: <payload %v>)`; a panic from a nil-pointer arg renders `<nil>` per gc's catchPanic.
- [x] **`Member`/`MethodSetOf` may over-match on unnamed container types** (mechanism observed during #715, unverified as a divergence): on `[]Upper`/`map[string]Upper` the member walk borrows the ELEMENT type's `UnmarshalJSON`; the jsonShape call site is gated by `td.Name != ""`, but the same `IfaceMember`/`MethodSetOf` walks run in the fmt probes and other dispatch paths — audit whether the vm-level member walk itself needs the named-type guard. → audited: no divergence found. `MethodSetOf`/`typeMethodInfoU` on a `[]Upper`/`map[string]Upper` typedef yields an empty set (synthetic container typedefs carry no `Methods`; the walk reads `td.Methods` + `EmbedSpecs` only, never the element type). Verified on every reachable surface: `reflect` NumMethod/Implements/TypeAssert all report 0/false like gc, `s.MarshalJSON` traps `select ... on slice` rather than binding the element method, iface conversion fails, and fmt formats `[S:a S:b]` per element like gc. A declared `type MySlice []U` keeps its own methods — matching gc on all sides, so no vm-level named-type guard is needed.
- [x] **`panic while printing panic value` folds inner panic names to `runtime.errorString`** (documented approximation in #714, error text only): gc names the inner panic's concrete type (`boundsError`, …); the marker maps every `*RuntimeError` to `runtime.errorString`. → fixed: `RuntimeError.Kind` carries the gc type name (`boundsError` for index/slice bounds via `BoundsPanic`/`SliceBoundsPanic`); `panicPrintFatal.describe` prints a bare-string inner panic raw instead of `type string`.
- [x] **difffuzz cannot pin unrecovered/fatal panics** (harness gap noted during #714): a case whose expectation is `runErr != nil` fails the run's exit-status check, so fatal-path divergences (`panic while printing panic value`, uncaught panics) can only be covered by `minigo_test`-style host tests, not `testdata/difffuzz` pins. Extending the harness (e.g. a `want.err` oracle) would let them pin.
- [x] **`--src strings` traps in `strings.Index`** (found in [docs/sketch/experiment-oapi-codegen-perf.md](./docs/sketch/experiment-oapi-codegen-perf.md)): `minigo run --src strings` on `strings.Index("chicken", "ken")` traps `cannot use int64 as int` at the `stringslite.Index(s, substr)` return. `--src sort` fails differently: `sort.Slice` reaches `internal/reflectlite` (`unsafe.Pointer` undefined). → `--src strings`/`--src bytes` fixed: bound helpers declared `int` were returning `int64(...)`-tagged results; all `return int64(` sites for `int` APIs now return `int` (+`bytealg.MaxBruteForce` bound). The `--src sort`/unsafe.Pointer remainder stays — tracked in the `unsafe.Pointer` entry below.
- [x] **`bytealg.MaxLen` was a hardcoded 64 regardless of arch**: upstream `internal/bytealg index_<arch>.go` sets it per arch (amd64 63/31, arm64/ppc64x 32, s390x/loong64 64). Now `bytealgMaxLen()` follows GOARCH (amd64 takes the conservative 31 without CPUID — the bound only gates IndexString eligibility, so smaller is always safe). (found by fresh stack review)
- [x] **host `any` parameters run a struct's niladic methods** ([docs/sketch/experiment-oapi-codegen-perf.md](./docs/sketch/experiment-oapi-codegen-perf.md), step 5): `structDataHost` invokes every niladic exported method when a script struct crosses any host `any` parameter. That serves text/template's `evalField`, but it is SILENT for side-effecting methods. `sync.Pool.Put`/`New` are now opaque; `sync.Map.Store`/`LoadOrStore`/`Swap`, `atomic.Value.Store`/`Swap` and `context.WithValue` still marshal. A general "containers keep script values opaque" rule would beat a per-method list. → fixed: `opaqueContainers` names the host types whose `any` method args are pure storage (`*sync.Pool`, `*sync.Map`, `*context.valueCtx`, `*minigo.atomicBox`); their calls route to `callOpaqueStore`, which boxes script args verbatim (UConst materialized, GoValue unwrapped) instead of marshaling — gc's interface box — so niladic methods never fire on Store and a struct key even works. Pin: `testdata/difffuzz/syncmap_store_opaque`.
- [x] **source text/template: field access on a parenthesized pipeline panics** ([docs/sketch/experiment-oapi-codegen-perf.md](./docs/sketch/experiment-oapi-codegen-perf.md)): `{{(index .Items 1).Name}}` under `--src text/template` panics with `reflect: call of reflect.Value.Field on struct Value` in exec's `evalField`. Native prints the field. The chain node's receiver reaches `evalField` as a bad minireflect value. → fixed: `minireflect.wrap` re-wrapped a `GoValue{*RValue}` (the script repr of `reflect.Value`) into `RValue{val: GoValue{*RValue}}` — Kind() read the facade's struct shape while get() still yielded the box. `wrap` now adopts the inner view.
- [x] **html/template from source cannot run** ([docs/sketch/experiment-oapi-codegen-perf.md](./docs/sketch/experiment-oapi-codegen-perf.md)): with `--src html/template,text/template,encoding/json,encoding/hex,encoding/base64,encoding/base32,slices,maps` it stops at `bytes.IndexAny` (unbound); adding `--src bytes` stops at `bytealg.IndexByte` ("cannot use int64 as int", like `--src strings`). → the named walls are clear: with `--src bytes` the source `bytes.IndexAny` runs through bound bytealg (now `int`-returning), and `{{.}}` in an href executes `<a href="a&amp;b">x</a>` like gc. → viability verified: `make tmpltests-html` runs the upstream suite at 103/105 PASS (the 2 leftovers are upstream-broken SKIPs, `TestIssue31810`/`TestTemplateLookUp`) — stack #776 (escaper, clone, typed-content, zip-fs paths all exercised).
- [x] **`&` on a package-level interface variable yields `*dynamicType`** (found by tools/tmpltests): `var siVal = I(S{"a","b"}); &T{F *I: &siVal}` traps `cannot use &S as *I` at init where gc builds the `*I`. `&` on a local interface var or param works (`func ptrI(i I) *I { return &i }`), so the global cell's `&` resolves the contained concrete value instead of the interface slot. → fixed: inferred `var x = e` decls (package and function-local) now stamp the cell's declared type like `x := e` does (the `stampInferredTyps`/`stampVarTyps` OpCoerce family); `inferredBindTyp` resolves an iface-typed ident RHS to its declared type so `var j = i` keeps I; the `&siVal → ptrI(siVal)` rewrite is dropped from tools/tmpltests. Pin: `testdata/difffuzz/addr_ifacevar`.
- [x] **`%T` on `&` of an interface-typed variable prints the concrete pointee** (found fixing the `&siVal` divergence): `var i I = S{...}; fmt.Printf("%T", &i)` prints `*main.S` where gc prints `*main.I` — the formatter resolves `&`'s pointee through the stored value and ignores the cell's stamped interface type. Also `*any` and `*interface{}` are distinct typedefs under the assignability check, so `T{A *interface{}: &a}` on `var a = any(v)` still traps where gc accepts. → fixed: `%T` resolves the pointee's declared type through the pointee cell's stamped `Typ` (`addr_of_iface_cell`), and the predeclared `any` typedef is identical to the empty `interface{}` so `*any` assigns to `*interface{}` (`any_iface_ptr`).
- [x] **bound `fmt.Sscan` missing** (tools/tmpltests): `--src text/template` died in `parse/node.go` `newNumber` on any complex-literal template — blocked upstream `TestExecute`/`TestComparison` wholesale. → fixed: `fmt` Scan/Sscan/Fscan family bound via `scanFn` (script refs mirrored into host vars seeded with the current pointee, results written back through `SetRef`) (#747).
- [x] **`--src text/template` rejects oversized hex literals** (tools/tmpltests, TestExecute "ideal too big"): `{{0x10000000000000000000}}`-scale literals report `illegal number syntax: "0x"` where go reports the number-overflow error the test expects. Number lexing accepts the magnitude or produces the wrong diagnostic. (The `illegal number syntax: "0x"` symptom was a bound `uint64` literal materializing at the `int64` default inside `fmt.Format` — fixed by #753; the row passes on the merged tree.)
- [x] **minireflect: `reflect.Value.Uint` on a `uint8`-typed value fails inside `eq`** (tools/tmpltests, TestComparison): `eq (index `x` 0) 'x'` errors `reflect.Value.Call: panic: reflect: call of reflect.Value.Uint on uint8 Value` — a rune/`uint8` value boxed without a usable Uint path; same family as the `.Int on int Value` facet.
- [x] **bound `errors.AsType` missing** (tools/tmpltests): `TestExecError_CustomError` needs `errors.AsType[*E]` — a generic host func returning (T, bool); the bind mechanism for a generic instantiation is unclear. (`os.DirFS` is bound + `io/fs` stays source-interpreted — a bound io/fs cannot call embed.FS's script-side methods — and same-spelled host containers retag to the declared type; TestParseFS now passes, #756.)
- [x] **minireflect value-family divergences in the upstream text/template suite** (tools/tmpltests): `TestAddrOfIndex` fixed by the `GoValue{*RValue}` peel in `wrap` (#750); `TestExecError`/`TestExecuteError`/`TestFunctionCheckDuringCall`/`TestInterfaceValues` (`IsNil on struct Value` / `Int on int Value` paths) fixed by declared-result-typed Call results (#751) + interface-kind `IsNil` answering via `IsNilIface` (#754); `TestExecutePanicDuringCall` fixed by propagating the callee's panic raw through `reflect.Value.Call` (#755). Remaining rows are their own entries below (`TestIssue48215`, "range int8", `TestMaxExecDepth`, `TestExecError_CustomError`).
- [x] **`RType.AssignableTo`: a bound host type does not implement a bound/script interface typedef** (tools/tmpltests, `TestExecute` "Interface Call"): `{{stringer .S}}` on `map[string]any{"S": bytes.NewBufferString("x")}` errored `wrong type for value; expected fmt.Stringer; got *bytes.Buffer` where gc prints the buffer. → fixed in three layers (#757, #759, #770): `RValue.Type()` returns `hostTypeOf(reflect.TypeOf(gv.V))` when the stamped td is nil or merely echoes the payload's name (a `*bytes.Buffer` box now Implements `fmt.Stringer`), `typeOfValue` reports an `IfaceNil`'s tag so `Elem()` on an interface holding a typed nil keeps `Kind=ptr` (the "if on typed nil interface value" row), and the td↔td `Implements`/`AssignableTo` path answers host-backed typedefs from the real host method set via `hostRTOf` (#770).
- [x] **`%T` on a range-over-int element prints `*minigo.fmtValueN`** (tools/tmpltests, `TestExecute` "range int8" — first remaining row): `{{range $v := .}}{{printf "%T%d" $v $v}}{{end}}` on `int8(5)` should print `int80int81...`; the harness run prints `*minigo.fmtValue0*minigo.fmtValue1...`. NOT reproducible standalone (same template + `int8(5)` data via `Execute`, `New().Funcs().Parse()`, package-level `[]execTest` var, struct-field `.data` all print `int8`), so the trigger lives in the real exec_srctest.go flow — suspect the element's `Convert(v.Type())` picks up a `*fmtValue` type or the funcs-map/`test` loop-var path repacks `$v`.
- [x] **`FieldByName` promotion fails through a function-local embedded pointer** (found by tools/tmpltests `TestIssue48215`): `type A2 struct{ S string }; type B2 struct{ *A2 }` declared inside a function gives `reflect.TypeOf(B2{}).FieldByName("S")` → `(nil, false)` where gc returns `(S, [0 0])`. The package-level `type B struct{ *A }` works. Likely `fieldTypes`/`elemOf` resolves the embedded `*A2` via the package index, which cannot see function-local typedefs (the frame-local typedef table `localTypedefOf` is not consulted). → fixed: typedefs synthesized in `resolveTypeRef`/`elemTypeRef`/`resolveExpr`/`sliceTypOf` (pointer, slice, map, chan, anon-struct/iface/func arms) dropped the decl-time `LocalTypes` snapshot, so `A2` under `*A2` fell through to the package index; every synthesized typedef now carries `LocalTypes` forward. Pin: `testdata/difffuzz/reflect_local_embedptr`.
- [x] **`TestMaxExecDepth` semantics vs minigo frame limit** (tools/tmpltests): the test expects template's own `maxExecDepth` error; minigo's interpreter frame limit (10000) fires first. Resolved on the harness side: `srcRewrites` now copy+patches `exec.go` to `maxExecDepth = 250`, so the real upstream guard fires well under the frame limit.
- [x] **nil/container re-tag by spelled-name unified distinct types** (union review → fresh review, proven): the same-spelled twin retag in `coerceConcrete` compared unqualified spellings, so script-declared same-named types from different scopes silently re-tagged (repro: `type T []int` pkg-level value assigned into a function-local `type T []string` slot was accepted, gc rejects named-to-named). → fixed by the `sameSpelledTwin` predicate: the value's tag must be host-minted (`Spec == nil`) so the host `[]fs.DirEntry` → script `[]DirEntry` twin survives while script types stay distinct; plus a slice-kind guard in the Named arm (#756). Positive path pinned in `testdata/difffuzz/iofs_twin_retag` (negative case unpinnable — gc compile-errors).
- [x] **`fmt.Fscanf` panicked: scanFn head converter arity mismatch** (fresh review, verified repro): the binding passed `heads=2` but `scanReader` returned a 1-element slice, so `head[1]` was out of range. → `scanFn` now goNatives whatever head args the converter did not produce, and all nine Scan bindings carry `Target` for `%T` signature spelling (#747; Fscan family pinned in `testdata/difffuzz/fmt_sscan`).
- [x] **tools/tmpltests false-PASS paths** (fresh review): `FailNow` panicked without marking the T failed, the driver skipped `RunCleanups` on panic/Skip, and `fail()` propagated only one level so a grandchild subtest failure was lost. → shim `markFailed` walks every ancestor, cleanups run deferred like go test, and `runOne` drops a pre-set GOROOT instead of duplicating it (#745).
- [x] **`reflect.ValueOf(t).Type()` leaked `*minireflect.RType`** (fresh review): boxed facade reflect.Type values reported the internal struct name where gc prints `*reflect.rtype`. → `valueOfValue` views the real host descriptor for host-backed types and stamps script-typed boxes with the `*reflect.rtype` typedef; pinned in `testdata/difffuzz/reflect_rtype_box` (#757).
- [x] **tools/tmpltests: extend coverage** ([docs/sketch/ja/exploration-template-src-tests.md](./docs/sketch/ja/exploration-template-src-tests.md)): currently `text/template` exec+multi tests, 42/46 PASS (remaining rows: "range int8" %T, TestIssue48215, errors.AsType, TestMaxExecDepth). → done in stack #776: all non-SKIP rows pass — `make tmpltests` 46/46, `make tmpltests-parse` 17/17, `make tmpltests-html` 103/105 (2 upstream-broken SKIPs). Still open, tracked as their own entries: `example*_test.go` external test packages (entry below) and range-over-func `iter` rows once the interpreter supports them.
- [x] **32-bit targets do not build** (found while checking the `goroutineID` fallback): `GOOS=linux GOARCH=arm go vet ./vm` fails in `minireflect/rvalue.go`. Constants such as `uintptr(1) << 48` and `0x6d696e69676f` overflow a 32-bit `uintptr`. Either size them per `unsafe.Sizeof(uintptr(0))` or declare 64-bit only with a build constraint and say so in the README. → fixed: `maxAlloc` (1<<48 on 64-bit, 1<<28 on 32-bit) and the `fakeAddr` sentinel ("minigo" on 64-bit, its high word on 32-bit) are sized per `unsafe.Sizeof(uintptr(0))` — `GOOS=linux GOARCH=arm go vet ./...` and `go build ./...` now pass.
- [x] **`sync.Pool.Put` of a script struct value aliases it** (#699 review, also on main): `p.Put(s); s.N = 2; p.Get().(S).N` prints 2 where gc prints 1. The struct crosses opaque now, but a value-typed struct must be copied at Put like Go's interface boxing. → fixed: the three store paths snapshot like gc's boxing — `OpaqueKey`'s orig is now `runtime.Copy(v)` (mapKey→orig restore returned the live struct), `namedStoreValue` deep-copies the payload, and `opaqueStoreValue`'s verbatim path copies too (unhashable structs). Key and value snapshots verified — mutating the source key does not move the entry. Pin: `testdata/difffuzz/opaque_store_copy`.
- [x] **copying a host-backed value aliases it** (#699 review follow-up, also on main): `var a bytes.Buffer; b := a; b.WriteString("x")` prints `1 1` where gc prints `0 1` (`strings.Builder` too). A `HostNew` typedef's zero value is a pointer-shaped `GoValue` box, and assignment copies the box, not the host struct. → fixed: `runtime.Copy`'s Named case snapshots `*struct` boxes whose tag is a `KindStruct`+`HostNew` typedef (the tag distinguishes `var a T` from a host-returned `*T` that must share — verified `var p *bytes.Buffer = &a` still aliases correctly), and `coerce` applies the same snapshot for untagged values landing on a HostNew-struct slot. Covers `:=`, explicit `var`, params, returns, map/chan stores, and nested struct fields. Pin: `testdata/difffuzz/hostnew_assign_copy`.
- [x] **a named-int index loses `s[i].N++` on a struct slice element** (#699 review, also on main): with `type I int`, `s[i].N++` on `[]S` leaves `s[0].N` unchanged (gc: incremented), while `m[i][0]++` on a map works. The `I`-tagged index likely misses the element ref path.
- [x] **inferring a type parameter from a host error value traps `undefined: T`** (found while writing `generic_infer_cache`, also on main): with `func kind[T any](x T) string`, both `var err error = errors.New("e"); kind(err)` and `for _, v := range []any{1, errors.New("e")} { kind(v) }` (on the error) trap where gc binds T=error / T=any. `var v any = errors.New("e"); kind(v)` works. The static type seems to be dropped for these call sites, and a host error value yields no typedef of its own.
- [x] **non-error host values still leave type parameters unbound** (follow-up to the host-error inference fix): `kind(strings.NewReader("r"))` traps `undefined: T` where gc binds T=*strings.Reader. Only error-implementing host values yield a typedef for inference; other host results have no static type the call site can see. A constraint-aware `any` fallback (or synthesizing a typedef from the reflect type) would close the same trap for non-error host values. → fixed: argTypedef synthesizes a typedef for non-error host values via hostTypedefOf (reflect type → named typedef with a HostNew box, so `kind(strings.NewReader)` binds T=*strings.Reader and interface constraints read the real method set)
- [x] **Bound `strconv.IntSize` is missing** ([docs/sketch/experiment-oapi-template-cache.md](./docs/sketch/experiment-oapi-template-cache.md#minimum-reproductions)): `fmt.Println(strconv.IntSize)` traps `undefined: strconv.IntSize`; native prints `64` on arm64. Source base64 decoding also reaches it through JSON byte-slice decoding. Bind the architecture-appropriate constant without replacing the rest of the bound package.
- [x] **bound `strconv` is missing `ErrRange`/`ErrSyntax` and the `NumError` type** (found while binding `IntSize`) → fixed: sentinels bound as `GoValue` boxes so `errors.Is`/Unwrap see the real objects, `strconv.NumError` bound as a host typedef, and `errors.As`/`AsType` match `GoValue`-boxed host errors on their reflect type (`matchAsTarget`/`sameErrTypHost`, intrinsics.go) — pin `testdata/difffuzz/strconv_numerr`.
- [x] **JSON-decoded byte slices lose assignment type information** ([docs/sketch/experiment-oapi-template-cache.md](./docs/sketch/experiment-oapi-template-cache.md#minimum-reproductions)) → fixed: `RValue.SetBytes` stamped a synthesised anonymous `KindSlice` typedef (spelled "slice") that rejected every `[]byte` target; it now stamps the slot's declared typedef `v.td` like `MakeSlice` already does — pin `testdata/difffuzz/json_decode_bytes` (SRC `encoding/json encoding/hex slices`).
- [x] **untyped `1<<uint(x)` picks up the shift count's type** (found via tmpltests `TestParseZipFS`, source `compress/flate` `huffmanDecoder.init`): `code != 1<<uint(max)` traps `mismatched types int and uint` where gc types the shift result as `int` — an untyped left operand of a non-constant shift takes its default type, independent of the (unsigned) count operand. The shift-typing path widens the result to the count's type instead. → fixed: `staticOpTyp` resolved a shift's type from its count operand (arithmetic's "named side wins" rule); shifts now resolve to the left operand's type — untyped takes its default — pin `testdata/difffuzz/shift_untyped_lhs`.
- [x] **`""` is accepted as a template function name** (tmpltests `make tmpltests-html`, upstream `TestBadFuncNames`): `template.New("x").Funcs(map[string]any{"": f})` succeeds where gc reports an error — the empty-name check in `funcName`/`badName` is missed or weakened under source-interpreted `text/template`. → fixed: root cause was composite-literal key classification — `unfoldTypExpr` never resolved `pkg.T` selectors, so `FuncMap{name: f}` treated `name` as a field-name constant ("name") instead of an expression. `unfoldTypExpr`/`unfoldSpecType` now resolve selectors through the imported package's index in the spec's own file scope — pin `testdata/difffuzz/src_pkgtype_maplit_keys`.
- [x] **host nil-pointer panic inside upstream `TestEscapeSet`** (tmpltests `make tmpltests-html`): the test driver recovers `runtime error: invalid memory address or nil pointer dereference` — a host-side dereference in the escaper path (likely minireflect on a nil receiver/GoValue), not a script panic the test could produce. → fixed: a field declared with a function-local type (`[]*dataItem` inside the test) is invisible to the package index, so `fieldTypes` emitted a hole td and `Index`/`Elem` propagated `td == nil` into an addressable view — `Addr()` dereferenced `v.td` unconditionally. It now recovers the type via `typeOfValue(v.get())` like `Type()` does — pin `testdata/difffuzz/reflect_addr_localtype`.
- [x] **bound `archive/zip` cannot read deflated entries** (tmpltests `make tmpltests-html`, upstream `TestParseZipFS`): `ParseFS` on the test's zip archive fails `error parsing files: zip: unsupported compression algorithm` — bound zip lacks the Deflate path (`compress/flate` works bound when reached directly). → fixed: the real chain was four layered walls, not a bound-pkg gap — (a) `sync.Map` lost the `decompressors` registration because `opaqueStoreArg` crossed `*runtime.Named` by pointer (fixed: Named crosses by value + `UConst` payloads materialize — pin `testdata/difffuzz/syncmap_named_keys`); (b) `1<<uint(max)` shift typing in `compress/flate` (fixed — see `shift_untyped_lhs` entry); (c) a field typedef kept `*[maxNumLit+maxNumDist]int`'s length as the raw const expr while `new()` folded its own, so `TypIdentical` saw different spellings and the pointee check trapped "cannot use &[316]int as *[*ast.BinaryExpr]int" (fixed: array lengths fold before the pointee identity compare — pin `testdata/difffuzz/arraylen_named_const`); (d) `hash/crc32` amd64 init reads `unsafe.Offsetof(cpu.X86.HasAVX512VPCLMULQDQ)` — the intrinsic was a stub (fixed: the compiler passes the field name alongside the base and the intrinsic measures the offset — pin `testdata/difffuzz/unsafe_offsetof_field`). `TestParseZipFS` passes end-to-end.
- [x] **tmpltests: `srcRewrites` only patches the target package** (`tools/tmpltests`): upstream `TestMaxExecDepth` under `make tmpltests-html` trapped `stack exhausted` because `text/template/exec.go` — a `-src` dependency there — was symlinked verbatim and kept `maxExecDepth = initMaxExecDepth()` (100000) while the interpreter frame limit is 10000. → fixed: `srcRewrites` is keyed by package path and a rewritten `-src` dependency's directory materializes (real dirs + symlinked siblings + patched copies); `TestMaxExecDepth` passes under both `tmpltests` and `tmpltests-html`.
- [x] **tmpltests: `example*_test.go` cannot run** (see tools/tmpltests/README.md): examples are `package template_test` external tests — they need a second generated package dir (e.g. `text/template_test`) instead of the same-dir driver; `// Output:` expectations live in comments so the driver must parse them and compare captured process stdout; and deps are unbound/incomplete — `log` resolves to source-interpreted GOROOT but traps on `fmt.Appendln`, `examplefiles_test.go` needs `os.MkdirTemp`/`os.Create`/`filepath`/`io.WriteString`/`os.RemoveAll` + `template.ParseGlob`/`ParseFiles`/`Must`. → done: `-examples` mode copies the files into `<work>/goroot/src/<pkg>_test/`, drives each `ExampleXxx` between `EXOUT` markers, and compares captured stdout against the `// Output:`/`// Unordered output:` comment host-side; `fmt.Append*` got binds so source `log` works. New targets `make tmpltests-examples` (8/8) and `make tmpltests-html-examples` (9/9) — all 17 upstream examples pass.
- [x] **named-basic values don't reach host `String()`** (found in the review-response `time.Month` fix): `fmt.Println(time.January)` prints `1` where gc prints `January`, and `m.String()` traps — the unified `Named{Typ: monthTD, V: int64}` shape never reaches the host's String method. Const and conversion paths agree with each other now (#802) but both diverge from gc. → fixed: `runtime.HostScalarOf` materializes a host-scalar typedef's script-shaped payload (stored int64 or const-domain UConst) to the bound Go scalar at read sites — member dispatch, fmt rendering, `goNative` host crossings — so `time.Month(3).String()`/`m.String()`/`m + 1` print `March`/`April`, and `%d` still reads `3`. `json.Number` gets `HostNew` so `String`/`Int64`/`Float64` resolve through the StructTag machinery (string payload kept — `len`/`[:]`/concat unaffected). Pin: `testdata/difffuzz/named_basic_host_methods`.
- [x] **`unsafe.Sizeof` on structs is still runtime-value based** (same family as the `unsafe.Offsetof` fix): `unsafeSizeOf` accumulates the sizes of the value's current field payloads instead of the declared field types — the `minireflect` layout helpers (`fieldOffset`/`sizeOf`/`alignOf`, shared via `OffsetOf` in #804) should drive it too. → fixed: `unsafeSizeof`/`unsafeAlignof` recover the operand's declared typedef (`sizeDeclTyp`) and answer through exported `minireflect.SizeOf`/`AlignOf` — struct fields pad per declared widths, arrays size elem·N, `unsafe.Alignof` aligns by declared type. Also fixed `sizeOf` reporting 4 for `complex64` (it's 8, and fed `fieldOffset` too — `unsafe.Offsetof` on structs with complex64 fields was misaligned). Host boxes answer via `reflect.Type.Size` (`complex128(1)` → 16). Pin: `testdata/difffuzz/unsafe_sizeof_decl`.
- [x] **`unsafe.Sizeof`/`Alignof` on an interface-typed operand reads the stored value**: `var x any = int64(1); unsafe.Sizeof(x)` answered 8 (gc 16 — the interface pair), and `sl[0]` on `[]I` sized the concrete element — interface slots arrive unboxed, so the declared typedef is lost at the value level. → fixed: the compiler rewrites `unsafe.Sizeof`/`Alignof` calls to carry the operand's static typedef (the `staticTyp` lane used by generics and assert-panic messages) as extra arguments — ident/local/global/upvalue statics via `OpLocalTyp`/`OpGlobalTyp`/`OpUpvalTyp`, an indexed operand `c[i]` via its container's typedef so the intrinsic sizes the element type (`unsafeStaticTyp` peels pointer containers for `parr[i]`). Residual: operand shapes `staticTyp` cannot type (selector `s.f`, call results, dereferences) still fall back to the value path — `s.f` on an interface field answers 16 today because the box measures 16 anyway. Pin: `testdata/difffuzz/unsafe_sizeof_iface`.
- [x] **`errors.Is` cannot see a script `Unwrap() []error` child's subtree**: the host `errors.Is` only walks `Unwrap() error`, so `errors.Is(err, target)` misses targets wrapped through a script multi-child unwrap (the `findAsTarget` walk added for As/AsType in #800 handles both shapes via `errKids`, but `Is` goes through the host stdlib for host-side targets). → fixed: `errIs` reimplements gc's walk script-side — `err == target` via canonical `TryOpaqueKey` equality for script values (native == for natives, never cross-kind), each node's own `Is(error) bool` (new `scriptError.Is` dispatches the script `Is` member), then `errKids` children depth-first — so a script `Unwrap() []error` fans out and `errors.Is` on mixed chains matches gc. Pin: `testdata/difffuzz/errors_is_script_walk`.
- [x] **`inspect.Expr`: function-local type/var/const names get a package `SymbolID`** ([#920](https://github.com/podhmo/minigo/issues/920)): `localNames` only collected `:=`/range/param bindings, so `type local struct{...}` or `var x T` inside a body was attributed to the package and `inspect.Resolve` trapped `no decl local in <pkg>`. Local `DeclStmt` names now count as "possibly local", so `SymbolID` is nil and `Resolve` returns nil.
- [x] `inspect.Expr` attribution gaps found reviewing #924 > **predeclared funcs/consts are attributed to the package**: `predeclared` only lists type names, so `len`, `new`, `append`, `nil`, `true`, `iota`, ... fall through to `{<pkg>, len}` and `Resolve` traps `no decl len in <pkg>`. Expected `{:builtin:, len}` and a nil `Resolve`.
- [x] `inspect.Expr` attribution gaps found reviewing #924 > **a bare import name is attributed to the package**: `Expr` on the `strings` of `strings.Repeat(...)` reports `{<pkg>, strings}` and `Resolve` traps. A file-scope import binding should yield no SymbolID.
- [x] `inspect.Expr` attribution gaps found reviewing #924 > **residual: `Import.LocalName` is a path-tail approximation**: the import name comes from the path's last element (minus a `/vN` suffix), not the imported package clause, so the bare `yaml` of `gopkg.in/yaml.v3` (tail `yaml.v3`) or `bar` of `github.com/x/go-bar` is not recognized and still traps in `Resolve`. Selector attribution and `UsedSymbols` share the approximation; reading the package clause (e.g. via the planned `syntax.ParsePackageClause`) would fix all three.
- [x] `inspect.Expr` attribution gaps found reviewing #924 > **non-referring identifiers get a SymbolID**: a selector's `Sel` (`x.B`'s `B`, `strings.Repeat`'s `Repeat`), composite-literal keys (`local{B: 1}`), struct field names, labels (`L:`, `break L`) and the blank `_` all report `{<pkg>, name}` and `Resolve` traps. These positions name no declaration and should yield no SymbolID.
- [x] `inspect.Expr` attribution gaps found reviewing #924 > **`UsedSymbols` ignores shadowing**: `UsedSymbolsOf` counts every `x.Sel` whose `x` matches an import name, so `strings.TrimSpace` on a local `var strings = ...` or a param `strings` is reported as an import use. Fixed by scoping each local binding by position (params over the body, `:=`/`var` after their statement, type params over the decl), so a shadow hides only the selects inside its scope.
- [x] `inspect.Expr` attribution gaps found reviewing #924 > **duplicate local-scope analysis** (found in the #929 reuse review): `syntax/lang.go`'s `localDecls` and #928's `inspect.localScopes` were near-identical position-span scope walks. Both now use `syntax.LocalScopes`, which follows the spec (a `:=`/`var` name is in scope after its statement, params only over the body, receiver type params bound) — this also fixes the language gate accepting `min := min(1, 2)` in a go1.17 module, which gc rejects.
- [x] `inspect.Expr` attribution gaps found reviewing #924 > **duplicate predeclared-name tables** (#929 reuse review): inspect's `predeclared`/`predeclaredValues`, compile's `universeIdent` value list and runtime's `predeclaredTypeName` now share `syntax.IsPredeclaredType`/`IsPredeclaredValue`. Left as is: compile's `predeclaredTypeNames` (it lacks `comparable`; whether that is deliberate is unverified, so its type-position lookups were not switched), `complete.go`'s keyword-ish `predeclared` candidates, and `examples/convert-define`'s own list (separate module).
- [x] `inspect.Expr` attribution gaps found reviewing #924 > **share composite-literal key classification with compile**: inspect's `litKeysRefer` looks one level deep, while compile's `literalKeysAreExprs` / `peelLitType` (`compile/compile.go`) also peel elided inner literals and resolve aliases, local and imported types. `peelLitType` is stateless and could move to `syntax`; the name resolution would go through `TypeExpr.Unwrap` or the index. Would close most of the "composite-literal keys of an unknown literal type" residual above.
- [x] **`inspect.Resolve` traps on a member of a host-bound package** ([#921](https://github.com/podhmo/minigo/issues/921)): `Resolve` on `time.Time` trapped `no decl Time in time` because the resolver only looked in the (absent) index. It now answers the Kind "host" pseudo-decl `Symbol` gives; a name the bound package lacks still traps.
- [x] **`inspect`: no non-trapping variant of `Resolve`** ([#922](https://github.com/podhmo/minigo/issues/922)): traps are unrecoverable (`recover()` sees nil while deferred funcs still run), and `Resolve` has no `Lookup*` twin, so a walk cannot degrade gracefully on an unresolvable name. Add `LookupResolve` per the trap/Lookup pairing, and document the defer/recover behavior during a trap.
- [x] **`inspect.Implementers` treats a bound embedded interface as no requirement**: for `type S interface{ fmt.Stringer }`, `Implementers(p, S)` lists every type in the package (found reviewing #932; same on main). The embed resolves to a host decl with no AST, so `requiredSpecs` contributes nothing for it. The method set of a bound interface is only reachable through reflect (or `SourceOf`); until then it should be an unknown requirement (trap or skip the iface), not an empty one.
- [x] **`convert-define`: element-wise conversion through generic instantiations** (`check04-generic-instantiation`, PR #132 review): `type SrcList List[int]` unwraps only as far as the instantiation — `Unwrap`/`SymbolID` cannot resolve `List[int]`, so the walk stops at the parametric spec `[]T` and the real element types are invisible; both sides warn (`no conversion covers ... (generic instantiation)`) and keep the honest raw assignment instead of leafCast's uncompilable `DstList(src)`. Real support needs type-argument substitution (instantiating the spec `[]T` as `[]int`), which the inspect layer deliberately does not do.
- [x] **Repo-enumeration surface for scripted detection** (surfaced by the test-detect spike, [docs/sketch/plan-test-detect.md](./docs/sketch/plan-test-detect.md)): `syntax.File.Imports`/`inspect.ImportsOf` already expose per-file imports, but nothing enumerates "every package under a root across nested go.mod's" with test-file presence — `pkg/locator` only resolves a named import inside one module. Such a surface would let affected-package detection run as a script step on the shared parse (combined world: detect → gen-sync → generate → test in one pass).
- [x] **REPL `:load` approximations** (#433; resolved: took-over prompt decls/cells are restored by re-load or the new `:unload`, grouped decls split per spec, shadowed var/const names blanked to keep specs and `iota`, `:ls` tags loaded decls with their file): a loaded var that took over a same-named prompt var is gone (not reverted to the prompt value) once removed from the file and reloaded; a grouped prompt `type ( A ...; B ... )` is dropped whole when a load declares `A`. Conversely, a prompt decl taking over one name of a loaded multi-name var/const spec drops the whole spec, and dropping a spec from a loaded const group shifts `iota` and implicit repetition for the specs after it. No unload short of `:reset`, and `:ls` does not say which file a decl came from.
- [x] **`unsafe.Pointer` as a type is unbound** (tools/tmpltests, also `--src sort`'s `internal/reflectlite` wall in the `--src strings` entry): `UPI unsafe.Pointer` fields and `unsafe.Pointer(&n)` conversions trap. tmpltests currently rewrites `unsafe.Pointer`→`any` in copied test files; real support needs the pointer type plus conversions.
- [x] **`reflect.Value.Convert` from `[]rune` to `string` writes bytes, not runes** (found while reworking minireflect doc comments): `reflect.ValueOf([]rune("あい")).Convert(reflect.TypeOf("")).Interface()` prints `BD` under minigo and `あい` under go. The string branch of `RValue.Convert` (minireflect/rvalue.go) writes each element with `WriteByte`; a rune element type must append the rune.
- [x] **`Engine.SourceOf` should read source regardless of `ModeDeny`** (found while verifying inspect doc comments): SourceOf exists to read code for understanding, so a deny mode — which gates imports — should not stop it. A bound denied path already loads its source, but an unbound denied path fails because `SourceOf` falls through to `loadPath`, which checks the mode. Make SourceOf bypass the mode check for unbound paths too. Resolved: denied paths now take the same private `srcs` build as bound paths, so deny keeps gating imports/`Package` while SourceOf reads the source.
- [x] **qualified const/var operand types went unread for source-indexed packages** (member-inference slice of [#724](https://github.com/podhmo/minigo/issues/724) — not the whole issue): `var y = inner.C` bound y as unknown — a source package's `Globals` stay empty until init runs — so `y == i` skipped the mismatched-types check gc applies (`invalid operation: mismatched types rune and int`). Reading `p.Index` decls through the declaring file's own scope resolves it.
- [x] **foreign member chains and bound host-scalar consts typed through declaration-origin scope** (slice of [#724](https://github.com/podhmo/minigo/issues/724)): `var y = inner.Dur` where `inner.Dur = time.Second` bound y as unknown — caller-import rewriting could not re-scope `time` since the caller never imports it — so `y == i` evaluated where gc rejects (`mismatched types time.Duration and int64`).
