A synthesized `.imp:` qualifier now binds the declaring file's own import ref directly, and a bound member carrying a raw host scalar (`time.Second`) gets its tag by matching the host type back to the package's typedefs.
- [x] **a script-side declared `*runtime.Package` type does not unify with the intrinsic's package value** (found by the extract-schema prototype, [docs/sketch/plan-extract-schema.md](./docs/sketch/plan-extract-schema.md)): `func load() *runtime.Package { return inspect.DirOf(d) }` traps `cannot use *runtime.Package as *runtime.Package` — the `runtime` package a script imports and the engine's intrinsic `runtime.Package` are different type identities, so the return-type conversion check rejects the value. `:=`-inferred slots work (gen-sync never needed the annotation). Either unify the identities or make the trap message name the mismatch.
- [x] **script return values cross to the host as `*runtime.Tuple` / boxed containers, and every host re-implements the unwrap** (extract-schema spike, same shape gen-sync already absorbed): a `(string, error)` script return arrives as `*runtime.Tuple` (manual `Elems` indexing + `IfaceTaggedNil`/`GoValue` error-slot decoding), and a `map[string]any` return arrives as `*runtime.Map` with nested values still boxed — re-feeding it as a `Call` arg needs a recursive host-side deep-unwrap (`unwrapDeep` in extract-schema, gen-sync carries the same code shape). A shared helper (e.g. `runtime.DeepUnwrap` or a tuple/result extractor) would deduplicate the pattern every script-driven example now hand-rolls.
- [x] **`inspect.TypeFields` (intrinsic) is errorless where host `TypeFieldsOf` errors** (extract-schema spike): on a non-composite `TypeExpr` the script-visible intrinsic returns an empty list while the host twin reports an error, so a walk must gate on `Kind` first or silently lose fields. Also, `Decl.Doc`/`Field.Doc` keep the trailing `\n` — emitters must trim. Both are minor API-shape nits rather than bugs; unify or document the asymmetry.
- [x] **script arg boundary rejects `[]map[string]any` on spelling, and `[]any` elements arrive `*runtime.GoValue`-boxed** (extract-schema DescRules spike): `func Main(..., rs []map[string]any)` traps `cannot use []map[string]interface {} as []map[string]any` — the arg check is spelling-based (`interface {}` vs `any`, and `interface{}` vs `interface {}` flip the space). Passing `[]any` instead lands each element as an unindexable `*runtime.GoValue`. Only `[]string`/`[]byte`-shaped element types auto-unbox reliably; structured plugin data had to move to string spellings. Unify the spelling comparison or deep-unbox `[]any` elements.
- [x] **`inspect.Symbol(pkg, name)` traps on a missing name — no non-trapping lookup** (extract-schema desc-hook spike): optional plugin entry points (`DescField`/`DescDecl`) can only be probed by scanning `inspect.Decls` and matching name+kind by hand. A `Lookup`-style soft variant (nil on miss) would make "does this package export X?" a one-liner.
- [x] **`x.(*inspect.Field)`/`func(*inspect.Field, ...)` asserts fail on inspect view types — not registered, not interned** (extract-schema desc-hook spike): `inspect.Value(pp,"DescField").(func(*inspect.Field,string)string)` traps "interface conversion: ... types from different packages", and `x.(*inspect.Field)` inside a plugin traps `undefined: inspect.Field` — view types aren't type symbols. NOTE: source-package imports ARE interned across `inspect.DirOf` roots — `.(func(*extschema.Config))` asserts cleanly — so typed plugin contracts work; it's only the stub/intrinsic types that lack identity. Register the inspect view types (and intern them) so hooks can name `*inspect.Field` directly; until then `func(any,...)` + `reflect.FieldByName` is the workaround.
- [x] **a package's loading path (bound stub vs source index) changes its behavior — let callers choose** (extract-schema plugin spike): whether a package resolves through bound intrinsics or parsed source is decided per package and invisibly, but the consequences leak: `*inspect.Field`/`Decl` aren't interned while `*extschema.Config` is, `inspect.Standard()` is only true behind the bound shadow, `-stdlib leaf|walk` exists precisely because `time.Time` can't afford full parsing, and a symbol absent from the bind map traps `undefined` instead of falling through to source. An option to request "source interpretation only" (or "bound-only") per lookup/package would make the choice visible and let tools pin behavior instead of inheriting whatever the bound coverage happens to shadow — either as a load flag/mode on the existing entry points, or as **separate load functions** (e.g. a `DirOf`-source and a `BoundOf`-stub split) so the loading path is spelled in the call.
- [x] **extract-schema `-pkg` mode is dead: `inspect.DirOf(target)` runs before the pkgMode branch** (extract-schema corpus m04/m05, [minigo-usecasefuzz](https://github.com/podhmo/minigo-usecasefuzz) `extract-schema/`): the script calls `inspect.DirOf(target)` unconditionally, then only probes `inspect.PackageOf` inside the pkgMode check — so `-pkg example.com/m` and `-pkg time` both trap in `DirOf` on a non-directory string. Reorder so pkg mode skips `DirOf`, or soften `DirOf` itself (see next entry).
- [x] **`inspect.DirOf` traps on any unresolvable path — same hole as `inspect.Symbol`** (corpus m04/m05/m08/p06, sibling of the "no non-trapping lookup" entry above): `DirOf` on a missing dir, `-pkg` target string, or absent `-plugin` dir exits with `runtime trap: ...`; no soft variant exists, so tools cannot distinguish "no such dir" from "dir has no Go files" or degrade gracefully. Extend the non-trapping-`Lookup` ask to the dir/package loaders.
- [x] **generic type params resolve as package symbols → `inspect.Symbol: no T` trap** (corpus c06/c07/c11): `Box[T]` fields, generic type decls, and aliases to `Box[int]` all attribute `T` to the declaring package, so the schemaFor walk traps where gc keeps the param unbound. SymbolID needs a type-param marker (or the walk a bound-substitution surface — the `convert-define` generic-instantiation entry above is the same wall from the other side).
- [x] **`-stdlib walk` on packages with cgo files traps `cgo is not supported: import "C"`** (corpus l02): `inspect.SourceOf` on `net` dies on the first cgo-importing file instead of skipping it, making walk mode unusable for whole stdlib subtrees. Filter `import "C"` files the way `_test.go` files are filtered, or soften the failure per-file.
- [x] **`inspect.Value` hands scripts raw const cells — enum values never arrive as JSON scalars** (corpus n04/n05/l05): `Value` returns the const's `Elem`, so untyped int consts materialize as literal-text `UConst`s (`"1"`) and typed-bound consts (e.g. `time.Duration`) arrive as `*runtime.GoValue` and emit via `String()` (`"-2562047h47m16.854775808s"`); a struct-typed const emits `"{0 0}"`. Also `EnumMembersOf` misses members whose type is only inferred (`Microsecond = 1000 * Nanosecond`). Either the API returns materialized scalars or the members contract documents the wrapper types.
- [x] **`Sig.ParamFields` counts `*ast.Field` groups, not params — arity checks have holes** (corpus p05): `func TwoArgs(a, b *extschema.Config)` is one ParamField, so a plugin signature check written as `len(ParamFields) != 1` admits it and the later `.(func(*C))` assert panics `interface conversion`. A names-flattened param view (or a documented count-the-names recipe) would close the hole.
- [x] **extract-schema tool-side divergences found by the corpus** (recording only; fixes are example-side): `exported()` uses an ASCII `A-Z` check so non-ASCII exported fields vanish (n06); the recursive-embed rescue invents a `"TypeName"` property where `encoding/json` drops the subtree (e02); `json:",string"` and other tag options are ignored (t01); `jsonschema-override:"required=..."` silently no-ops (t03); `[]byte` maps to an array instead of JSON's base64 string (s02). Ideal outputs are pinned as DIFF wants in the corpus.
- [x] **extract-schema `-pkg` cannot resolve stdlib type names** (corpus m05, follow-up of the `-pkg` entry above): `-pkg time Time` exits FAIL with `type "Time" not found in time` — `inspect.LookupPackageOf` answers the bound host package (globals only, no source index), and `Main` never falls back to `inspect.SourceOf`, so source-level decls of bound stdlib paths are unreachable. Decide whether the -pkg loader should retry through `SourceOf` (and how that composes with `-stdlib leaf`) — expected verdict moves from today's graceful FAIL to a real schema once pinned. Resolved: `-pkg` retries bound shadows through `inspect.LookupSourceOf` — the `-stdlib` policy only governs named-type refs inside the walk, so leaf stays a ref-level choice.
- [x] **`*runtime.Package` has no typedef tag — engine-internal values bypass the tag system** (residual of #875): a `*runtime.Package` value belongs to no case in `typeOfValue`/`pointeeTag`/`Deref`, so `func() *runtime.Package` signatures assert/coerce only through the `HostNew`-native comparison added there. Runtime-internal values need a real typedef tag story (or a documented carve-out) rather than per-type host paths. Resolved: the opaque-handle class (`Package`/`TypeDef`/`Iterator`/`ImportRef`) synthesizes its tag through `internalTypedef` → `hostTypedefOf`, and `TypIdentical` compares host typedefs by native type, so tag funnels answer like any other pointer.
- [x] **`inspect.FileOf` / `inspect.SourceOf` have no soft `Lookup*` variant** (deferred from #866): `LookupDirOf`/`LookupPackageOf` return nil on miss, but the file and source loaders still trap; no corpus case hit them, so the variants were never built. Add `LookupFileOf`/`LookupSourceOf` if a caller needs graceful probing of single files or bound-shadowed source.
- [x] **compile: map-literal keys read as field names under a generic map, a named slice or `&T` elision**: with `const k = "kk"`, `Pair[string, int]{k: 1}` (`type Pair[K comparable, V any] map[K]V`), `type Ms []map[string]int; Ms{{k: 1}}` and `[]*map[string]int{{k: 1}}` store the key `"k"` instead of evaluating `k` — go prints `map[kk:1]`, minigo `map[k:1]`. `unfoldTypExpr` does not see through a generic instantiation, and `compileLit` peels the outer type syntactically (`syntax.PeelLitType`) before resolving names, so a named outer type or a `*T` element peels to nothing. inspect's `litTypeOf`/`litUnderlying` (`inspect/inspect.go`) resolve between peels and could serve as the model; pin a difffuzz case first.
- [x] **IMPORTANT — `f(xs...)` does not share the caller's slice** (wrong results, silently): with `func mut(xs ...int) { xs[0] = 99 }`, `xs := []int{1, 2}; mut(xs...)` leaves `xs` as `[1 2]` where go prints `[99 2]` — Go passes the slice itself, so the callee's element writes (and `append` within capacity) must reach the caller's backing array. Any in-place helper written variadically (`sortInPlace(v...)`, `fill(buf...)`) is affected. Found in the #961 follow-up review (pre-existing).
- [x] **flaky difffuzz case `opaque_store_copy`**: once on CI (#949, Go 1.26) minigo printed nothing where go prints `1\n2\n2\n{[1]} true`, and it passes locally on repeat. Unverified cause: the case reads `p.Get().(S)` right after `p.Put(s)`, but `sync.Pool` may drop an item (GC, or the goroutine moving to another P between Put and Get), so `Get` returns nil and the assertion panics. Pin the snapshot semantics without relying on Pool retention.
- [x] **host named basics still miss their source twin in composite and generic paths** (found reviewing #948; also wrong on main): a boxed host `token.Token` / `fs.FileMode` equals the source constants in `==`, `switch` and typed map keys, but not when nested or generic. Each is false/0 where go is true: struct/array equality with such a field (`rec{as.Tok} == rec{token.DEFINE}`; `eqlValue`'s field/elem loops skip `twinOperands`), struct map keys holding a host FileMode (the Token version hits — key normalisation differs), `map[any]int{fs.ModeDir: 1}[t]`, and generics (`eq[T comparable](as.Tok, token.DEFINE)` traps `cannot use Token as token.Token`; `slices.Contains`/`Index`/`Equal` miss).
- [x] interface-typed keys and composite fields drop the dynamic type > **map keys**: `map[any]int{int8(1): 1}[1]` was 1 and `map[any]int{M(2): 5}[uint32(2)]` 5 where go gives 0, and ranging yielded `int 2` for a `main.M` key. Interface-keyed maps now key by (dynamic type, value) (`runtime.Map.IfaceKey`, `runtime.IfaceKeyOf`), as do `any` fields of struct keys.
- [x] interface-typed keys and composite fields drop the dynamic type > **maps made outside make/literals key untyped**: `reflect.MakeMap`, host map snapshots and host `map[any]any` results now set `Map.IfaceKey` too.
- [x] interface-typed keys and composite fields drop the dynamic type > **equality of interface-typed slots**: `S{int8(1)} == S{1}` with `type S struct{ X any }` and `[1]any{M(2)} == [1]any{uint32(2)}` are true where go gives false: `eqlValue` peels Named tags field by field with no type check for interface-typed slots (boxed host named basics are already compared as (type, value) pairs).
- [x] **`maps.Keys`/`maps.Values` return slices, not `iter.Seq`** (found fixing their element types; also wrong on main): `for k := range maps.Keys(map[string]bool{"a": true})` prints the index `0` where go prints `a`, because the intrinsics hand back a `*runtime.Slice` that range treats as a slice. `slices.Sorted` happens to accept it (and reads the `[]K` typedef off it). Return a seq (`seqOf`) and carry the element type to `slices.Sorted` another way; `slices.Collect` and `maps.Collect` are unbound too (`undefined: slices.Collect`).
- [x] ordering/key gaps left by the host-twin review of #982–#986 > **`slices.SortedFunc` is unbound**.
- [x] Data-structure / algorithm-level performance candidates > **generic specializations recompile on every call**: `inferBinds` and `instantiateFunc` (`vm/vm.go`) return a fresh `*runtime.Function` without a `Chunk`, so each `Id(x)` re-runs `EnsureCompiled` (100k calls of a trivial `Id[T]`: ~99 ms vs ~50 ms non-generic; grows with body size). Cache specializations keyed by origin function + binds; the key must respect type identity, already-bound binds, and function-local types. Synthetic only: no compile frames in the grafana-openapi CPU profile. (Chunks and inferred instances were already cached by #702; explicit `F[T]` instances are now cached too — `BenchmarkGenericCall`.)
- [x] **exact-domain const ops lose gc's operand typing — `float64(7)/2` gives 3, `1 << n` with a var count traps overflow** (regression from #635, found re-running the minigo-usecasefuzz `language/` corpus): `float64(7) / 2` prints `3` instead of `3.5` — the typed const's float64 kind is dropped so the op runs as int division; and `n := uint(70); 1 << n` traps `constant 1180591620717411303424 overflows int` where gc defaults `1` to int and wraps to 0 — a non-constant shift count must not pull the left untyped const into const eval (`Shl/Shr` keying on the left operand's tag fires on the `1` first). Both pass at 74e368f and fail from 294a60a.
- [x] **typed nil host pointers read as untyped nil**: `goValueOf` turns a nil `*ast.FieldList` (`f.Recv` of an `inspect.AST` node) into `NIL`, so `var n ast.Node = f.Recv; n == nil` is true (go: false), `x.(*ast.FieldList)` fails, and `f.Recv.NumFields()` / `f.Doc.Text()` panic instead of calling the nil-receiver method. Deliberate in `goValueOf` today; a typed nil of the host pointer type would keep Go's semantics. Found in the #939 review.
- [x] **io/fs sentinels exist twice when io/fs is interpreted from source**: `errors.Is(err, fs.ErrNotExist)` on an `os.Open` error is false (`os.ErrNotExist` works), and `filepath.WalkDir` with a callback returning `fs.SkipDir` reports `skip this directory` as an error — the host `filepath` only knows the host `fs.SkipDir`. Found in the #939 review.
- [x] **a script-built `*fs.PathError` is not a host `*fs.PathError` to host os helpers**: `os.IsPermission(&fs.PathError{Op: "open", Path: "p", Err: fs.ErrPermission})` is false (go: true); `errors.Is` on it works. io/fs is interpreted, so the value is a script struct and `asErr` hands host `os.IsPermission`/`IsNotExist`/`IsExist` an adapter whose `underlyingError` type switch misses `*PathError`. Converting a source-twin struct to its host type at the boundary (or unwrapping script errors in these helpers) would fix it. Found while fixing the io/fs sentinel item.
- [x] **`filepath.Walk` is unbound**: `filepath.Walk(root, func(string, fs.FileInfo, error) error)` traps `undefined: filepath.Walk`; only `WalkDir` is bound. Found while fixing the io/fs sentinel item.
- [x] **a script error returned through a host io helper loses its identity**: with a script `Read` returning `me` (`var me = &myErr{}`), `_, err := io.ReadAll(r); err == me` is false and `err` prints as `myErr[]` (go: `mine true`). `readResult64` (`intrinsics.go`) decodes the callback's error with `asErr(goNative(...))`, which formats the script value into a fresh error; `hostErrOf` + `scriptErrUnbox` (as `filepath.Walk`/`WalkDir` now do) would keep it. The `template.Must` arm has the same `asErr(goNative(...))` shape. Found while binding `filepath.Walk`.
- [x] **shift with a non-constant count ignores the context type**: io/fs's `FileMode.String` does `m&(1<<uint(32-1-i))` and traps `mismatched types FileMode and int` — the untyped `1` must take FileMode from `m &`. `fmt.Println(fs.ModeDir)` prints `2147483648` instead of `d---------`. Found while fixing [#944](https://github.com/podhmo/minigo/issues/944).
- [x] **a nil host pointer coming back through `any` reads as untyped nil**: a typed nil `*T` stored in a host container and read back as `any` (`sync.Map` `Load`, `context.Value`) arrives as `NIL`, so `v == nil` is true where go reports false (the interface holds a dynamic type). The `any`-shaped return path does not keep the typed nil the way `goValueOf` now does for a `*T` result. Found in the #962 review.
- [x] **`atomic.Uint64` methods reject constants above MaxInt64**: `var u atomic.Uint64; u.Store(1<<64 - 1)` (and `u.CompareAndSwap(1<<64-1, 7)`) trap `constant 18446744073709551615 overflows int` (go: `true 7`). The host method's arguments are converted at `int` instead of the parameter's `uint64`. Found in the #961 follow-up review.
- [x] **`time.Weekday` constants are unbound**: `time.Monday` / `time.Saturday` trap `undefined: time.Monday` (go prints `Monday Saturday`), though `Time.Weekday()` itself works. Found fixing the min/max host twin (`testdata/difffuzz/minmax_host_month` leaves Weekday out for this reason).
- [x] **slog `TextHandler` `ErrorContext` crashes**: logging through a TextHandler with `ErrorContext` traps `*[]uint8, not *buffer.Buffer`. Found reviewing stack #975.
- [x] **slog `JSONHandler` writes `"level":0`** (was: traps on every record — gone since `json.NewEncoder` is bound, #1023): `slog.New(slog.NewJSONHandler(w, nil)).Info("hi")` emits `"level":0` where go emits `"level":"INFO"`, so `slog.Level`'s `MarshalJSON` is not reached on that path. TextHandler works. Found in the gosimilarity re-verification ([docs/sketch/ja/experiment-gosimilarity-reverify.md](./docs/sketch/ja/experiment-gosimilarity-reverify.md) §3.3).
- [x] **map keys of a named basic type lose the name when printed or ranged**: `fmt.Println(map[time.Month]int{time.March: 3})` prints `map[3:3]` (go `map[March:3]`), `type K int` with a `String` method prints `map[1:1]` for `map[K]int{1: 1}` (go `map[k:1]`), and `for k := range m` yields `%T` `int` (go `time.Month` / `main.K`). Keys are stored as their bare payload; the `*runtime.Map` render (`intrinsics.go`) and range could re-tag them with the map's key typedef (`mapElemTyps`). Found fixing the `time.Weekday` binding (`testdata/difffuzz/time_weekday_bound` avoids printing the whole map).
- [x] **`t.Add(time.Duration(5))` traps `Add arg 0: cannot use *runtime.UConst as time.Duration`**: a conversion of an untyped constant to a bound host scalar keeps its `UConst` payload, and the host-method argument path does not materialize it (go prints `5` for `.Nanosecond()`). `t.Add(time.Second)`, `t.Add(3*time.Hour)` and `time.Date(..., time.Month(7), ...)` work. Present on main both before and after #1014. Found while rebasing #1011.
- [x] **script values lose their Go type across a host `any` parameter**: basics and slices/arrays/maps of basics, bound host types and `any` now cross at their Go type. What remains is a design limit (script-declared types cannot exist as Go types), documented in README.md's "Currently unsupported".
- [x] **unbound stdlib members met running gosimilarity under minigo** (realworld candidate): `runtime.AddCleanup` (flagx calls it per FlagSet — a Go 1.24+ API showing up in libraries) and `exec.CommandContext` (`exec.Command` is bound). `json.NewEncoder` is already tracked. With a ~60-line flag shim and fnv-for-maphash, gosimilarity ran end-to-end on a 659-function target and reproduced the native scan's 110 pairs, ~150x slower.
- [x] **IMPORTANT — `json.Marshal` emits unexported struct fields** (private data leaks, silently): `json.Marshal(A{Name: "n", body: "secret"})` with an unexported `body string` gives `{"name":"n","body":"secret",...}` where go gives `{"name":"n"}`; `json.NewEncoder(w).Encode` shares the path (`goJSON`). Found via gosimilarity's `--format json` printing its private `tree`/`body` fields ([docs/sketch/ja/experiment-gosimilarity-reverify.md](./docs/sketch/ja/experiment-gosimilarity-reverify.md) §3.1).
- [x] **an escaped range-over-func `yield` called after the loop finished corrupts a live frame** (#1024 made an existing bug worse): go panics `range function continued iteration after whole loop exit`; before #1024 minigo silently re-ran the loop body, and now the yield closure pushes onto the loop's frame that the pool already handed to another call (unrecoverable nil deref / index -1). `driveFuncIter` sets `it.Exited` only on early exit — set it after the producer returns normally too. ([docs/sketch/ja/experiment-gosimilarity-reverify.md](./docs/sketch/ja/experiment-gosimilarity-reverify.md) §3.4)
- [x] **`json.Encoder` / reflected host-call leftovers** (found reviewing #1023; `Encode` itself now takes raw script args like `json.Marshal`). ([docs/sketch/ja/experiment-gosimilarity-reverify.md](./docs/sketch/ja/experiment-gosimilarity-reverify.md) §3.4)
- [x] `json.Encoder` / reflected host-call leftovers > **reflected host calls write host maps back into script slices**: after `text/template` `Execute(w, rows)` (and any reflected host method taking `[]map[...]`) a caller's `[]map[string]any` holds `*runtime.GoValue` maps and `rows[0]["a"]` traps `index on *runtime.GoValue`. `callReflectFunc`'s write-back replaces elements whose own kind is `Map` with `goValueOf(ev)`; it should skip them like it skips `any` elements holding containers.
- [x] `json.Encoder` / reflected host-call leftovers > **json details**: the cycle error lacks go's ` via <type>` suffix (`errJSONCycle`; the #1023 tests pin the short text); `%T` of an encoder prints `*minigo.jsonEncoder` (go `*json.Encoder`) and of `runtime.AddCleanup`'s result `minigo.cleanupHandle` (go `runtime.Cleanup`); an empty-name tag (`json:",omitempty"`) drops its options in `jsonFieldKey`.

- [x] Data-structure / algorithm-level performance candidates > **builtin calls pay a heap `defer` and an args copy each**: `VM.call`'s `*runtime.BuiltinFunc` arm sits inside `for {}`, so its `defer func(){...}()` is never open-coded (2.9 GB), and #1024 added `args = append([]runtime.Value{}, args...)` for every builtin (3.0 GB) though only arg-retaining builtins (`task.F`) need it. `len`/`make`/`math.Min` alone are ~120M calls on the gosimilarity scan; also consider a dedicated opcode for unshadowed `len`/`cap`. ([docs/sketch/ja/experiment-gosimilarity-reverify.md](./docs/sketch/ja/experiment-gosimilarity-reverify.md) §2.2) Script profile (`--scriptprofile`, reverify §6.7): the builtin-calling lines of gosimilarity's `distance.func1` take ~55% of the run, and the leaf early-return path (`len` ×2 + `math.Min`) alone ~24% — this lever outranks the cells reuse.
- [x] **flaky: `TestDiffRegressions/host_method_typed_const_arg` hangs ~30s intermittently** (main too; 1/15 to 8/25 runs): `Timer.Reset(1ns)` fired the real timer before `chanFeed.arm` closed the old `done`; the superseded pump held the only fire and `select` picked `done` at random, dropping it. The pump now prefers delivery over `done`; pinned as `testdata/difffuzz/timer_reset_tiny_fire`. ([docs/sketch/ja/experiment-gosimilarity-refactoring.md](./docs/sketch/ja/experiment-gosimilarity-refactoring.md))
- [x] **timer/ticker `Stop`/`Reset` leave a stale fire in the proxy channel**: since go1.23 no value sent before `Stop`/`Reset` is receivable afterwards, and `Timer.Stop` returns true for a fired-but-unreceived timer. minigo's `chanFeed` keeps the old value in its 1-buffer (`select { case <-tm.C: }` after `Reset(time.Hour)` sees it; go sees none) and `Stop` returns false. Fix direction: retire the pump and drain `C` inside `Stop`/`Reset`, counting a drained/dropped value as pending. Done: `chanFeed.stop`/`reset` retire the pump and wait for it before touching the real timer, then drain `C`; pinned as `testdata/difffuzz/timer_stop_reset_stale`.

- [x] **Preserve deferred operand types at binding sites**: `gate_rangedeferred`, `gate_recvdeferred`, and `gate_binddeferred` enforce the pinned diagnostics in both normal and strict conformance.
- [x] **a defined type over a host struct does not promote methods declared on the host's anonymous fields**: for `type Wrap Base` where the host `Base` embeds e.g. `*parse.Tree`, gc promotes Tree's methods into Wrap's method set (the promotion goes through the anonymous field, not Base's method set), but `hostFieldDepths`/the old `hostMemberInner(methods=false)` only count fields — `struct{ Wrap }` then misses `s.M()` for M declared on the embedded host field type. The method-set walk has the same gap it inherited. Found during M4-2c review fixes. → `hostPromotedMethodInner` probes each anonymous host field's method set and the defined-type walk contests promoted members at their embed depth, for both the selector and interface-satisfaction paths; pinned by `TestHostDefinedTypePromotedMethods`.
- [x] **unexported interface methods are matched by name only, ignoring declaring package**: `TypeDef.MReqs` and method sets are both `map[string]bool`-style name sets, so a script type's unexported `m()` can satisfy an interface requirement `m()` declared in a different package — in Go, unexported method names are package-scoped and never match across packages. Narrow in practice (script code is all `main`, host method sets only carry exported names), but the model cannot express the distinction until requirements record `(pkg, name)`. Found during M4-2b diagnostics work. → unexported names now carry a pkg-qualified key (`pkg.Path + "." + name`) in both requirement sets and method sets, while the plain name stays for member selection; same- vs cross-package satisfaction pinned by `TestIfaceUnexportedMethodPkg`.
- [x] **unverified impact: the global `templateFuncMap` TypeDef keeps the first engine's package** — resolved by P2-12: the typedef moved into `sessionState` (installbound.go `Engine.templateFuncMap`), so each session owns its own `*runtime.TypeDef` and `td.Pkg` names that session's bound package. Ownership pinned by `TestTemplateFuncMapPerSession`.: it is a package-level `*runtime.TypeDef` shared by text/template and html/template in every engine, and `Bind` sets `td.Pkg` only when nil (minigo.go:787), so later engines see the first engine's `*runtime.Package`. ([docs/sketch/spike-rearchitecture.md](./docs/sketch/spike-rearchitecture.md) S6)
- [x] **a lazy stdlib install could overwrite an explicit `Bind` landing mid-install** (reported on [#1149](https://github.com/podhmo/minigo/pull/1149)'s review; fixed in [#1150](https://github.com/podhmo/minigo/pull/1150)): the installers called `Engine.Bind`, whose publish is unconditional — pausing a first-use build via `WithHostPolicy` and running `Bind` in the window lost `Explicit` to the default table. `Bind` now splits into `bindTable` (outside `e.mu`) and `bindPublishLocked` (check+publish in one critical section), and every installer binds through `bindBound`'s publish-time bound re-check.
- [x] **a typed-nil script argument could not reach a nilable parameter of a reflect-wrapped builtin**: `toNative` only treated `runtime.NIL` as nil, so `strconv.AppendInt(nil, i, 10)` spelled `*runtime.TypedNil` and failed `cannot use *runtime.TypedNil as []uint8` — which also made `expvar.Int.String()` (it calls `AppendInt(nil, …)`) silently fall back to a raw struct dump under `fmt.Println`. `toNative` now maps `*runtime.TypedNil` to the declared type's zero for pointer/slice/map/chan/func/interface params. Found while generating `strconv`'s bound table (P2-13).
- [x] **data race on a VM's process pointer between a host callback and the run's end**: a host callback on a foreign goroutine (`context.AfterFunc` fired by `cancel()` just before the run returns) read `v.proc` without `callMu` in `callBoundedCatch` while the owner's `ReleaseProc` cleared it; CI's `race-test` caught it on #1054. `VM.proc` is now an `atomic.Pointer[proc]`, read once per check-then-use. Pinned by `TestHostCallbackRacesProcRelease`.
- [x] **the array-length fold table leaked an entry per non-canonical length site per load**: `runtime.arrayFolds` was a process-global `sync.Map` keyed by `*ast.ArrayType` that never shrank, so a host building a fresh Engine per request (`examples/storybook` `Book.Render`) kept every loaded `[N]T`/`[...]T` node alive; it is now keyed by `weak.Pointer[ast.ArrayType]` and a cleanup attached to the node deletes the entry with the AST.
- [x] **data race on a shared array typedef between script goroutines**: the interpreter folded each evaluated array length into the shared type AST in place (`foldArrLen`, `OpFoldArrayLen`, the `[...]T` literal), racing with every goroutine spelling or comparing the type (and tearing the `ast.Expr` interface, so `constant.MakeFromLiteral` could see an empty literal); folds now go through `runtime.FoldArrayLen` into a side table read via `ArrayLenExpr`/`FoldedArrayLen`, so the AST is never written after parsing and a canonical `[3]T` literal never touches the table.
- [x] **data race on a shared typedef's lazy field cache between script goroutines**: `Engine.anonLeafSpelling` copied the whole `*TypeDef` (`ctx := *td`) to get a mutable `LocalTypes` map, which read the `fieldTypes`/`ifaceReqs`/`ifaceSigs`/`hostType`/`typeTag` `atomic.Value`s and the `memo` atomic pointer non-atomically while a concurrent `fieldTypedefs` populated them via `TypeDef.SetFieldTypes` — CI's `race-test` caught it on `TestArrayTypedefConcurrent`. The ctx now comes from `TypeDef.CloneUncached` (immutable fields only, caches dropped) plus a fresh `LocalTypes` map.
- [x] **a function-local struct type with an array field sized by a function-local const has a zero-length field** (also wrong on main; found while writing the array-typedef race repro): `func main() { const k = 4; type loc struct{ A [k]int }; var l loc; l.A[1] = 5 }` panics `index out of range [1] with length 0` where go runs it; the field is zeroed through `arrayLen`, which likely cannot see a const declared in the enclosing function body (a `var a [k]int` in the same function works because the compiler evaluates `k` in scope and emits `OpFoldArrayLen`).
- [x] **a named map converted to an unnamed map type forks the map header**: `Map.Header` (used by `stampContainerTyp`) copied the order slices, so inserts, deletes and `clear` through one header were invisible to the other (ghost `a:<nil>` entries); a map's contents now live in one pointer-held store that every header shares, with only `Typ`/`IfaceKey` per header (`runtime.NewMap` builds one, `Map.Clone` copies one).
- [x] `inspect.Expr` attribution gaps found reviewing #924 > **other path-tail readers of an import's name**: `inspect.Import.Name` and `vet.go`'s stub-call import map now fall back to the located package clause (`runtime.ImportRef.PackageName`) for unaliased imports like `SymbolID`/`UsedSymbols` already did, so `vet` catches a stub call through `yaml` of `gopkg.in/yaml.v3` (pinned by `testdata/vetstub2` declaring `package vetstub`). Unverified: a host-bound package whose path tail is not its name (`Bind("gopkg.in/yaml.v3", ...)`) gets `Name` `yaml.v3`, so the VM's real-name fallback likely cannot bind the bare `yaml` either.
- [x] `inspect.Expr` attribution gaps found reviewing #924 > **residual: composite-literal keys of an imported or type-parameter literal type stay unresolved**: both resolved. A generic instantiation's element/key typed by a decl type parameter substitutes the call site's argument (`Pair[string, map[string]int]{k: {KeyA: 1}}` resolves inner `KeyA`). An imported type resolves through `ImportRef.Peek` — only when the package is already materialized, so inspect never loads a package to classify (`url.Values` resolves, a cold `http.Header` still reports nil; under-resolution preserved).
- [x] `inspect.Expr` attribution gaps found reviewing #924 > **`vet.go` stub-call detection uses a per-decl shadow set**: verified — a funclit param named like the import hid real stub calls across the decl. `scanStubCalls` now asks `syntax.LocalScopes.At(name, pos)`, so a call is skipped only where a local binding is actually in scope (`funclitShadow` in `testdata/vetmain/other.go` pins it).
- [x] `inspect.Expr` attribution gaps found reviewing #924 > **unverified: cost of the func-wide "possibly local" model**: verified and replaced — `Expr`/`TypeExpr`/`Node` anchor the file's `syntax.LocalScopes` instead of the func-wide `localNames` set, so attribution refuses only where a local binding is actually in scope. Pinned divergences the func-wide model hid: a `strings` var in one block no longer shadows `strings.TrimSpace` in a sibling (`ShadowBlock`), and a sibling-block var no longer hides the package map type used as a literal type (`SiblingVarShadow`); in-scope shadows still refuse (`ShadowMe`, `SiblingScopes`).
- [x] `inspect.Expr` attribution gaps found reviewing #924 > **duplicate local-name collection: `inspect.localNames` vs `vet.go`'s `declaredNames`**: vet.go's `declaredNames` is gone — `scanStubCalls` runs on `syntax.LocalScopes` now. `inspect.localNames` stays: it feeds `Expr`'s "possibly local" refusal (a different consumer, see the item above) rather than a call-site scan.
- [x] `inspect.Expr` attribution gaps found reviewing #924 > **dot-imported names**: verified — an identifier brought in by `import . "pkg"` fell through the `Ident` branch of `SymbolID` and was attributed to the declaring package; it now reports no SymbolID (under-resolution, matching the shadowing rule) when the file has a dot import and no package member claims the name.
- [x] **`inspect.MethodSet` drops members promoted from host-bound embeds it cannot read**: a struct embedding `*bytes.Buffer` or `io.Reader` now promotes the host type's reflect method set (`inspect/host_members.go` `hostMethodSpecs`, honoring the pointer-embed rule); interface embeds keep preferring the requirement AST when the binding carries one. `Implementers` sees the members too. Promoted host methods carry `Decl=nil` and `Via` = the embedded decl.
- [x] interface-typed keys and composite fields drop the dynamic type > **array keys with interface elements**: `map[[1]any]int{{M(2)}: 1}[[1]any{uint32(2)}]` is 1 where go gives 0 — `writeKeyElem` renders array elements untyped (no element typedef in `runtime`; struct keys use `TypeDef.CachedFieldTypes`). → array keys render each element through `typedScalarKeyOf` ([`writeArrayElems`]), so an element boxed by an interface-typed slot carries its dynamic type like `writeFieldsRepr` does for struct fields; pinned by `difffuzz/mapkey_array_iface_elem`.
- [x] **`errors.As(err, &ute)` with `*json.UnsupportedTypeError` is false** (also on main): `json.Marshal(make(chan int))` returns the host `*json.UnsupportedTypeError`, but a script `var ute *json.UnsupportedTypeError; errors.As(err, &ute)` reports false where go reports true.
- [x] **`iter.Pull` / `iter.Pull2` are broken for every seq** (also wrong on main): `next, stop := iter.Pull(slices.Values([]int{7, 8}))` returns `0 false` from the first `next()` and the second call panics `iter.Pull: next called again before yield`, where go prints `7 true`, `8 true`, `0 false`. Script seqs fail the same way, so it is the source-interpreted `iter.Pull` (coroutine switch) rather than the intrinsic seqs.
- [x] **unverified: `slices.SortFunc`/`SortStableFunc` order ties differently from go**: `sortByCmpFunc` uses `sort.SliceStable` for both, while go's `slices.SortFunc` is pdqsort (unstable for more than 12 elements), so elements comparing equal may come out in a different order than go prints. `slices.SortedFunc` (iterseq.go) already uses Go's own `slices.SortFunc`.
- [x] **maps seq helpers over host maps stay eager and untyped**: `maps.Keys`/`Values` of a host `map[any]any` (a `GoValue`, not a `runtime.Map`) fall back to `seqOf` over a snapshot with no `iter.Seq[K]` type, and `maps.All`/`Insert` reject it.
- [x] **unbound stdlib members met reviewing the host-twin fixes**: `slices.ContainsFunc`, `slices.Compare`, `slices.Replace`, `maps.EqualFunc` all trap `undefined:`. The comparing ones should reuse `VMCaller.Equal`/`lessScript` so host named basics keep meeting their source twins. (Bound together with `slices.CompactFunc`/`CompareFunc`/`IsSortedFunc`/`MaxFunc`/`MinFunc`.)
- [x] **Correct extract-schema plugin flag help**: the `-plugin` help string now advertises `func(*extschema.Config)` hooks, matching the hook-selection signature and the Go docs.
- [x] **Interpreter hot paths found by profiling realworld `grafana-openapi`** ([docs/sketch/ja/exploration-legacy-sync.md](./docs/sketch/ja/exploration-legacy-sync.md) §5.5; `go/parser` over grafana `pkg/api`, ~5.5s, 4.7 GB allocated):
- [x] Interpreter hot paths found by profiling realworld `grafana-openapi` > **frame allocation** (`prepFrame`, 34% of allocations; re-measured on main 7c6e1ed3: 0.70 of 2.09 GB): `&frame{}` itself is 367 MB (a ~190-byte struct per call), pre-filled `&Cell{NIL}` for non-param locals 190 MB, param cells 76 MB, the locals slice 54 MB. Pool frames, or carve them from a VM-owned arena; frames retained by `v.unwinding`/tracebacks must not be recycled while referenced. See the per-call items under the candidates entry below. Done: `framePool` recycles frame, `fr.locals`/`fr.ltyps`/`fr.slotRefs` and the boxed-cell block (`fr.cells`) is reused when `!chunkCellsEscape(ch)`; boxed slots share one `cells` block instead of a `&Cell{NIL}` per local (vm/call.go prepFrameSeen).
- [x] Interpreter hot paths found by profiling realworld `grafana-openapi` > **interface satisfaction walks the dynamic method set twice**: `ifaceSatisfied` calls `MethodSetOf` and then `MethodFuncsOf`, and each runs a full `methodInfoOfValue` → `methodWalkU` (~160 MB on grafana-openapi earlier; 359 MB, 17% of allocations, on main 7c6e1ed3). Resolved by `MethodInfoOf` (M4-2a): one `methodWalkU` pass returns names, unsure and signature-bearing funcs, and `ifaceSatisfiedUncached`/the missing-method diagnostic both read that single set.
- [x] Interpreter hot paths found by profiling realworld `grafana-openapi` > **#485's selector lowering costs ~0.2 s on grafana-openapi**: every `x.f` with a var base now emits a storage ref (`refTargetBase`), so plain field reads allocate `FieldRef` chains and recurse through cells in `selectMember`. Done: the cell verdict is tri-state (cellYes/cellNo/cellUnknown), so a select whose member provably cannot bind the operand's storage — a field, a value-receiver method call or method value, interface dispatch, a pointer operand — compiles the base to its value; only pointer-receiver and unprovable members keep the ref. Element selects (`s[i].f`) keep OpIndexRef — a value read deep-copies the element, breaking nested-ref writes, `[:]` on array fields, and promoted pointer methods. ZZSel microbench (300k field reads + value calls + one ptr call): −10% allocs, −10% alloc space.
- [x] Data-structure / algorithm-level performance candidates > **operand stack grows from empty on every call** (`frame.push`, 244 MB on grafana-openapi): record the chunk's max stack depth at compile time and allocate once (or slice a VM-owned stack). Toward the shared contiguous value stack listed under the last item. Done: `frame.push` already slices the VM-owned `v.vals` arena (each frame's operands are a window `vals[base:base+sp]`); a memprofile of the append bench shows no `frame.push` allocations — only growth copies of the persistent arena remain.
- [x] Data-structure / algorithm-level performance candidates > **global resolution cost per `OpGlobal`**: `resolveGlobalE` calls `fileOf` (`Fset.PositionFor` + filename map) and then `Scopes` + a mutex-guarded `Env.Get` on every access (~5.6% cum CPU on grafana-openapi; `fileOf` itself ~0.9%). An inline cache needs invalidation — `Globals` entries are replaced/deleted by the REPL (`repl/repl.go`, `repl/load.go`), `OpNewGlobal`, and lazy materialization — e.g. an `Env` generation counter; the file must come from a per-instruction file id or resolve descriptor, since `__init__` chunks span several files.
- [x] Data-structure / algorithm-level performance candidates > **generic calls still pay per-call key spelling and type re-specialization** (`BenchmarkGenericCall`, 100k calls: plain ~38 ms, inferred `Id(i)` ~61 ms, partial `Pair[string]("k", i)` ~115 ms, `Box[int].Get()` ~140 ms): `inferCached`/`instantiateFunc` hit their caches but rebuild a string key (`InstKey` + `AppendTypeKey`) on every call — a per-call-site inline cache on the callee pointer and argument typedef pointers would skip it. A generic type's method re-instantiates its receiver type `Box[T]` in the prologue each call (`instantiate` → `specializeType` + `checkTArgs`, ~3.8 KB/call). Landed (#1301): the TypeDef arm of `instantiate` caches specializations per package on `SpecKey` (spec identity + TypeID spell of binds, args and — for function-local generics — outerTypeArgs; a REPL redeclaration mints a new spec) — `RunMethod` 334 → 132 ms/op, 696 → 34 MB/op, 3.2M → 1.7M allocs/op. Landed: a `genCallSlot` per-OpCall-site memo pins the callee + argument identity (const payload pointer, resolved static, or value typedef) and reuses the inferred instance, skipping inferKey spelling and the cache probe — `RunInferred` ~144 → ~105 ms/op, 17.6 → 12.0 MB/op, 1.3M → 1.0M allocs/op; `RunPartial` ~210–250 → ~174 ms/op, ~60 → 35 MB/op. Landed: the `OpInstantiate` site memo pins the (base, peeled type-args) pointer identity and replays the result — `RunExplicit` 126 → 96 ms/op, 20.0 → 12.8 MB/op; `RunMethod` (the per-call receiver `Box[T]` re-instantiation) 158 → 104 ms/op, 40.8 → 26.4 MB/op.
- [x] Data-structure / algorithm-level performance candidates > **`Map.Delete` is O(n)** (`runtime/value.go`): it scans and compacts `Order`/`Keys`, so n deletes cost O(n²). Done: a lazily built `canonical key → position` index (built at 64+ entries) locates the slot, which is tombstoned rather than shifted; the store compacts once dead slots reach half, walks compact on `BeginWalk` so a delete-then-reinsert is not re-produced (Go-legal), and every reader resolves live presence through `Pairs`. 10k-entry drain: 28.2 → 4.5 ms/op. Remaining: `CanonicalKey` still stringifies struct/array keys through `strings.Builder` on every lookup. Synthetic only: not visible on grafana-openapi.
- [x] Data-structure / algorithm-level performance candidates > **gosimilarity residuals** (alloc profile after the frame-pooling round, ~58 GB total): `prepFrameSeen` ~19 GB — mostly frames of chunks whose cells can escape (landed: every chunk now pools cells and flags `cellsLeaked` on publish; the BoundMethod receiver prepend is a VM scratch slice — `args = append([]Value{c.Recv}, args...)` is gone); `Walk.Container` ~3.8 GB (host-boundary `DeepUnwrap` output, semantically required); `hostHelpers.arity` `[]any` arg slice ~1.9 GB (landed: pooled `[]any` scratch behind `hostArgs`/`releaseHostArgs`); `Map.Insert`/`CanonicalKey` ~3 GB (script-map key interning; landed: `typeTagOf` memoized per typedef — its repr-side double call per key no longer respells; investigated further: interning cannot skip the content-addressed repr build — the key material must exist to hash it — and for scalar keys, which dominate the profile, `CanonicalKey` already passes through alloc-free, so the ~3 GB is inherent entry storage in `Pairs` buckets plus the parallel `Order`/`Keys` arrays; shrinking it means a fused `map[ck]struct{val, disp}` or a different ordered-map structure, not interning). Remaining levers are the later/larger ones above: a per-VM contiguous frame arena and shrinking `runtime.Cell`.
- [x] Data-structure / algorithm-level performance candidates > **`chunkCellsEscape` is true for nearly every chunk, so #1024's cell-block reuse rarely applies**: `OpLocalRef` is emitted not only for `&x` but for every store target (`x = y`, `a, b = b, a`, `x op= y`, the base of `s[i] = v`/`m[k] = v`, range targets) and for every variable used as a selector base (`x.f`, `x.M()` — the compiler cannot tell a pointer-receiver call from a field read). Landed: every chunk lays the pooled block out one cell per slot; `localCell` flags the frame `cellsLeaked` when it publishes a pooled cell (`&x`, a capture), freeFrame drops the block like `cellsShared`, and `OpNewLocal`/`OpRenewVar` overwrite an unpublished cell in place. `&x` now emits a publishable ref (B=1) — it previously read as transient and would have returned an unflagged pooled cell. On a never-capturing boxed-local microbench: 27.2 → 12.8 MB/op, 2.0M → 1.4M allocs/op. Residual: transient store-target refs still promote without flagging (truly transient by compiler proof). ([docs/sketch/ja/experiment-gosimilarity-reverify.md](./docs/sketch/ja/experiment-gosimilarity-reverify.md) §2.1)
- [x] Intrinsic coverage + element types > **residual: `Node` views rooted at non-func decls** (exploration-legacy-sync.md §10): done — `inspect.Spec(s)` / `SpecOf` roots a `Node` view at a var/const `ValueSpec` or a type `TypeSpec`, so const initializer exprs and type-spec internals get Kind/Role/Detail children and `Expr` anchoring like a func body.
- [x] **REPL-side package introspection** (user wish, PR #397 discussion; related: [docs/sketch/plan-test-detect.md](./docs/sketch/plan-test-detect.md)): done — `:imports <ref>` / `:importers <ref>` meta-commands (`repl/pkginfo.go`) answer the imports-only queries over `pkg/modulewalk`; ref resolves like `:ls` (session-import name, dir anchored at the session cwd, import path; empty = entered package), test-file imports get a ` (test)` suffix, and the answers are pure filesystem scans — nothing is loaded or interpreted.
- [x] **CLI parity leftovers (realworld gap verification)**: done in https://github.com/podhmo/minigo/pull/1314 — `minigo run a.go b.go` runs the file list as one program (`Engine.RunFiles`); `minigo help -h` prints usage.
- [x] **`syntax.ParseImports` / `syntax.ParsePackageClause`: light parse stages with narrow results** (go-scan reuse, [pre-todo](./docs/sketch/ja/exploration-goscan-reuse--pre-todo.md)): separate functions returning imports / package name only — never a mode option on `ParseFile`, so no truncated `*ast.File` can flow downstream into full-AST consumers (go-scan `ScanPackageFromFilePathImports` shape). Landed in `syntax/lightparse.go`; body syntax past the header is never scanned, a malformed header returns the error plus whatever was recovered.
- [x] **`TypeExpr` resolution-path recording** (go-scan `ResolutionPathKey`): record the chase path always-on as call-local state (one ctx put + per-hop append; nothing persists on symbols; no diagnostic mode) so cycle/failure errors can report where resolution walked. Landed in `internal/typedef/respath.go`: call-local `respath` records named-decl hops through the alias/underlying/ref chases; failures annotate `(resolving a.T -> b.U)` and revisit cycles error instead of truncating.
- [x] **`modulewalk` package** (verdict: adopted): imports-only layer enumerating packages and importers across nested `go.mod` — go-scan `modulewalker.go` + `discoverModules` shape (`PackageImports`, `Walk`/`Visitor`, `FindImporters`, `UnscannedGoFiles`). Type names `modulewalk.Walker`/`Visitor`; tooling layer, not interpreter. Depends on the `ParseImports` entry; answers "Repo-enumeration surface" and feeds REPL introspection. Landed as `pkg/modulewalk` over `resolve.ScanRepo` + `syntax.ParseImports`; `FindImportersAggressively` (git-grep shortcut) not ported.
- [x] **`astwalk` extraction — consumer-first** (verdict: adopted as direction): do not create the package empty — go-scan's `astwalk` was removed (#993) as an unused one-function package. Extract from the first real consumer (`inspect.UsedSymbolsOf`'s file walk, a task needing body traversal, or a host tool) in the shape that consumer needs. Bind path (`inspect` vs `minigo.dev/astwalk`) undecided. Landed as `pkg/astwalk` (`Each[N ast.Node]` — the typed-node iteration the inspect/vet/index walks repeated); consumers moved: `inspect.UsedSymbolsOf`/`CommentsOf`, `vet.scanStubCalls`, `index` local-gen numbering.
- [x] **`ImportManager` port** (go-scan `importmanager.go`): alias rules for generated code — keyword → `_pkg`, collision → numbered suffix, path-hash fallback, `Qualify` — as a shared host package (`pkg/importmanager`) plus a thin intrinsic. convert-define already carries an ad-hoc one (`examples/convert-define/generator/importmanager.go`); the port lifts it (reconciling rules with go-scan's) so gen-sync and scripts share it too. Landed as `pkg/importmanager` (`Manager`/`New`) + the `minigo.dev/importmanager` intrinsic; convert-define's ad-hoc copy was lifted onto it.
- [x] **ptr-receiver `MarshalText`/`MarshalJSON` on fields of an addressable parent is not seen** (residual gap found while fixing the TextMarshaler item): gc's `json.Marshal(&v)` sees ptr-receiver marshalers on `v`'s fields (`{"p":{"X":6}}` marshals as `"pt6"` when `(*T).MarshalText` exists); the bound codec probes the field's value method set only, so it misses them — the decoder's `prior` machinery tracks addressability but the marshal side has no equivalent.
- [x] **nested `UnmarshalJSON` receives the re-encoded literal, not the original byte span** (documented approximation in #715): fixed by the `jsonDom` rework — every node carries its raw span and objects keep ordered member pairs, so a nested `UnmarshalJSON` sees the original literal and `DisallowUnknownFields` checks every duplicate occurrence like gc.
- [x] **compile: map-literal keys read as field names under a type parameter**: with `const k = "kk"`, `func f[M ~map[string]int]() M { return M{k: 1} }` and `[]E{{k: 2}}` (`E ~map[string]int`) store the key `"k"` — go prints `map[kk:1]`. `unfoldTypExpr` treats a bound type parameter as opaque, so `litTypeAt` (`compile/compile.go`) cannot see the map shape; the bound `*runtime.TypeDef` in `c.binds` (or the constraint's core type) knows it, but its `Anon` AST is scoped to the instantiating file. Follow-up of the litkey_named_peel fix.
- [x] **`%T` / `reflect.TypeOf` of an unnamed host slice misspell it**: `es, _ := os.ReadDir("."); fmt.Printf("%T", es)` printed `interface{}`/`[]*os.unixDirent` under minigo where go prints `[]fs.DirEntry`; a host `[]ast.Stmt` printed `[]Stmt`. Fixed: host slice adapters tag the slice typedef with the element's host identity (`hostbridge.HostSliceTypOf`) and `DisplayName` spells slices/chans by `DisplayName(td.Elem)` when the element carries `HostIdent` — maps keep the anon spelling for the key. Found while fixing [#939](https://github.com/podhmo/minigo/issues/939).
- [x] **host pointer deref, host field writes and host slice element writes**: `*f.Name` / `*strings.NewReader("abc")` trap `deref of non-pointer *runtime.GoValue`; `ce.Fun = ast.NewIdent("x")` and `ce.Args = append(ce.Args, ...)` trap `cannot use *runtime.Struct as ast.Expr` (a script-built node cannot become a host field value); `ce.Args[0] = lit` is silently lost because `goValueOf` copies host slices. Found in the #939 review. (A boxed host map/slice element — `r.URL.Query()["tag"][0] = v` — now writes host backing via `VM.hostElemRef`; host struct fields still copy.) → landed: `*p` on a boxed host pointer resolves through `runtime.HostPtrRef` (reads box the pointee's storage, `*p = v` writes through; `Deref` stays ref-only so peel loops end on the GoValue leaf); `ce.Fun = ast.NewIdent(...)`, `ce.Args = append(ce.Args, ...)` and `ce.Args[0] = lit` all write the host backing — pinned by `testdata/difffuzz/host_ptr_deref` and `host_field_write`.
- [x] **`os.PathError`, `os.LinkError`, `os.SyscallError`, `os.NewSyscallError`, `os.Link` are unbound**: each traps `undefined: os.…`, so `errors.As(err, &le)` with `var le *os.LinkError` on an `os.Rename` error cannot be written. `os.PathError` must alias the source-interpreted `fs.PathError` (not a separate host type). A quick `hostType("os.LinkError", …)` binding builds the struct but `os.IsExist(&os.LinkError{Err: os.ErrExist})` stays false (the boxed `Err` field does not reach the host as `fs.ErrExist`). Found while fixing the script-built `*fs.PathError` item.
- [x] **host maps and arrays lose element identity like slices did**: `anonArrayTypX(anonExprOf(...))` in `goValueOf` and `anonMapTypX` in `SnapshotOf` spelled named elements bare and set no `TypeDef.Elem`; the map/array/slice unbox paths now share `HostMapTypOf`/`HostArrayTypOf`/`HostSliceTypOf` (elem carries `HostPkgElemTypedef`), `DisplayName` spells `map[`<anon key>`]T` and `[n]T` by `DisplayName(td.Elem)` like slices, and `hostElemKey` interns them by the same words — pinned by `difffuzz/fmt_t_hostmap` (`map[string]time.Duration`, `[3]time.Duration`). A named host KEY still spells bare — `TypeDef` has no key typedef slot.
- [x] **`bytes.Buffer.Bytes()` result does not alias the buffer**: `bs := buf.Bytes(); bs[0]++; buf.String()` prints the unmodified content under minigo (go shows the write). `goValueOf` copies a host `[]byte` result into a fresh script slice, so writes never reach the host backing; same shape as the host slice element-write item above. Found while reviewing the host `[]byte` perf stack. → landed: the unbox arms (`[]byte`/`[]string`/`[]any` and the unnamed-slice fallback) carry the backing as `Slice.Host`; `SetElem` commits scalar stores to it and reslices/`copy`/index refs share it — pinned by `difffuzz/host_slice_alias`.
- [x] **a struct embedding a host struct is not an io.Writer for the io helpers**: `type T struct{ bytes.Buffer }` (also `*bytes.Buffer`, `strings.Builder`) passed as `io.Copy(&t, r)` / `fmt.Fprint(&t, …)` traps `not an io.Writer: *runtime.Struct`, where go promotes `Write` from the embed. `asWriterVM` (`intrinsics.go`) looks up `Write` through `runtime.IfaceMember`, which misses methods promoted from a host-bound embed. A related case: a struct embedding `strings.Builder` that overrides `Write` panics `strings.Reader.WriteTo: invalid WriteString count` in `io.Copy` (the promoted `WriteString` path). Found while fixing the io.Copy host-box path ([#950](https://github.com/podhmo/minigo/issues/950), [#955](https://github.com/podhmo/minigo/pull/955)).
- [x] **`minigo vet` hint: `os.ReadFile` used only as text** ([#950](https://github.com/podhmo/minigo/issues/950)): flag `b, err := os.ReadFile(p)` whose `b` is only passed to `string(...)` (or `len`) and suggest `io.Copy` into a `strings.Builder`, which stays on the host (README "Performance notes"). Today `vet` only reports unregistered stub calls (`vet.go`); this would be its first performance hint, aimed at agents that run `vet` before `run`.
- [x] **a panic inside a script `Write`/`Read` called from a host io helper becomes an error**: `scriptWriter.Write` / `scriptReader.Read` (`intrinsics.go`) return the script panic as an error, so `io.Copy` returns `err="panic: boom"` and execution continues where go panics. They should re-panic like `callOrPanic`. Found in the #955 review.
- [x] **io helper edge cases found in the #955 review**: `json.Indent`/`Compact` and `bufio.NewReadWriter` are unbound.
- [x] **`errors.As` into an `*os.PathError` target misses**: `_, err := os.Open(missing); var pe *os.PathError; errors.As(err, &pe)` reports `false` and leaves `pe` nil (go: `true`, `pe != nil`), while the same call with `var pe *fs.PathError` works. The `errors.As` intrinsic (`findAsTarget`, `intrinsics.go`) does not see the script's `os.PathError` (an alias of the source-interpreted `fs.PathError`) as the host `*fs.PathError` in the chain. Found while fixing typed nil host pointers.
- [x] **`os.FileMode` is undefined**: bound `os.FileMode` as a `KindAlias` typedef to `io/fs.FileMode` (same shape as `os.PathError`'s alias); the interner resolves the bound alias to the canonical fs typedef, so `namedIdentOf` now reads a member typedef's qualified name as its identity — `os.FileMode(0755) == fs.FileMode(0755)` passes both the compile gate and run-time compare. Pinned in `difffuzz/os_filemode_alias`.
- [x] **non-constant shift: non-modular ops between two untyped operands settle at int**: `/`, `%`, `>>` (and ops stacked behind them, unary `-`/`^`) now ride a Wrap value as deferred `WrapOps` that replay at the materialization width instead of settling at int — `var x int8 = (1<<n) / 3` prints -42 like go. Comparisons still settle at int, which is gc's rule (both sides of `==` with no typed context default to int). Pinned in `difffuzz/wrap_deferred_ops`.
- [x] **typed float constant expressions don't round to their type at each step**: the typed exact-domain fold (`constInKind` converts the kind, never rounds) keeps arbitrary precision where gc rounds every typed constant operation to the type — `float64(float32(1)/3)` prints 0.3333333333333333 (go 0.3333333432674408), `float32(0.1)+float32(0.2) == 0.3` is false (go true), `1e300*float64(10)/1e10` prints 1e+291 (go 1.0000000000000001e+291). Pre-existing; found in the #961 review. Fixed: `constInKind` reads operands in the named float/complex domain (float32/64, complex64/128 round via Float32Val/Float64Val) and the typed fold tags `roundConstInKind` on the folded result, so every step rounds like gc; pinned by `difffuzz/tconst_float_opround`.
- [x] **`int(c)` / `float64(c)` / `string(c)` / `bool(c)` / `complex128(c)` drop the type**: `convertConst` returns the bare untyped constant when the target is the constant's own default type, so `int(5)/2.0` prints 2.5 (go 2). Tagging it `Named{int, UConst}` breaks `sync.Map` keys (`opaqueKeyOf` keys a Named int apart from a bare int64), so typed const decls of these five types still bind through the var coerce instead of the `T(e)` conversion other typed consts now use. Found in the #961 follow-up review.
- [x] **a comparison's result is typed `bool` in the operand gate**: Go's comparison yields an untyped bool, but `staticOpTyp` types `x < y` (non-constant operands) as `bool`, so `type B bool; var w B; w == (x < y)` traps `mismatched types B and bool` (go: true). The same gate rejects a logical op: `var v B = true; w := v && x > 1` traps the same way (go: `true main.B`). Found in the #961 follow-up review.
- [x] **`reflect.DeepEqual` on a host scalar named type against its conversion**: `reflect.DeepEqual(time.Second, time.Duration(1e9))` is false (go: true); `time.Second == time.Duration(1e9)` is fine. `deepEql` (`intrinsics.go`) falls back to `reflect.DeepEqual(goNative(a), goNative(b))`, and the two sides do not marshal to the same host `time.Duration`. Found while fixing DeepEqual on boxed typed nils.
- [x] **a non-nil host named slice stays a box for indexing and slicing**: a `net.IP` from `net.ParseIP("1.2.3.4")` is a `GoValue` (kept boxed for method dispatch), so `ip[3]`, `ip.To4()[3]`, `ip[:2]` and `for i, b := range ip` trap `index on *runtime.GoValue` (go: indexes the bytes). `len`/`cap` measure the host slice since the nil-net.IP fix; index/slice/range need the same reflect fallback (or an unboxed slice that keeps the host methods). Nil named host maps are not typed nils yet either (`goValueOf` mints `HostNil` only for named slices). Found while fixing nil `net.IP`. Plain and comma-ok index on a boxed host map/slice (`ip[3]`, `r.URL.Query()["tag"]`) now reads through reflect (`VM.hostIndex`, see docs/sketch/ja/experiment-storybook.md); slicing, range, index writes, `delete` and `clear` followed (`VM.hostSlice` / `hostSetIndex`, iterator kind 'h'). The nil-named-host-map part is split into its own entry.
- [x] **a nil named host map is not a typed nil**: `goValueOf` mints `HostNil` only for named slices, so a nil `url.Values` / `http.Header` from a host call stays a box : `httptest.NewRequest("GET", "/?a=1", nil).Form == nil` and `r.Trailer == nil` are false (go: true), as is a host-handed `net.IP(nil) == nil`; `==` on a GoValue does not consult `rv.IsNil()` (`hostNilEmbed` does). index/range/delete/len on it go through reflect fine. Split from the net.IP indexing entry; confirmed in the #1049 review.
- [x] **boxed host named slices through append/copy/conversions/in-place helpers**: `append`, `copy`, `f(ip...)`, `[]byte(ip)` / `string(ip)` / `[4]byte(ip)` / `net.IP(b)`, `slices.*` (in-place ones write the host backing, `Clone`/`Delete` return a `net.IP`), `sort.Slice` and script-side `net.IP{1, 2, 3, 4}` / `make(net.IP, 4)` now work on a boxed host named slice (`vm/hostslice.go`, `hostslice.go`, `testdata/hostnamed`). The aliasing a script copy cannot keep (`[]byte(ip)`, a callee writing its `...byte` parameter) is listed in README "Currently unsupported".
- [x] **nested writes into a boxed host map element bypass the host backing outside single assignment**: `x["A"][0], x["A"][1] = x["A"][1], x["A"][0]` on an `http.Header` is silently dropped (the multi-assign `pin` path in `compile/compile.go` reads `x["A"]` by value), as are `p := &x; (*p)["A"][0] = v` (the base after `refThrough` is a Cell, not the GoValue `hostElemRef` looks for) and `p := &x["A"][0]; *p = v`; `p := &ip[1]; *p += 1` traps `deref of non-pointer *runtime.DerefRef`. Single `x["A"][0] = v` works (`VM.hostElemRef`). Found in the #1049 review.
- [x] **a boxed host named string (`template.HTML`) has no operators**: `x + "s"`, `x < "s"` and `[]byte(x)` work on a boxed or script-tagged host named string; a sum stays a `template.HTML` (`binaryOp`, `unboxGoValue`, `testdata/hostnamed`).
- [x] **a read of an unnamed host slice is a script copy, so writes through the alias are lost**: on an `http.Header` handed in by the host, `s := h["A"]; s[0] = "v"`, `sort.Strings(h["A"])`, `slices.Reverse(h["A"])`, `copy(h["A"], src)` and `for _, v := range h { v[0] = "w" }` all change a script copy (go: the header's own backing); `h["A"] = append(h["A"], ...)` and element stores through the map (`h["A"][0] = v`, tuples, `&h["A"][0]`) do reach it. Root: `goValueOf` unboxes an unnamed host slice into a fresh script `Slice`, same family as the host-slice element-write and `bytes.Buffer.Bytes()` entries above. Found testing the nested-write fix. → landed: the unbox keeps the host backing in `Slice.Host` and every element-write path (`OpSetIndex`, `IndexRef.Set`, `copy`, range-temp stores) funnels through `SetElem`; `sort.Strings`/`slices.Reverse`/`copy` mutate the header like gc.
- [x] **conversion of a boxed host named map to another map type traps**: `map[string][]string(r.URL.Query())`, `url.Values(r.Trailer)` and `http.Header(r.Form)` trap `cannot convert url.Values to map[string][]string` / `cannot convert http.Header to Values` (nil or not; go converts). The boxed named slice conversions are in the host named slices entry above. Found testing the nil host map fix.
- [x] **`&r.Form` of a host struct field is not a host pointer**: `json.Unmarshal(data, &r.Form)` on a host-handed `*http.Request` fails `json: Unmarshal(nil)` (go fills the field); the address-of a host struct field reaches the host call as nil. Related to the host field writes entry above. Found testing the nil host map fix.
- [x] **`&s[i]` with an out-of-range index does not panic at the address-of**: `p := &s[5]` on `[]int{1}` runs on (go: `index out of range [5] with length 1` at that statement) because `OpIndexRef` defers the bounds check to the first use of `*p`; the same holds for a boxed host slice (`&h["nope"][0]`, `&ip[9]`). Found testing the nested-write fix.
- [x] **assigning into or ranging over a boxed host named map traps**: `w.Header()["X-A"] = v` and `q := r.URL.Query(); q["z"] = v` trap `index assign on *runtime.GoValue`; `for k, v := range r.URL.Query()` traps `range over *runtime.GoValue` (go: writes the header / iterates the values). Reads go through `VM.hostIndex` now; `setIndex` and range need the same reflect fallback. A `url.Values` from a script call to `url.ParseQuery` does not reproduce it — take the map from a host method (`r.URL.Query()`, `w.Header()`). Found in the storybook experiment (docs/sketch/ja/experiment-storybook.md).
- [x] **unbound host members found in the #962 follow-up**: `net.ParseMAC`, `net.IPMask`, `time.LoadLocation` and `time.Tick` trap `undefined: …` under the host-bound `net` / `time` packages. Found while fixing nil host values.
- [x] **a script struct embedding a host pointer does not satisfy interfaces through promoted methods**: `type F struct{ *os.File }; var c interface{ Name() string } = F{os.Stdout}` traps `cannot use main.F as interface{}` (go: ok), though `F{os.Stdout}.Name()` itself works. The method-set walk (`typeMethodInfoU`, `dispatch.go`) does not promote the host embed's reflect methods. Found in the #962 review. Blocks `multipart.FileHeader.Open` from returning its `sectionReadCloser` (embeds host `*io.SectionReader` + `io.Closer`) as `multipart.File` — `cannot use multipart.sectionReadCloser as File` (`testdata/difffuzz/multipart_fileheader_open` PENDING).
- [x] **`%T` of a host slice of package types drops the package**: an unboxed host slice whose element is (a pointer to) a package type, read from an `inspect.AST` node, prints `[]*Ident` / `[]Expr` under `%T` (go: `[]*ast.Ident` / `[]ast.Expr`) — its typedef's spelling comes from `anonExprOf`, which spells named elements bare. Relatedly `%T` of `os.Stdout` prints `*minigo.engineStdout` (go: `*os.File`). Found in the #962 review. The slice side was covered by HostSliceTypOf; `os.Stdout` now spells `*os.File` via APITypeBox.
- [x] **leftovers from the #962 follow-up review** (record only): a dot import `. "regexp"` with `b.(*Regexp)` does not match the host `*regexp.Regexp`; `reflect.DeepEqual` of two separate `os.Open` errors for the same path is false (go: true — equal `*fs.PathError` contents); a zero `regexp.Regexp` struct value traps on `String()` / deref (go: `""`); `[]byte(ip)` on a `net.IP` traps (the box does not convert). Found in the #980 review. All four verified fixed: dot-imported *Regexp asserts, DeepEqual of PathErrors, zero Regexp.String, and []byte(net.IP) all match gc.
- [x] **`unsafe.Sizeof(f())` evaluates its operand and types the result `int`**: `s := unsafe.Sizeof(f())` runs `f` (go never evaluates a Sizeof operand) and `%T` prints `int` (go `uintptr`). Found in the #961 follow-up review.
- [x] **`max`/`min` ignore a NaN that is not the first operand**: `max(1.0, math.NaN())` and `min(1.0, math.NaN())` print 1 (go `NaN`: any NaN operand makes the result NaN). Found in the #961 follow-up review.
- [x] **`max`/`min` of a time.Duration var and an untyped constant print the raw number**: `d := 3*time.Second; fmt.Println(max(d, 5000000000))` prints `5000000000` (go `5s`) — the constant operand's result does not take the Duration type. Found in the #961 follow-up review.
- [x] **`atomic.AddInt32((*int32)(&cv), 2)` retypes a named int32**: with `type C int32; var cv C = 1`, after the call `%T` of `cv` prints `int32` (go `main.C`): the store through the converted pointer writes a plain int32 into the `C` cell. Found in the #961 follow-up review.
- [x] **generic inference through a `~*E` core type**: `func zero[E any, PE interface{ ~*E }](p PE) E`; `zero(&x)` traps `type argument type does not satisfy constraint interface{}` (go infers E=int from PE=*int and prints 5). Found in the #961 follow-up review.
- [x] **`slices.Index([]uint8{}, 300)` runs instead of failing to compile**: it prints -1 where gc rejects `cannot use 300 (untyped int constant) as uint8 value ... (overflows)`; the intrinsic does not convert an untyped constant argument to the element type. Found in the #961 follow-up review.
- [x] **`math.Sincos` is undefined**: `s, c := math.Sincos(0)` traps `undefined: math.Sincos` (go `0 1`). Found in the #961 follow-up review.
- [x] **`*os.File.Name()` and `os.MkdirTemp`/`CreateTemp` results spell the absolute host path**: after `os.Chdir(d)`, `os.Open("f.txt")` gives `f.Name()` `/abs/d/f.txt` (go: `f.txt`), so errors from the file (`read f.txt: file already closed`) also show the absolute path; `os.MkdirTemp("sub", "x")` returns `/abs/.../sub/x123` (go: `sub/x123`). The bound ops open `e.fsPath(p)`; the path-error rewrite (`scriptPathErr`) only covers errors returned by the call itself. Found reviewing #963/#964.
- [x] **a host value satisfies an interface assertion by method name alone**: `fmt.Errorf("one %w", a).(interface{ Unwrap() []error })` succeeds (go: fails — `*fmt.wrapError` has `Unwrap() error`), and a multi-`%w` error asserts to `interface{ Unwrap() error }`. Host reflect methods carry no decl signature (`vm.Hooks.MethodFuncsOf`), so `ifaceSatisfied` compares names only; comparing the reflect method type against the interface member signature would close it. Found reviewing #964 (fmt.Errorf multi-%w).
- [x] **an unknown member of a bound package in a type position is silently accepted**: `var x *json.Nope` (gc: `undefined: json.Nope`) runs, and `errors.As(err, &x)` just reports false; only an expression-position use (`err.(*json.Nope)`) traps `undefined`. The pointer-typed zero resolves its element lazily, so the missing member is never looked up. Found reviewing #963/#964.
- an unknown member of a bound package in a type position is silently accepted > fixed: `checkTypeExprMembers` walks the type expression and traps `undefined: pkg.X` when an indexed package demonstrably lacks the type member — value-position sub-exprs (`[N]T` array lengths) are skipped so const members like `strconv.IntSize` are not misclassified. difffuzz `type_member_missing` (strict-lazy compile path does not run the check yet — `STRICT_PENDING` marker pinned).
- [x] **methods of `reflect.Value` invoked on a Value-of-Value dispatch on the box, not the boxed value**: `vv := reflect.ValueOf(v)` then `vv.MethodByName("IsValid").Call` reported the box's validity — true on a box of a zero Value (go false) — and `Int` on a box of an int panicked "call of reflect.Value.Int on ptr Value" (go: 42). `Method`/`MethodByName` bound the host methods on the outer host Value storing the facade; they now bind the facade's own methods so the call operates on the boxed value like gc. Found in the #1344 review.
- [x] **`os.IsNotExist` on a typed-nil `*fs.PathError` does not panic**: `var p *fs.PathError; os.IsNotExist(error(p))` panics in gc (nil dereference inside `underlyingError`), minigo returns false. `hostErrArg` boxes the typed nil as a scriptError instead of handing the host a real nil `*fs.PathError`. Found reviewing #963.
- [x] **`errors.As(err, &me)` with `me *json.MarshalerError` fails for a script MarshalJSON error**: the bound `json.Marshal` reports the failure as minigo's own `wrapError` (a real `*json.MarshalerError` needs a `reflect.Type` for the script type), and no `MarshalerError` typedef is bound. Found reviewing stack #975.
- [x] **`Engine.fsPath` cleaning changes os semantics**: `os.Stat("")` / `os.Chdir("")` succeed on the cwd (go: `stat : no such file or directory`), `os.Lstat("d/f.txt/")` on a file succeeds (go: `not a directory`), and `filepath.WalkDir("lnk/")` with a symlinked root does not descend (go follows the trailing slash). Also `filepath.EvalSymlinks("lnk/nope")` under a symlinked cwd (`/tmp` -> `/private/tmp`) reports the `/private/...` path (go: `lstat d/nope`): `scriptPathErr` only maps paths under the unresolved `ap`. Found reviewing stack #975.
- [x] **`fmt.Errorf` `%w` misuse prints `%!v`**: `fmt.Errorf("x %w", nil)` prints `x <nil>` (go: `x %!w(<nil>)`), a missing operand prints `%!v(MISSING)` (go: `%!w(MISSING)`), and a non-error operand prints its value (go: `%!w(int=3)`) — `rewriteWrapVerbs` turns `%w` into `%v` before host fmt sees it. Found reviewing stack #975.
- `fmt.Errorf` `%w` misuse prints `%!v` > fixed: a nil/non-error %w operand's slot carries the literal `%!w(<nil>)` / `%!w(TYPE=value)` marker for %v to print (`badWrapOperand`), and a missing operand's directive becomes `%[K]v` over an appended `%!w(MISSING)` (`rewriteWrapVerbsOps`). difffuzz `fmt_errorf_badwrap`.
- [x] **`slices` binding is missing exported funcs**: `CompactFunc`, `Compare`, `CompareFunc`, `ContainsFunc`, `IsSortedFunc`, `MaxFunc`, `MinFunc`, `Replace` trap `undefined: slices.X` (the curated `e.Bind("slices", …)` table in `intrinsics.go` shadows the whole package). `Grow`/`Clip` were added for log/slog; the iterator helpers (`All`, `Values`, `Backward`, `Collect`, `AppendSeq`, `Chunk`, `SortedFunc`, `SortedStableFunc`) in #1020. Found fixing the slog TextHandler crash.
- `slices` binding is missing exported funcs > fixed: the `slices` table in internal/stdlib/slices/slices.go already binds all eight (probe: CompactFunc/Compare/CompareFunc/ContainsFunc/IsSortedFunc/MaxFunc/MinFunc/Replace outputs match gc byte-for-byte).
- [x] **conversions between incompatible pointer types are not rejected statically**: `x := 1; _ = TL(&x)` (`type TL *time.Location`) and `TL(new(bytes.Buffer))` are accepted silently, while `BP(time.UTC)` (`type BP *bytes.Buffer`) and `(*strings.Reader)(p)` with `p *bytes.Buffer` fail only at run time; gc rejects all four at compile time. Found reviewing #1013.
- conversions between incompatible pointer types are not rejected statically > fixed: `convertPointer` now rejects a `(*T)(p)` whose pointee's underlying type differs from T's — an untagged source (bare `&x` cell or untagged host box) compares the pointee value's type spelling against T's `underlyingShape` unless it is a host value of T itself, and a tagged pointee compares host typedefs by `HostType` (two host structs' empty `Fields` lists made `StructFieldsEq` vacuously true). Pinned by `PtrConvDeclBad`/`PtrConvHostBad` in testdata/fuzzfix; the traps fire at conversion time — a compile-time rejection needs a different mechanism.
- [x] **`append` growth capacity diverges for elements not 16 bytes wide**: the append builtin grows the host `[]runtime.Value` (16-byte elements), so size-class rounding differs from gc: `cap(append(make([]byte, 3), make([]byte, 4)...))` is 7 (go: 8), and `cap(append(make([]byte, 0, 3), 1, 2, 3, 4))` is 6 (go: 32). `slices.Grow` shares this path. Growth should round by the element type's size. Found fixing `slices.Grow` (#1013).
- `append` growth capacity diverges for elements not 16 bytes wide > fixed: `runtime.GrowSliceCap` ports gc's nextslicecap + roundupsize size classes measured against the script element type's byte size, plus the 32-byte stack-buffer cap a small non-spread append on an empty slice takes (append is `Forwards` now, so a real `xs...` arrives as the `Spread` marker); `slices.Grow` uses the plain growslice path. Residual: an append consumed inside an expression (`cap(append(s, x...))`, a chained append) gets the stack-buffer cap from us but growslice's from gc — the builtin cannot see its call context. difffuzz `append_grow_cap`.
- [x] **`io.LimitReader(r, 2).(*io.LimitedReader).R.(*rd)` is false**: the host `LimitedReader.R` holds the reader proxy; reading the field back does not hand the script its own `*rd`. Found reviewing stack #975.
- `io.LimitReader(r, 2).(*io.LimitedReader).R.(*rd)` is false > fixed: the io reader/writer proxies implement the existing `runtime.ScriptBoxed` contract (`ScriptValue`)— `hostbridge.GoValueOf` already unwraps a ScriptBoxed proxy alongside `StructOrig`/`OpaqueKeyOrig` — `l.R` resolves to the script's own reader for pointer, value, and host (`*strings.Reader`) receivers. Residual (separate assert bug): a `*T` stored in `any` still asserts to `T` (`var i any = &rd{}; i.(rd)` is true, go: false), so `l.R.(rd)` on a pointer reader is true — record under the `&x`-assert family. difffuzz `io_limit_reader_field`. (assert residual fixed: a Cell in an interface box is the *T value, so `i.(rd)` now returns ok=false like gc — difffuzz `iface_ptr_val_assert`)
- [x] **`var sf bufio.SplitFunc = bufio.ScanLines; fmt.Printf("%T", sf)` prints the unnamed signature** (go: `bufio.SplitFunc`): assigning a bound func to a variable of a bound named func type does not tag the value with the declared type. Found reviewing stack #975.
- `var sf bufio.SplitFunc = bufio.ScanLines; fmt.Printf("%T", sf)` prints the unnamed signature > fixed: the func/pointer tagging gates keyed on `td.Spec != nil`, which only script-declared types carry — bound named func types like `bufio.SplitFunc` have `Name`+`Pkg` but no Spec, so the value stayed bare. The store-coercion and both conversion sites now use `tagIsNamed` (Spec or Name), which still leaves anonymous `func()`/`*T` typedefs bare. difffuzz `named_func_typetag`.
- [x] **`template.FuncMap` is undefined under the bound `text/template`**: bound under text/template and html/template; `Template.Funcs` adapts each script func to a host func of its declared signature (basics/error keep their Go type, other types are `any`) and hands bound funcs their host Target (docs/sketch/ja/experiment-storybook.md §9).
- [x] **bound-dispatched `UnmarshalJSON` drops the named slice's tag on `*t = strings`** (difffuzz `json_unmarshaljson_namedslice`): a field's `(*Types).UnmarshalJSON` assigning `*t = strings` stores the bare `[]string` — the implicit `[]string -> Types` conversion does not run through the receiver-cell store — so `s.Type.Slice()` traps `select Slice on slice`. An explicit `*t = Types(strings)` tags correctly, and the same assignment via a direct `(&s.Type).UnmarshalJSON(...)` call tags correctly too; only the bound json dispatch path loses it. Blocks dropping `encoding/json` from `--src` in realworld oapi-codegen-examples (kin-openapi `(*Types).UnmarshalJSON`).
- bound-dispatched `UnmarshalJSON` drops the named slice's tag on `*t = strings` > fixed: `unmarshalRecv` now sets `Typ: td` on the receiver cells it mints (and backfills a typaless Cell prior), so `*t = strings` coerces through the declared type. Re-verify dropping `encoding/json` from `--src` in realworld oapi-codegen-examples.
- [x] **FuncMap funcs with script-typed params**: each argument converts to the declared param type before the script call (`funcMapArgs`/`hostArgAs`, `vm/vm.go`) — a slice/array/map of script structs, `[]*Pt`, named `Pts`, nested `[][]Pt`, variadic `...[]Pt` bind like gc's; a pointer fits a value param like the template engine's indirection; a mismatch reports gc's `wrong type for value; expected T; got U` (under `error calling f:`).
